  2798  (2138)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  2799  (2139)      % THE LENGTH IS NOT A PARAMETER - THE STRING IS ASKED FOR IT.
  2800  (2140)      %
  2801  (2141)      % Every caller passes a literal, and each one used to hand the count in
  2802  (2142)      % beside it. Eighteen hand-typed numbers that nothing could check: change a
  2803  (2143)      % word in a message and the number beside it is silently wrong, the build is
  2804  (2144)      % clean, and the reply goes out truncated or padded. That is the same trap
  2805  (2145)      % as an 'ALn' field width, which cost five wrong widths in one sitting.
  2806  (2146)      %
  2807  (2147)      % MAXINDEX(text, 1) is the DECLARED UPPER BOUND of the parameter, and a
  2808  (2148)      % string literal subscripts from ZERO, so the length is that plus one. It is
  2809  (2149)      % a STANDARD routine the compiler provides - no IMPORT - and it works on an
  2810  (2150)      % array parameter, which is exactly what this is. (It is NOT available in a
  2811  (2151)      % STANDARD routine, the FORTRAN calling sequence; this is an ordinary PLANC
  2812  (2152)      % one, so it is fine.)
  2813  (2153)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (BYTES) : buildAdmText(text)
  2814  (2154)          INTEGER : i, at, textLen
  2815  (2155)          MAXINDEX(text, 1) + 1 =: textLen
  2816  (2156)          kAdmRepl =: outBuf(0)
  2817  (2157)          0 =: outBuf(1)                        % no name: the SERVER is answering
  2818  (2158)          2 =: at
  2819  (2159)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  2820  (2160)          textLen =: outBuf(at + 1)
  2821  (2161)          at + 2 =: at
  2822  (2162)          FOR i IN 1:textLen DO
  2823  (2163)              text(i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  2824  (2164)              at + 1 =: at
  2825  (2165)          ENDFOR
  2826  (2166)          at RETURN
  2827  (2167)      ENDROUTINE
  2828  (2168)  
  2829  (2169)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  2830  (2170)      % Read a decimal system number out of an arrived message. 0 means "that was
  2831  (2171)      % not a number", which the caller reports rather than acting on.
  2832  (2172)      %
  2833  (2173)      % NO SIGN AND NO SPACES. An operator types "TRUNK 102"; anything else is a
  2834  (2174)      % mistake worth refusing out loud rather than interpreting generously.
  2835  (2175)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  2836  (2176)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER) : trunkNumber(at, length)
  2837  (2177)          INTEGER : i, d, value
  2838  (2178)          IF length < 1 THEN
  2839  (2179)              0 RETURN
  2840  (2180)          ENDIF
  2841  (2181)          0 =: value
  2842  (2182)          FOR i IN 1:length DO
  2843  (2183)              inBuf(at + i - 1) =: d
  2844  (2184)              IF d < 48 THEN
  2845  (2185)                  0 RETURN
  2846  (2186)              ENDIF
  2847  (2187)              IF d > 57 THEN
  2848  (2188)                  0 RETURN
  2849  (2189)              ENDIF
  2850  (2190)              (value * 10) + (d - 48) =: value
  2851  (2191)          ENDFOR
  2852  (2192)          value RETURN
  2853  (2193)      ENDROUTINE
  2854  (2194)  
  2855  (2195)  
  2856  (2196)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  2857  (2197)      % LIST-TRUNKS: every configured peer and what we believe about it.
  2858  (2198)      %
  2859  (2199)      % "102 up" / "103 down" / "200 unknown". UNKNOWN and DOWN are kept apart
  2860  (2200)      % deliberately - the first usually means the trunk was never answered and is
  2861  (2201)      % probably a configuration mistake, the second means a machine that was
  2862  (2202)      % there and went away. Collapsing them would hide the difference exactly
  2863  (2203)      % when an operator needs it.
  2864  (2204)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  2865  (2205)      % The answer to SET-NAME, in words. buildAdmText cannot do this one - it
  2866  (2206)      % takes a literal and a length, and this has a variable in the middle.
  2867  (2207)      % THE WELCOME NOW SAYS WHICH MACHINE THE CLIENT IS ON.
  2868  (2208)      %
  2869  (2209)      % It used to carry the joiner's name and an EMPTY text, and the client
  2870  (2210)      % ignored the text entirely - so filling it in is backward compatible: an
  2871  (2211)      % older client reads the name, reads a length, and does nothing with what
  2872  (2212)      % follows.
  2873  (2213)      %
  2874  (2214)      % WHY IT HAS TO COME FROM HERE. A client has NO WAY of knowing which
  2875  (2215)      % machine it is on. /map says so, but in a sentence written for a person to
  2876  (2216)      % read, and parsing prose to find out a fact is how a client ends up wrong
  2877  (2217)      % in a way nobody can explain. The server knows for certain - it asked the
  2878  (2218)      % kernel - so it says so at the one moment the client is guaranteed to be
  2879  (2219)      % listening.
  2880  (2220)      %
  2881  (2221)      % This is what lets a full-screen client put ANNA@FJELL in its corner
  2882  (2222)      % instead of leaving it blank or, worse, guessing.
  2883  (2223)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER) : buildWelcome(nameAt, nameLength)
  2884  (2224)          INTEGER : i, at, textAt, textLen
  2885  (2225)  
  2886  (2226)          kWelcome =: outBuf(0)
  2887  (2227)          nameLength =: outBuf(1)
  2888  (2228)          2 =: at
  2889  (2229)          FOR i IN 1:nameLength DO
  2890  (2230)              inBuf(nameAt + i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  2891  (2231)              at + 1 =: at
  2892  (2232)          ENDFOR
  2893  (2233)  
  2894  (2234)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  2895  (2235)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  2896  (2236)          at + 2 =: textAt
  2897  (2237)          textAt =: at
  2898  (2238)  
  2899  (2239)          putSysLabel(mySystem, at) =: at
  2900  (2240)  
  2901  (2241)          at - textAt =: textLen
  2902  (2242)          textLen =: outBuf(textAt - 1)
  2903  (2243)          at RETURN
  2904  (2244)      ENDROUTINE
  2905  (2245)  
  2906  (2246)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER : buildNameReply
  2907  (2247)          INTEGER : i, at, textAt, textLen
  2908  (2248)  
  2909  (2249)          kAdmRepl =: outBuf(0)
  2910  (2250)          0 =: outBuf(1)
  2911  (2251)          2 =: at
  2912  (2252)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  2913  (2253)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  2914  (2254)          at + 2 =: textAt
  2915  (2255)          textAt =: at
  2916  (2256)  
  2917  (2257)          IF lenSysName > 0 THEN
  2918  (2258)              putWord('this machine is now ', at) =: at
  2919  (2259)              FOR i IN 1:lenSysName DO
  2920  (2260)                  sysName(i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  2921  (2261)                  at + 1 =: at
  2922  (2262)              ENDFOR
  2923  (2263)          ELSE
  2924  (2264)              putWord('this machine has no name - it answers to ', at) =: at
  2925  (2265)              putSysLabel(mySystem, at) =: at
  2926  (2266)          ENDIF
  2927  (2267)  
  2928  (2268)          at - textAt =: textLen
  2929  (2269)          textLen =: outBuf(3)
  2930  (2270)          at RETURN
  2931  (2271)      ENDROUTINE
  2932  (2272)  
  2933  (2273)      % ONE TRUNK PER LINE, IN FIXED COLUMNS.
  2934  (2274)      %
  2935  (2275)      % This used to run every trunk together on one line separated by a single
  2936  (2276)      % space - "100 FJELL up 103 SKOGEN down" - with both fields as wide as
  2937  (2277)      % whatever happened to be in them, so nothing lined up and two trunks read
  2938  (2278)      % as one sentence. Reported by Ronny 2026-08-31.
  2939  (2279)      %
  2940  (2280)      % THE LINE BREAK IS A BYTE IN THE TEXT, not a second message. The whole
  2941  (2281)      % answer travels as ONE kAdmRepl and CHAT-MON prints it byte by byte, so a
  2942  (2282)      % break has to be something the PRINTER recognises: an LF, which showAnswer
  2943  (2283)      % turns into a CRLF. That costs no new kind and no wire change, and it fixes
  2944  (2284)      % EVERY listing carried on kAdmRepl rather than only this one.
  2945  (2285)      %
  2946  (2286)      % THE WIDTHS ARE NAMED, and the name field is ASKED OF THE DATA rather than
  2947  (2287)      % typed: maxNameLen is what pName is declared to hold, so the column cannot
  2948  (2288)      % drift from the buffer the way a hand-written 16 would.
  2949  (2289)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER : buildTrunks
  2950  (2290)          INTEGER : i, j, at, textAt, textLen, st, fieldAt
  2951  (2291)  
  2952  (2292)          kAdmRepl =: outBuf(0)
  2953  (2293)          0 =: outBuf(1)
  2954  (2294)          2 =: at
  2955  (2295)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  2956  (2296)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  2957  (2297)          at + 2 =: textAt
  2958  (2298)          textAt =: at
  2959  (2299)  
  2960  (2300)          FOR i IN 1:maxPeers DO
  2961  (2301)              IF peerSystem(i) >< 0 THEN
  2962  (2302)                  % A BREAK BEFORE EVERY LINE BUT THE FIRST, so the text never
  2963  (2303)                  % ENDS with one - a trailing break prints a blank line under
  2964  (2304)                  % every listing.
  2965  (2305)                  IF at - textAt > 0 THEN
  2966  (2306)                      lineBreak =: outBuf(at)
  2967  (2307)                      at + 1 =: at
  2968  (2308)                  ENDIF
  2969  (2309)  
  2970  (2310)                  % The system number, in a field of its own.
  2971  (2311)                  at =: fieldAt
  2972  (2312)                  putNumber(peerSystem(i), at) =: at
  2973  (2313)                  DO
  2974  (2314)                      WHILE at - fieldAt < idWide
  2975  (2315)                      spaceByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  2976  (2316)                      at + 1 =: at
  2977  (2317)                  ENDDO
  2978  (2318)  
  2979  (2319)                  % The name, padded to the widest this server can hold, so the
  2980  (2320)                  % state word starts at the same column on every line - including
  2981  (2321)                  % the lines where the peer has no name yet.
  2982  (2322)                  at =: fieldAt
  2983  (2323)                  IF pNameLen(i) > 0 THEN
  2984  (2324)                      FOR j IN 1:pNameLen(i) DO
  2985  (2325)                          pName(i, j) =: outBuf(at)
  2986  (2326)                          at + 1 =: at
  2987  (2327)                      ENDFOR
  2988  (2328)                  ENDIF
  2989  (2329)                  DO
  2990  (2330)                      WHILE at - fieldAt < maxNameLen + 1
  2991  (2331)                      spaceByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  2992  (2332)                      at + 1 =: at
  2993  (2333)                  ENDDO
  2994  (2334)  
  2995  (2335)                  peerState(i) =: st
  2996  (2336)                  IF st = peerUp THEN
  2997  (2337)                      putWord('up', at) =: at
  2998  (2338)                  ENDIF
  2999  (2339)                  IF st = peerDown THEN
  3000  (2340)                      putWord('down', at) =: at
  3001  (2341)                  ENDIF
  3002  (2342)                  IF st = peerUnknown THEN
  3003  (2343)                      putWord('unknown', at) =: at
  3004  (2344)                  ENDIF
  3005  (2345)  
  3006  (2346)                  % WHO PUT IT THERE. Only the LEARNED ones are marked, so the
  3007  (2347)                  % ordinary case - every trunk asked for by an operator - reads
  3008  (2348)                  % exactly as it always did. A "(learned)" beside a system
  3009  (2349)                  % nobody remembers configuring is the whole point: until now
  3010  (2350)                  % there was no way to tell the two apart at all.
  3011  (2351)                  IF peerFixed(i) = peerLearned THEN
  3012  (2352)                      spaceByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  3013  (2353)                      at + 1 =: at
  3014  (2354)                      putWord('(learned)', at) =: at
  3015  (2355)                  ENDIF
  3016  (2356)              ENDIF
  3017  (2357)          ENDFOR
  3018  (2358)  
  3019  (2359)          IF at = textAt THEN
  3020  (2360)              putWord('no trunks', at) =: at
  3021  (2361)          ENDIF
  3022  (2362)  
  3023  (2363)          % AND WHETHER MORE CAN STILL ARRIVE. An operator reading this list needs
  3024  (2364)          % to know it is the whole list and not just the part that has turned up
  3025  (2365)          % so far - "locked" is the difference between a network and a snapshot.
  3026  (2366)          IF NOT peersOpen THEN
  3027  (2367)              % ON A LINE OF ITS OWN, and that is not decoration. This marker is
  3028  (2368)              % about the WHOLE LIST, not about one trunk. While the listing ran
  3029  (2369)              % everything together on one line that was clear enough; one trunk
  3030  (2370)              % per line puts it hard against the last entry, where it reads as
  3031  (2371)              % if THAT machine were the locked one. Seen on D102 the first time
  3032  (2372)              % the new layout ran:
  3033  (2373)              %
  3034  (2374)              %     100   FJELL            up
  3035  (2375)              %     103   SKOGEN           up [locked]
  3036  (2376)              %
  3037  (2377)              % A break costs one byte and removes the wrong reading.
  3038  (2378)              IF at - textAt > 0 THEN
  3039  (2379)                  lineBreak =: outBuf(at)
  3040  (2380)                  at + 1 =: at
  3041  (2381)              ENDIF
  3042  (2382)              putWord('[locked]', at) =: at
  3043  (2383)          ENDIF
  3044  (2384)  
  3045  (2385)          at - textAt =: textLen
  3046  (2386)          textLen =: outBuf(3)
  3047  (2387)          at RETURN
  3048  (2388)      ENDROUTINE
  3049  (2389)  
  3050  (2390)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3051  (2391)      % LIST-MEMBER - who is seated here right now, and which room.
  3052  (2392)      %
  3053  (2393)      % SAME COLUMN DISCIPLINE AS buildTrunks: the name padded to maxNameLen+1
  3054  (2394)      % so the room column lines up under every seat, ONE seat per line with
  3055  (2395)      % lineBreak between them, no trailing break. An operator's own listing -
  3056  (2396)      % dispatched only IF fromAdmin, same guard as DROP-MEMBER and LIST-TRUNKS.
  3057  (2397)      %
  3058  (2398)      % memberName/memberLen are the SAME arrays findByName searches and
  3059  (2399)      % DROP-MEMBER clears, so this listing is always what DROP-MEMBER can act
  3060  (2400)      % on - never a stale copy of it.
  3061  (2401)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3062  (2402)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER : buildSeats
  3063  (2403)          INTEGER : i, j, at, textAt, textLen, fieldAt
  3064  (2404)  
  3065  (2405)          kAdmRepl =: outBuf(0)
  3066  (2406)          0 =: outBuf(1)
  3067  (2407)          2 =: at
  3068  (2408)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  3069  (2409)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  3070  (2410)          at + 2 =: textAt
  3071  (2411)          textAt =: at
  3072  (2412)  
  3073  (2413)          FOR i IN 1:maxSeats DO
  3074  (2414)              IF memberUsed(i) = 1 THEN
  3075  (2415)                  IF at - textAt > 0 THEN
  3076  (2416)                      lineBreak =: outBuf(at)
  3077  (2417)                      at + 1 =: at
  3078  (2418)                  ENDIF
  3079  (2419)  
  3080  (2420)                  % The name, padded to the widest this server can hold, so the
  3081  (2421)                  % room column starts at the same place on every line.
  3082  (2422)                  at =: fieldAt
  3083  (2423)                  IF memberLen(i) > 0 THEN
  3084  (2424)                      FOR j IN 1:memberLen(i) DO
  3085  (2425)                          memberName(i, j) =: outBuf(at)
  3086  (2426)                          at + 1 =: at
  3087  (2427)                      ENDFOR
  3088  (2428)                  ENDIF
  3089  (2429)                  DO
  3090  (2430)                      WHILE at - fieldAt < maxNameLen + 1
  3091  (2431)                      spaceByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  3092  (2432)                      at + 1 =: at
  3093  (2433)                  ENDDO
  3094  (2434)  
  3095  (2435)                  % The room, padded the same way, so the version column also
  3096  (2436)                  % starts at the same place on every line.
  3097  (2437)                  at =: fieldAt
  3098  (2438)                  IF lenMbrRoom(i) > 0 THEN
  3099  (2439)                      FOR j IN 1:lenMbrRoom(i) DO
  3100  (2440)                          mbrRoom(i, j) =: outBuf(at)
  3101  (2441)                          at + 1 =: at
  3102  (2442)                      ENDFOR
  3103  (2443)                  ENDIF
  3104  (2444)                  DO
  3105  (2445)                      WHILE at - fieldAt < maxNameLen + 1
  3106  (2446)                      spaceByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  3107  (2447)                      at + 1 =: at
  3108  (2448)                  ENDDO
  3109  (2449)  
  3110  (2450)                  % THE BUILD VERSION - blank for a client old enough to carry
  3111  (2451)                  % none, which is worth SEEING rather than hiding: a blank
  3112  (2452)                  % column is the visible difference between "matches" and
  3113  (2453)                  % "cannot say".
  3114  (2454)                  IF memberVerLen(i) > 0 THEN
  3115  (2455)                      FOR j IN 1:memberVerLen(i) DO
  3116  (2456)                          memberVer(i, j) =: outBuf(at)
  3117  (2457)                          at + 1 =: at
  3118  (2458)                      ENDFOR
  3119  (2459)                  ENDIF
  3120  (2460)              ENDIF
  3121  (2461)          ENDFOR
  3122  (2462)  
  3123  (2463)          IF at = textAt THEN
  3124  (2464)              putWord('no members', at) =: at
  3125  (2465)          ENDIF
  3126  (2466)  
  3127  (2467)          at - textAt =: textLen
  3128  (2468)          textLen =: outBuf(3)
  3129  (2469)          at RETURN
  3130  (2470)      ENDROUTINE
  3131  (2471)  
  3132  (2472)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3133  (2473)      % /map - the same picture, for somebody who is not an operator.
  3134  (2474)      %
  3135  (2475)      %     this is D100 - 102 up  103 down
  3136  (2476)      %     this is D100 - on its own
  3137  (2477)      %
  3138  (2478)      % Ronny, 2026-08-20: end users should be able to see how the network is
  3139  (2479)      % configured. The registry has held kind 12 for it ever since.
  3140  (2480)      %
  3141  (2481)      % WHY IT IS NOT JUST LIST-TRUNKS WITH A DIFFERENT NAME, and this is the
  3142  (2482)      % whole reason it is a ROOM kind rather than an admin one:
  3143  (2483)      %
  3144  (2484)      %   SEEING how the network is wired is not the same right as REWIRING it.
  3145  (2485)      %   LIST-TRUNKS arrives on the ADMIN port, from CHAT-MON, alongside
  3146  (2486)      %   START-TRUNK and STOP-TRUNK - the verbs that change things. /map arrives
  3147  (2487)      %   on the room port from an ordinary member and can change nothing.
  3148  (2488)      %
  3149  (2489)      % IT NAMES THIS MACHINE FIRST. A trunk list on its own is ambiguous the
  3150  (2490)      % moment there is more than one machine: "102 up" read on D102 would be
  3151  (2491)      % nonsense, and a user who has just been told they are "ANNA@D102" needs to
  3152  (2492)      % know which end they are looking from. mySystem comes from xmpfm2p on our
  3153  (2493)      % own magic at start-up, so it is what the KERNEL says we are, not what a
  3154  (2494)      % configuration file claims.
  3155  (2495)      %
  3156  (2496)      % Peers are two spaces apart and the number and state one, the same shape
  3157  (2497)      % the all-rooms /who uses, so the groups read apart at a glance.
  3158  (2498)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3159  (2499)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER : buildMap
  3160  (2500)          INTEGER : i, at, textAt, textLen, st, headAt
  3161  (2501)  
  3162  (2502)          kMap =: outBuf(0)
  3163  (2503)          0 =: outBuf(1)                        % no name: the SERVER is answering
  3164  (2504)          2 =: at
  3165  (2505)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  3166  (2506)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  3167  (2507)          at + 2 =: textAt
  3168  (2508)          textAt =: at
  3169  (2509)  
  3170  (2510)          putWord('this is ', at) =: at
  3171  (2511)          putSysLabel(mySystem, at) =: at
  3172  (2512)          putWord(' - ', at) =: at
  3173  (2513)  
  3174  (2514)          % REMEMBER WHERE THE HEADER ENDED, do not compute it.
  3175  (2515)          %
  3176  (2516)          % This was first written as "IF at - textAt > 15", 15 being the length
  3177  (2517)          % of "this is D100 - ". That is only true for a THREE-DIGIT system
  3178  (2518)          % number: on D19999 the header is 17 and the test would put two spaces
  3179  (2519)          % before the first peer and then still claim there were none. A length
  3180  (2520)          % that depends on data is not a constant, however constant it looks in
  3181  (2521)          % the machine you are sitting at.
  3182  (2522)          at =: headAt
  3183  (2523)  
  3184  (2524)          FOR i IN 1:maxPeers DO
  3185  (2525)              IF peerSystem(i) >< 0 THEN
  3186  (2526)                  IF at > headAt THEN
  3187  (2527)                      spaceByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  3188  (2528)                      at + 1 =: at
  3189  (2529)                      spaceByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  3190  (2530)                      at + 1 =: at
  3191  (2531)                  ENDIF
  3192  (2532)                  putSysLabel(peerSystem(i), at) =: at
  3193  (2533)                  spaceByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  3194  (2534)                  at + 1 =: at
  3195  (2535)                  peerState(i) =: st
  3196  (2536)                  IF st = peerUp THEN
  3197  (2537)                      putWord('up', at) =: at
  3198  (2538)                  ENDIF
  3199  (2539)                  IF st = peerDown THEN
  3200  (2540)                      putWord('down', at) =: at
  3201  (2541)                  ENDIF
  3202  (2542)                  IF st = peerUnknown THEN
  3203  (2543)                      putWord('unknown', at) =: at
  3204  (2544)                  ENDIF
  3205  (2545)              ENDIF
  3206  (2546)          ENDFOR
  3207  (2547)  
  3208  (2548)          % NO TRUNKS IS A REAL ANSWER AND IS SAID IN WORDS. "on its own" is what
  3209  (2549)          % a person wants to read; an empty list after a dash is what a program
  3210  (2550)          % would print. Same reason /topic spells out an absent topic.
  3211  (2551)          IF at = headAt THEN
  3212  (2552)              putWord('on its own', at) =: at
  3213  (2553)          ENDIF
  3214  (2554)  
  3215  (2555)          at - textAt =: textLen
  3216  (2556)          textLen =: outBuf(3)
  3217  (2557)          at RETURN
  3218  (2558)      ENDROUTINE
  3219  (2559)  
  3220  (2560)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3221  (2561)      % Forget everything a peer told us. Called when it goes Down, and before
  3222  (2562)      % storing a fresh list from it.
  3223  (2563)      %
  3224  (2564)      % WHOLESALE, NEVER MERGED. A peer that comes back has almost certainly
  3225  (2565)      % restarted, so anything we remember about its members is stale - the design
  3226  (2566)      % says treat Up as "start again". Merging would leave ghosts of people who
  3227  (2567)      % left while the machine was away, and nothing would ever remove them.
  3228  (2568)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3229  (2569)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER) : forgetRemote(system)
  3230  (2570)          INTEGER : i
  3231  (2571)          FOR i IN 1:maxRemote DO
  3232  (2572)              IF rmtSystem(i) = system THEN
  3233  (2573)                  0 =: rmtSystem(i)
  3234  (2574)                  0 =: rmtNameLen(i)
  3235  (2575)                  0 =: rmtRoomLen(i)
  3236  (2576)              ENDIF
  3237  (2577)          ENDFOR
  3238  (2578)      ENDROUTINE
  3239  (2579)  
  3240  (2580)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3241  (2581)      % Build the answer to a peer's "who is on you": our own seated members as
  3242  (2582)      % "NAME/ROOM NAME/ROOM ...".
  3243  (2583)      %
  3244  (2584)      % OUR OWN MEMBERS ONLY. What we know about OTHER machines is theirs to
  3245  (2585)      % report, not ours to relay - passing it on would let a wrong entry echo
  3246  (2586)      % around a ring of machines with nothing to stop it. Every machine speaks
  3247  (2587)      % only for itself; that is what makes the state converge.
  3248  (2588)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3249  (2589)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER : buildMembers
  3250  (2590)          INTEGER : i, j, at, textAt, textLen, need
  3251  (2591)  
  3252  (2592)          kTrkMbrs =: outBuf(0)
  3253  (2593)          0 =: outBuf(1)                        % no name: a MACHINE is answering
  3254  (2594)          2 =: at
  3255  (2595)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  3256  (2596)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  3257  (2597)          at + 2 =: textAt
  3258  (2598)          textAt =: at
  3259  (2599)          0 =: textLen
  3260  (2600)  
  3261  (2601)          FOR i IN 1:maxSeats DO
  3262  (2602)              IF memberUsed(i) = 1 THEN
  3263  (2603)                  % name + '/' + room, and a space if anything is there already
  3264  (2604)                  memberLen(i) + lenMbrRoom(i) + 1 =: need
  3265  (2605)                  IF textLen > 0 THEN
  3266  (2606)                      need + 1 =: need
  3267  (2607)                  ENDIF
  3268  (2608)  
  3269  (2609)                  IF textLen + need <= 250 THEN
  3270  (2610)                      IF textLen > 0 THEN
  3271  (2611)                          spaceByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  3272  (2612)                          at + 1 =: at
  3273  (2613)                          textLen + 1 =: textLen
  3274  (2614)                      ENDIF
  3275  (2615)                      FOR j IN 1:memberLen(i) DO
  3276  (2616)                          memberName(i, j) =: outBuf(at)
  3277  (2617)                          at + 1 =: at
  3278  (2618)                      ENDFOR
  3279  (2619)                      slashByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  3280  (2620)                      at + 1 =: at
  3281  (2621)                      FOR j IN 1:lenMbrRoom(i) DO
  3282  (2622)                          mbrRoom(i, j) =: outBuf(at)
  3283  (2623)                          at + 1 =: at
  3284  (2624)                      ENDFOR
  3285  (2625)                      textLen + need =: textLen
  3286  (2626)                  ENDIF
  3287  (2627)              ENDIF
  3288  (2628)          ENDFOR
  3289  (2629)  
  3290  (2630)          textLen =: outBuf(3)
  3291  (2631)          at RETURN
  3292  (2632)      ENDROUTINE
  3293  (2633)  
  3294  (2634)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3295  (2635)      % Store what a peer just told us. Replaces everything we held for it.
  3296  (2636)      %
  3297  (2637)      % Parses "NAME/ROOM NAME/ROOM ..." out of inBuf. A malformed entry - no
  3298  (2638)      % slash, or a name longer than a name can be - is DROPPED and the parse
  3299  (2639)      % carries on. One bad entry from a peer must not cost us the rest of its
  3300  (2640)      % list, and must certainly not write past the end of a table.
  3301  (2641)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3302  (2642)      % NO LABEL AND NO GO. A "skip this entry" jump would read more naturally,
  3303  (2643)      % but PLANC's label form is not something this file has ever used and a
  3304  (2644)      % guess costs a ten-minute build cycle. A BOOLEAN says the same thing and
  3305  (2645)      % cannot be got wrong.
  3306  (2646)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : &
  3307  (2647)              takeMembers(system, at, length)
  3308  (2648)          INTEGER : p, stop, nameAt, nameLen2, roomAt, roomLen2, row, i
  3309  (2649)          BOOLEAN : good
  3310  (2650)  
  3311  (2651)          forgetRemote(system)
  3312  (2652)  
  3313  (2653)          at =: p
  3314  (2654)          at + length =: stop
  3315  (2655)  
  3316  (2656)          DO
  3317  (2657)              WHILE p < stop
  3318  (2658)  
  3319  (2659)              % skip separators
  3320  (2660)              DO
  3321  (2661)                  WHILE p < stop AND inBuf(p) = spaceByte(0)
  3322  (2662)                  p + 1 =: p
  3323  (2663)              ENDDO
  3324  (2664)  
  3325  (2665)              % the name, up to a slash or a space
  3326  (2666)              p =: nameAt
  3327  (2667)              DO
  3328  (2668)                  WHILE p < stop AND inBuf(p) >< slashByte(0) AND &
  3329  (2669)                        inBuf(p) >< spaceByte(0)
  3330  (2670)                  p + 1 =: p
  3331  (2671)              ENDDO
  3332  (2672)              p - nameAt =: nameLen2
  3333  (2673)  
  3334  (2674)              % A SLASH MUST FOLLOW. Anything else is a malformed entry - the room
  3335  (2675)              % is left empty, the entry fails the test below and is dropped, and
  3336  (2676)              % the loop carries on with the next one. One bad entry from a peer
  3337  (2677)              % must not cost us the rest of its list.
  3338  (2678)              FALSE =: good
  3339  (2679)              0 =: roomLen2
  3340  (2680)              IF p < stop THEN
  3341  (2681)                  IF inBuf(p) = slashByte(0) THEN
  3342  (2682)                      p + 1 =: p
  3343  (2683)                      p =: roomAt
  3344  (2684)                      DO
  3345  (2685)                          WHILE p < stop AND inBuf(p) >< spaceByte(0)
  3346  (2686)                          p + 1 =: p
  3347  (2687)                      ENDDO
  3348  (2688)                      p - roomAt =: roomLen2
  3349  (2689)                      TRUE =: good
  3350  (2690)                  ENDIF
  3351  (2691)              ENDIF
  3352  (2692)  
  3353  (2693)              IF good THEN
  3354  (2694)                  IF nameLen2 > 0 AND nameLen2 <= maxNameLen AND &
  3355  (2695)                     roomLen2 > 0 AND roomLen2 <= maxNameLen THEN
  3356  (2696)                      0 =: row
  3357  (2697)                      FOR i IN 1:maxRemote DO
  3358  (2698)                          IF rmtSystem(i) = 0 THEN
  3359  (2699)                              IF row = 0 THEN
  3360  (2700)                                  i =: row
  3361  (2701)                              ENDIF
  3362  (2702)                          ENDIF
  3363  (2703)                      ENDFOR
  3364  (2704)                      IF row >< 0 THEN
  3365  (2705)                          system =: rmtSystem(row)
  3366  (2706)                          FOR i IN 1:nameLen2 DO
  3367  (2707)                              inBuf(nameAt + i - 1) =: rmtName(row, i)
  3368  (2708)                          ENDFOR
  3369  (2709)                          nameLen2 =: rmtNameLen(row)
  3370  (2710)                          FOR i IN 1:roomLen2 DO
  3371  (2711)                              inBuf(roomAt + i - 1) =: rmtRoom(row, i)
  3372  (2712)                          ENDFOR
  3373  (2713)                          roomLen2 =: rmtRoomLen(row)
  3374  (2714)                      ENDIF
  3375  (2715)                  ENDIF
  3376  (2716)              ENDIF
  3377  (2717)          ENDDO
  3378  (2718)      ENDROUTINE
  3379  (2719)  
  3380  (2720)      % Is this member in the same room as that one?
  3381  (2721)      %
  3382  (2722)      % Compared byte by byte on the stored name. A room is only "the same room"
  3383  (2723)      % if it is spelled the same - there is no room id, because a room is not a
  3384  (2724)      % registered thing any more, just a name people agree on.
  3385  (2725)      %
  3386  (2726)      % DECLARED HERE, ABOVE buildWho, AND THAT POSITION IS LOAD-BEARING. It used
  3387  (2727)      % to sit next to broadcast, 250 lines further down; PLANC needs a routine
  3388  (2728)      % declared before it is called, so buildWho could not have used it even if
  3389  (2729)      % somebody had thought to. Moved up 2026-08-22 when /who was fixed.
  3390  (2730)      ROUTINE VOID, BOOLEAN (INTEGER, INTEGER) : sameRoom(slotA, slotB)
  3391  (2731)          INTEGER : i
  3392  (2732)          IF lenMbrRoom(slotA) >< lenMbrRoom(slotB) THEN
  3393  (2733)              FALSE RETURN
  3394  (2734)          ENDIF
  3395  (2735)          FOR i IN 1:lenMbrRoom(slotA) DO
  3396  (2736)              IF mbrRoom(slotA, i) >< mbrRoom(slotB, i) THEN
  3397  (2737)                  FALSE RETURN
  3398  (2738)              ENDIF
  3399  (2739)          ENDFOR
  3400  (2740)          TRUE RETURN
  3401  (2741)      ENDROUTINE
  3402  (2742)  
  3403  (2743)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3404  (2744)      % Which topic entry belongs to this member's room? 0 if the room has never
  3405  (2745)      % had a topic set.
  3406  (2746)      %
  3407  (2747)      % Compared the same way sameRoom compares: byte for byte on the spelling.
  3408  (2748)      % A room IS its name here, so LOBBY and lobby are two rooms, and the topic
  3409  (2749)      % table has to agree with the broadcast about that or a topic would show up
  3410  (2750)      % in a room that cannot hear the messages.
  3411  (2751)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3412  (2752)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER) : findTopic(slot)
  3413  (2753)          INTEGER : t, i
  3414  (2754)          BOOLEAN : match
  3415  (2755)  
  3416  (2756)          FOR t IN 1:maxTopics DO
  3417  (2757)              IF topicRmLen(t) = lenMbrRoom(slot) THEN
  3418  (2758)                  TRUE =: match
  3419  (2759)                  FOR i IN 1:topicRmLen(t) DO
  3420  (2760)                      IF topicRoom(t, i) >< mbrRoom(slot, i) THEN
  3421  (2761)                          FALSE =: match
  3422  (2762)                      ENDIF
  3423  (2763)                  ENDFOR
  3424  (2764)                  IF match THEN
  3425  (2765)                      t RETURN
  3426  (2766)                  ENDIF
  3427  (2767)              ENDIF
  3428  (2768)          ENDFOR
  3429  (2769)          0 RETURN
  3430  (2770)      ENDROUTINE
  3431  (2771)  
  3432  (2772)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3433  (2773)      % Claim a topic entry for this member's room, or 0 if the table is full.
  3434  (2774)      %
  3435  (2775)      % An unused entry is one with topicRmLen 0 - a room name is never empty,
  3436  (2776)      % so zero cannot collide with a real one.
  3437  (2777)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3438  (2778)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER) : claimTopic(slot)
  3439  (2779)          INTEGER : t, i
  3440  (2780)  
  3441  (2781)          findTopic(slot) =: t
  3442  (2782)          IF t >< 0 THEN
  3443  (2783)              t RETURN                          % already has one; reuse it
  3444  (2784)          ENDIF
  3445  (2785)  
  3446  (2786)          FOR t IN 1:maxTopics DO
  3447  (2787)              IF topicRmLen(t) = 0 THEN
  3448  (2788)                  FOR i IN 1:lenMbrRoom(slot) DO
  3449  (2789)                      mbrRoom(slot, i) =: topicRoom(t, i)
  3450  (2790)                  ENDFOR
  3451  (2791)                  lenMbrRoom(slot) =: topicRmLen(t)
  3452  (2792)                  0 =: topicTextLen(t)
  3453  (2793)  
  3454  (2794)                  % TAKE A COPY OF THE DEFAULT AS IT IS NOW. This is what makes
  3455  (2795)                  % "INITIALIZE 20" apply to rooms created LATER without reaching
  3456  (2796)                  % back into rooms that already have messages - the copy is
  3457  (2797)                  % taken once, here, at the moment the room comes into being.
  3458  (2798)                  histDefault =: histSize(t)
  3459  (2799)                  0 =: histNext(t)
  3460  (2800)                  t RETURN
  3461  (2801)              ENDIF
  3462  (2802)          ENDFOR
  3463  (2803)          0 RETURN                              % full - the caller must say so
  3464  (2804)      ENDROUTINE
  3465  (2805)  
  3466  (2806)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3467  (2807)      % Find a room entry BY NAME, out of the incoming buffer, or 0.
  3468  (2808)      %
  3469  (2809)      % A SEPARATE ROUTINE FROM findTopic, which looks a room up by the SEAT of
  3470  (2810)      % somebody in it. This one is for an operator naming a room they are not in
  3471  (2811)      % - and may not even exist yet. One routine taking "which kind of thing is
  3472  (2812)      % this" would be a flag deciding what the code means, which is the same
  3473  (2813)      % mistake sameRoom and sameRoomAs were deliberately kept apart to avoid.
  3474  (2814)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3475  (2815)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER) : findRoomNamed(at, len)
  3476  (2816)          INTEGER : t, i
  3477  (2817)          BOOLEAN : match
  3478  (2818)  
  3479  (2819)          IF len < 1 THEN
  3480  (2820)              0 RETURN
  3481  (2821)          ENDIF
  3482  (2822)          FOR t IN 1:maxTopics DO
  3483  (2823)              IF topicRmLen(t) = len THEN
  3484  (2824)                  TRUE =: match
  3485  (2825)                  FOR i IN 1:len DO
  3486  (2826)                      IF topicRoom(t, i) >< inBuf(at + i - 1) THEN
  3487  (2827)                          FALSE =: match
  3488  (2828)                      ENDIF
  3489  (2829)                  ENDFOR
  3490  (2830)                  IF match THEN
  3491  (2831)                      t RETURN
  3492  (2832)                  ENDIF
  3493  (2833)              ENDIF
  3494  (2834)          ENDFOR
  3495  (2835)          0 RETURN
  3496  (2836)      ENDROUTINE
  3497  (2837)  
  3498  (2838)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3499  (2839)      % Claim a room entry for a NAMED room, or 0 if the table is full.
  3500  (2840)      %
  3501  (2841)      % Lets a room be configured BEFORE anybody is in it, which is the useful
  3502  (2842)      % case: an operator sets up the room's history and the first person to
  3503  (2843)      % arrive gets the size that was intended rather than the default.
  3504  (2844)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3505  (2845)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER) : claimRoomNamed(at, len)
  3506  (2846)          INTEGER : t, i
  3507  (2847)  
  3508  (2848)          findRoomNamed(at, len) =: t
  3509  (2849)          IF t >< 0 THEN
  3510  (2850)              t RETURN
  3511  (2851)          ENDIF
  3512  (2852)          IF len < 1 THEN
  3513  (2853)              0 RETURN
  3514  (2854)          ENDIF
  3515  (2855)          IF len > 16 THEN
  3516  (2856)              0 RETURN                          % a room name cannot be longer
  3517  (2857)          ENDIF
  3518  (2858)  
  3519  (2859)          FOR t IN 1:maxTopics DO
  3520  (2860)              IF topicRmLen(t) = 0 THEN
  3521  (2861)                  FOR i IN 1:len DO
  3522  (2862)                      inBuf(at + i - 1) =: topicRoom(t, i)
  3523  (2863)                  ENDFOR
  3524  (2864)                  len =: topicRmLen(t)
  3525  (2865)                  0 =: topicTextLen(t)
  3526  (2866)  
  3527  (2867)                  % Same copy as claimTopic, for the same reason - see the note
  3528  (2868)                  % there. A room configured by name before anybody is in it is
  3529  (2869)                  % still a room being CREATED.
  3530  (2870)                  histDefault =: histSize(t)
  3531  (2871)                  0 =: histNext(t)
  3532  (2872)                  t RETURN
  3533  (2873)              ENDIF
  3534  (2874)          ENDFOR
  3535  (2875)          0 RETURN
  3536  (2876)      ENDROUTINE
  3537  (2877)  
  3538  (2878)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3539  (2879)      % Open this room's history file, creating it the first time.
  3540  (2880)      %
  3541  (2881)      % Returns the device number, or 0 if it could not be had. A history that
  3542  (2882)      % cannot be opened is NOT a reason to refuse the room: the chat carries on
  3543  (2883)      % without it, and that is deliberate - losing the record of a conversation
  3544  (2884)      % is bad, losing the conversation is worse.
  3545  (2885)      %
  3546  (2886)      % OPENED ONCE AND LEFT OPEN. A room is written on every line anybody types,
  3547  (2887)      % and an open plus a close in that path would put two file operations
  3548  (2888)      % between a keystroke and the room hearing it.
  3549  (2889)      %
  3550  (2890)      % TRY THE PLAIN OPEN FIRST, CREATE ONLY IF IT FAILS. A create at a name
  3551  (2891)      % that already exists is refused (SINTRAN error 62), so create-first fails
  3552  (2892)      % on every run after the first - which is every run that matters.
  3553  (2893)      %
  3554  (2894)      % ACCESS CODE 2 IS RANDOM READ/WRITE, and it has to be: MN117 and MN120
  3555  (2895)      % require it, and the whole ring depends on addressing a block directly.
  3556  (2896)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3557  (2897)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3558  (2898)      % Ask SINTRAN to create this room's history file.
  3559  (2899)      %
  3560  (2900)      % The same shape as makeLog, and for the same reason: MON50 cannot create,
  3561  (2901)      % CreateFile (221B) is a separate call, and it has no MONn routine - which
  3562  (2902)      % is why it goes through MONITOR_CALL. That works here because
  3563  (2903)      % MON-CALL-1B-A00 supplies 5MON_P and this program already needs it for
  3564  (2904)      % TimeOut.
  3565  (2905)      %
  3566  (2906)      % The name carried here is the FULL specification WITH the type -
  3567  (2907)      % "(SYSTEM)CHATH1:DATA" - where MON50 takes the name and the type as two
  3568  (2908)      % separate strings. Two calls, two conventions.
  3569  (2909)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3570  (2910)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER) : makeHistFile(t)
  3571  (2911)          INTEGER : i, at
  3572  (2912)  
  3573  (2913)          FOR i IN 1:sizeNameBuf DO
  3574  (2914)              blankByte(0) =: nameBuf(i - 1)
  3575  (2915)          ENDFOR
  3576  (2916)  
  3577  (2917)          0 =: at
  3578  (2918)          FOR i IN 1:lenHistUser DO
  3579  (2919)              histUser(i - 1) =: nameBuf(at)
  3580  (2920)              at + 1 =: at
  3581  (2921)          ENDFOR
  3582  (2922)          FOR i IN 1:5 DO
  3583  (2923)              histStem(i - 1) =: nameBuf(at)
  3584  (2924)              at + 1 =: at
  3585  (2925)          ENDFOR
  3586  (2926)          digitZero + t =: nameBuf(at)
  3587  (2927)          at + 1 =: at
  3588  (2928)          colonByte(0) =: nameBuf(at)
  3589  (2929)          at + 1 =: at
  3590  (2930)          FOR i IN 1:4 DO
  3591  (2931)              histType(i - 1) =: nameBuf(at)
  3592  (2932)              at + 1 =: at
  3593  (2933)          ENDFOR
  3594  (2934)  
  3595  (2935)          % One page per block, and the ring addresses histSize of them.
  3596  (2936)          histSize(t) =: histPages
  3597  (2937)          MONITOR_CALL(221B, nameBuf, startAddr, histPages)
  3598  (2938)      ENDROUTINE
  3599  (2939)  
  3600  (2940)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER) : histOpen(t)
  3601  (2941)          INTEGER : dev, i
  3602  (2942)          BOOLEAN : failed
  3603  (2943)  
  3604  (2944)          IF t < 1 THEN
  3605  (2945)              0 RETURN
  3606  (2946)          ENDIF
  3607  (2947)          IF histDev(t) >< 0 THEN
  3608  (2948)              histDev(t) RETURN                 % already open
  3609  (2949)          ENDIF
  3610  (2950)  
  3611  (2951)          % "(SYSTEM)CHATH" + the table index as one digit. The index is 1..8 so
  3612  (2952)          % a single digit always fits, and digitZero + t is the character for it.
  3613  (2953)          %
  3614  (2954)          % BUILT WITH A LOOP, not eight assignments. The first version of this
  3615  (2955)          % copied the five stem bytes out one at a time, which was five chances
  3616  (2956)          % to type the wrong subscript and would have needed rewriting the moment
  3617  (2957)          % the name changed - which it then did, on the very next build.
  3618  (2958)          FOR i IN 1:lenHistUser DO
  3619  (2959)              histUser(i - 1) =: histName(i - 1)
  3620  (2960)          ENDFOR
  3621  (2961)          FOR i IN 1:5 DO
  3622  (2962)              histStem(i - 1) =: histName(lenHistUser + i - 1)
  3623  (2963)          ENDFOR
  3624  (2964)          digitZero + t =: histName(lenHistUser + 5)
  3625  (2965)  
  3626  (2966)          % A FLAG, NOT THE DEVICE NUMBER. This file's own header says it in as
  3627  (2967)          % many words - "leaves 46 in the device number when the file is not
  3628  (2968)          % there - NOT zero. Never test the number".
  3629  (2969)          %
  3630  (2970)          % AND THE FLAG IS CLEARED AFTER THE ON BLOCK, NOT BEFORE. logOpen right
  3631  (2971)          % above records why, measured in CHAT.PLNC on 2026-08-19: "cleared
  3632  (2972)          % before, the handler body had already run and every open reported
  3633  (2973)          % failure while returning a good device". The first version of this
  3634  (2974)          % routine cleared it before - so a PERFECTLY GOOD open was reported as
  3635  (2975)          % a failure, the create branch ran, and the final "IF failed THEN
  3636  (2976)          % 0 =: dev" then threw away the device it had just been given.
  3637  (2977)          0 =: dev
  3638  (2978)          ON ROUTINEERROR DO
  3639  (2979)              TRUE =: failed
  3640  (2980)          ENDON
  3641  (2981)          FALSE =: failed
  3642  (2982)  
  3643  (2983)          MON50(histName(0:lenHistUser + 5), histType, 2) =: dev
  3644  (2984)  
  3645  (2985)          IF failed THEN
  3646  (2986)              % ---- MON50 CANNOT CREATE A FILE --------------------------------
  3647  (2987)              %
  3648  (2988)              % Quoting the name does NOTHING - measured in CHAT.PLNC and written
  3649  (2989)              % down beside makeLog above: "CreateFile is 221B and it is a
  3650  (2990)              % separate call". The first version of this routine quoted the name
  3651  (2991)              % and believed it, so no history file was ever created and the only
  3652  (2992)              % one that ever worked was the one made BY HAND from a terminal.
  3653  (2993)              %
  3654  (2994)              % Same shape as makeLog: fill the buffer with SPACES first so
  3655  (2995)              % whatever was in the rest is never read as part of the name, then
  3656  (2996)              % copy the full specification INCLUDING the type over the front.
  3657  (2997)              %
  3658  (2998)              % PAGES = THE RING'S SIZE, so every block the ring will ever
  3659  (2999)              % address exists from the start. A file that grows as it is written
  3660  (3000)              % would work for an append, but this writes block (id MOD size)
  3661  (3001)              % straight away and the last block is touched long before the ones
  3662  (3002)              % before it.
  3663  (3003)              makeHistFile(t)
  3664  (3004)  
  3665  (3005)              ON ROUTINEERROR DO
  3666  (3006)                  TRUE =: failed
  3667  (3007)              ENDON
  3668  (3008)              FALSE =: failed
  3669  (3009)              MON50(histName(0:lenHistUser + 5), histType, 2) =: dev
  3670  (3010)          ENDIF
  3671  (3011)  
  3672  (3012)          % STILL FAILED MEANS NO HISTORY, AND THAT MUST LEAVE ZERO BEHIND -
  3673  (3013)          % not 46, not whatever MON50 happened to write. Everything downstream
  3674  (3014)          % tests histDev against 0 to decide whether there is a file at all.
  3675  (3015)          IF failed THEN
  3676  (3016)              0 =: dev
  3677  (3017)          ENDIF
  3678  (3018)  
  3679  (3019)          % NO histNext RECOVERY SCAN. A version on 2026-09-02 tried to rebuild
  3680  (3020)          % histNext by reading every block's stored id, so on-disk history
  3681  (3021)          % would survive an RT-load. It is REMOVED because it is UNSAFE on a
  3682  (3022)          % CONTINUOUS file: the unwritten pages are NOT guaranteed zero, and a
  3683  (3023)          % garbage block whose first word happens to equal its own block number
  3684  (3024)          % was counted as a real message - MEASURED as a fresh room reporting
  3685  (3025)          % held=23 after two lines. Surviving a reload needs histNext persisted,
  3686  (3026)          % or a much stricter block-validity test (id AND a sane name AND
  3687  (3027)          % lengths); until one is written and PROVED, history is best-effort and
  3688  (3028)          % starts empty after a reload, which is the behaviour that shipped
  3689  (3029)          % before the scan.
  3690  (3030)  
  3691  (3031)          dev =: histDev(t)
  3692  (3032)          dev RETURN
  3693  (3033)      ENDROUTINE
  3694  (3034)  
  3695  (3035)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3696  (3036)      % Write one message into the ring.
  3697  (3037)      %
  3698  (3038)      % ONE BYTE PER WORD, AND NO PACKING. A block is 256 words and the biggest
  3699  (3039)      % message is a 16-byte name plus 200 bytes of text - 219 words with three
  3700  (3040)      % words of header, which fits with room to spare. Packing two bytes to a
  3701  (3041)      % word would save space in a block that is already mostly empty, and would
  3702  (3042)      % buy a shift-and-mask on every byte in both directions for it. The block
  3703  (3043)      % is the unit the machine gives us; there is nothing to gain by filling it.
  3704  (3044)      %
  3705  (3045)      %   word 0   the id
  3706  (3046)      %   word 1   how many bytes of name
  3707  (3047)      %   word 2   how many bytes of text
  3708  (3048)      %   then     the name, then the text
  3709  (3049)      %
  3710  (3050)      % The id goes in the block as well as in the table because the table does
  3711  (3051)      % not survive a restart and the file does. Reading a block back and finding
  3712  (3052)      % an id that is not the one the ring expects is how a half-written or
  3713  (3053)      % never-written block is told from a real one.
  3714  (3054)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3715  (3055)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : &
  3716  (3056)              histSave(t, slot, textAt, textLength)
  3717  (3057)          INTEGER : dev, i, at, id, blk, nLen, tLen
  3718  (3058)  
  3719  (3059)          IF t < 1 THEN
  3720  (3060)              RETURN
  3721  (3061)          ENDIF
  3722  (3062)          % ---- SAY WHAT HAPPENED, INTO THE LOG --------------------------------
  3723  (3063)          %
  3724  (3064)          % Three builds have now gone to the machine on a THEORY about why no
  3725  (3065)          % history file appears - the filename, then the user, then the create
  3726  (3066)          % call - and each time the only evidence was "OPENED 0 TIMES", which
  3727  (3067)          % says nothing about WHICH step failed or whether this code ran at all.
  3728  (3068)          %
  3729  (3069)          % One log line ends that, the same way printing the LDN and the status
  3730  (3070)          % ended the no-wait hunt in the client. It costs one line per message
  3731  (3071)          % and comes out the moment the answer is known.
  3732  (3072)          histOpen(t) =: dev
  3733  (3073)          logLine('SV hist slot      ', t)
  3734  (3074)          logLine('SV hist dev       ', dev)
  3735  (3075)  
  3736  (3076)          IF dev = 0 THEN
  3737  (3077)              RETURN                            % no history; the room still works
  3738  (3078)          ENDIF
  3739  (3079)  
  3740  (3080)          memberLen(slot) =: nLen
  3741  (3081)          textLength =: tLen
  3742  (3082)  
  3743  (3083)          % BOUNDED, because PLANC checks no array bound and a long name plus a
  3744  (3084)          % long text written past the end of histBlock would corrupt whatever
  3745  (3085)          % the loader put after it.
  3746  (3086)          IF nLen > 16 THEN
  3747  (3087)              16 =: nLen
  3748  (3088)          ENDIF
  3749  (3089)          % histHdrNew, not 3: the stamp and the six calendar words sit in
  3750  (3090)          % front of the name now, so the text has that much less room.
  3751  (3091)          IF histHdrNew + nLen + tLen > histWords THEN
  3752  (3092)              histWords - histHdrNew - nLen =: tLen
  3753  (3093)          ENDIF
  3754  (3094)  
  3755  (3095)          histNext(t) =: id
  3756  (3096)          id =: histBlock(0)
  3757  (3097)          nLen =: histBlock(1)
  3758  (3098)          tLen =: histBlock(2)
  3759  (3099)  
  3760  (3100)          % ---- WHEN, ASKED ONCE AND STORED AS THE MACHINE GIVES IT -----
  3761  (3101)          % Read HERE rather than passed in, because this is the moment the
  3762  (3102)          % line is kept - a time taken earlier and carried down would be
  3763  (3103)          % the time of something else.
  3764  (3104)          MN113(histCal)
  3765  (3105)          histStamp =: histBlock(3)
  3766  (3106)          histCal(1) =: histBlock(4)        % second
  3767  (3107)          histCal(2) =: histBlock(5)        % minute
  3768  (3108)          histCal(3) =: histBlock(6)        % hour
  3769  (3109)          histCal(4) =: histBlock(7)        % day
  3770  (3110)          histCal(5) =: histBlock(8)        % month
  3771  (3111)          histCal(6) =: histBlock(9)        % FULL year, 1998 not 98
  3772  (3112)          histHdrNew =: at
  3773  (3113)  
  3774  (3114)          FOR i IN 1:nLen DO
  3775  (3115)              memberName(slot, i) =: histBlock(at)
  3776  (3116)              at + 1 =: at
  3777  (3117)          ENDFOR
  3778  (3118)          FOR i IN 1:tLen DO
  3779  (3119)              inBuf(textAt + i - 1) =: histBlock(at)
  3780  (3120)              at + 1 =: at
  3781  (3121)          ENDFOR
  3782  (3122)  
  3783  (3123)          % THE RING, and it is this one line. No compaction, no rewrite, no
  3784  (3124)          % check for "is it full" - block id MOD size is where this message
  3785  (3125)          % lives and the one it displaces is the one N messages ago.
  3786  (3126)          id - ((id / histSize(t)) * histSize(t)) =: blk
  3787  (3127)  
  3788  (3128)          ON ROUTINEERROR DO
  3789  (3129)              0 =: dev                          % a failed write must not unwind
  3790  (3130)          ENDON
  3791  (3131)          MN120(dev, 0, histBlock, blk, histWords)
  3792  (3132)  
  3793  (3133)          id + 1 =: histNext(t)
  3794  (3134)      ENDROUTINE
  3795  (3135)  
  3796  (3136)      %
  3797  (3137)      % A separate routine from sameRoom because the two read from DIFFERENT
  3798  (3138)      % TABLES. One routine taking "which table" would be a flag deciding what the
  3799  (3139)      % code means - it reads fine and goes wrong in silence the day a caller
  3800  (3140)      % passes the wrong one.
  3801  (3141)      %
  3802  (3142)      % DECLARED HERE, ABOVE buildWho, because PLANC needs it declared before it
  3803  (3143)      % is called - the same reason sameRoom had to move up when /who was fixed.
  3804  (3144)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3805  (3145)      ROUTINE VOID, BOOLEAN (INTEGER, INTEGER) : sameRoomAs(slot, row)
  3806  (3146)          INTEGER : i
  3807  (3147)          IF lenMbrRoom(slot) >< rmtRoomLen(row) THEN
  3808  (3148)              FALSE RETURN
  3809  (3149)          ENDIF
  3810  (3150)          FOR i IN 1:lenMbrRoom(slot) DO
  3811  (3151)              IF mbrRoom(slot, i) >< rmtRoom(row, i) THEN
  3812  (3152)                  FALSE RETURN
  3813  (3153)              ENDIF
  3814  (3154)          ENDFOR
  3815  (3155)          TRUE RETURN
  3816  (3156)      ENDROUTINE
  3817  (3157)  
  3818  (3158)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3819  (3159)      % Build the answer to /who: every seated member's name, one space between.
  3820  (3160)      %
  3821  (3161)      % The name field is EMPTY - the room is answering, not a member - and the
  3822  (3162)      % names all go in the text. One message, not one per member: a message each
  3823  (3163)      % would multiply the scarcest thing XMSG has by the size of the room. The
  3824  (3164)      % measurement is in DOC/BRINGUP-ORDER-AND-TRAPS-2026-08-18.md: "Data
  3825  (3165)      % transmit blocks" has a limit of TEN and a 20-user burst hits it.
  3826  (3166)      %
  3827  (3167)      % BOUNDED, AND THE BOUND IS NOT DECORATION. outBuf holds 256 bytes and the
  3828  (3168)      % header eats four, so 252 are left; sixteen seats of sixteen characters
  3829  (3169)      % plus separators come to 271. A full room of long names would therefore
  3830  (3170)      % write past the end of outBuf, and PLANC checks no array bound - it would
  3831  (3171)      % simply corrupt whatever the loader put next. Names that do not fit are
  3832  (3172)      % dropped, which is a worse answer than a longer buffer would give and a
  3833  (3173)      % far better one than a corrupted program.
  3834  (3174)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3835  (3175)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER) : buildWho(askerSlot)
  3836  (3176)          INTEGER : i, j, at, textAt, textLen, room
  3837  (3177)  
  3838  (3178)          kWho =: outBuf(0)
  3839  (3179)          0 =: outBuf(1)                        % no name: the ROOM is answering
  3840  (3180)          2 =: at
  3841  (3181)          0 =: outBuf(at)                       % text length, high byte
  3842  (3182)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)                   % text length, low byte, filled in below
  3843  (3183)          at + 2 =: textAt
  3844  (3184)          textAt =: at
  3845  (3185)          0 =: textLen
  3846  (3186)  
  3847  (3187)          FOR i IN 1:maxSeats DO
  3848  (3188)              % THE ASKER'S ROOM ONLY - and this test was MISSING until
  3849  (3189)              % 2026-08-22, which made /who the one thing on this server that
  3850  (3190)              % crossed a room boundary. Measured on D100: TESTER alone in LOBBY,
  3851  (3191)              % ANNA in GENERAL, and TESTER's /who answered
  3852  (3192)              %     CHAT: in (LOBBY): TESTER ANNA
  3853  (3193)              % The client's "in (LOBBY)" label made the answer not merely too
  3854  (3194)              % long but FALSE - it named a room ANNA was not in. Messages were
  3855  (3195)              % isolated the whole time; only the roster leaked, which is why
  3856  (3196)              % this survived a two-room test that watched what people SAID.
  3857  (3197)              %
  3858  (3198)              % sameRoom is the same compare the broadcast uses. Using it here
  3859  (3199)              % rather than a fresh loop is the point: the roster and the
  3860  (3200)              % broadcast must never disagree about who is in the room.
  3861  (3201)              IF memberUsed(i) = 1 THEN
  3862  (3202)                IF sameRoom(i, askerSlot) THEN
  3863  (3203)                  % What this name needs: a separator if it is not the first,
  3864  (3204)                  % then the name itself.
  3865  (3205)                  memberLen(i) =: room
  3866  (3206)                  IF textLen > 0 THEN
  3867  (3207)                      room + 1 =: room
  3868  (3208)                  ENDIF
  3869  (3209)  
  3870  (3210)                  IF textLen + room <= 250 THEN
  3871  (3211)                      IF textLen > 0 THEN
  3872  (3212)                          % A BYTE FROM A BYTES ARRAY, not a string literal.
  3873  (3213)                          % "' ' =: outBuf(at)" is what stood here and the
  3874  (3214)                          % compiler answered
  3875  (3215)                          %   1082 (422)/BUILDWHO *** ERROR
  3876  (3216)                          %        - ILLEGAL DATA TYPE "OUTBUF"
  3877  (3217)                          % because 'x' is a STRING and outBuf(at) is a BYTE.
  3878  (3218)                          % Copying out of a one-byte BYTES gives the element the
  3879  (3219)                          % type it wants - the same move oneChar uses on the
  3880  (3220)                          % client side for printing.
  3881  (3221)                          spaceByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  3882  (3222)                          at + 1 =: at
  3883  (3223)                          textLen + 1 =: textLen
  3884  (3224)                      ENDIF
  3885  (3225)                      FOR j IN 1:memberLen(i) DO
  3886  (3226)                          memberName(i, j) =: outBuf(at)
  3887  (3227)                          at + 1 =: at
  3888  (3228)                      ENDFOR
  3889  (3229)                      textLen + memberLen(i) =: textLen
  3890  (3230)                  ENDIF
  3891  (3231)                ENDIF
  3892  (3232)              ENDIF
  3893  (3233)          ENDFOR
  3894  (3234)  
  3895  (3235)          % ---- AND THE PEOPLE ON THE OTHER MACHINES --------------------------
  3896  (3236)          %
  3897  (3237)          % Phase C. Same room, but on a peer, shown QUALIFIED - "ANNA@D102" -
  3898  (3238)          % because two machines can each have an ANNA and a bare name would make
  3899  (3239)          % them look like one person. The qualification is what makes a federated
  3900  (3240)          % room honest rather than merely bigger.
  3901  (3241)          %
  3902  (3242)          % ROOM MATCHED THE SAME WAY as the local test: byte for byte on the
  3903  (3243)          % spelling, because a room IS its name. The comparison is against the
  3904  (3244)          % ASKER's room, not against anything the peer said about rooms.
  3905  (3245)          FOR i IN 1:maxRemote DO
  3906  (3246)              IF rmtSystem(i) >< 0 THEN
  3907  (3247)                  IF sameRoomAs(askerSlot, i) THEN
  3908  (3248)                      % name + '@' + 'D' + up to 5 digits, plus a separator
  3909  (3249)                      rmtNameLen(i) + 8 =: room
  3910  (3250)                      IF textLen + room <= 250 THEN
  3911  (3251)                          IF textLen > 0 THEN
  3912  (3252)                              spaceByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  3913  (3253)                              at + 1 =: at
  3914  (3254)                              textLen + 1 =: textLen
  3915  (3255)                          ENDIF
  3916  (3256)                          FOR j IN 1:rmtNameLen(i) DO
  3917  (3257)                              rmtName(i, j) =: outBuf(at)
  3918  (3258)                              at + 1 =: at
  3919  (3259)                          ENDFOR
  3920  (3260)                          atByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  3921  (3261)                          at + 1 =: at
  3922  (3262)                          putSysLabel(rmtSystem(i), at) =: at
  3923  (3263)                          at - textAt =: textLen
  3924  (3264)                      ENDIF
  3925  (3265)                  ENDIF
  3926  (3266)              ENDIF
  3927  (3267)          ENDFOR
  3928  (3268)  
  3929  (3269)          % Now the length is known. It cannot exceed 250, so the high byte the
  3930  (3270)          % format carries stays zero.
  3931  (3271)          at - textAt =: textLen
  3932  (3272)          textLen =: outBuf(3)
  3933  (3273)          at RETURN
  3934  (3274)      ENDROUTINE
  3935  (3275)  
  3936  (3276)  
  3937  (3277)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3938  (3278)      % Build the answer to /topic: the ROOM NAME in the name field, the topic in
  3939  (3279)      % the text. An empty text means the room has no topic yet - the client says
  3940  (3280)      % so in words rather than showing a blank line.
  3941  (3281)      %
  3942  (3282)      % The room name goes in the NAME field on purpose. It is the one message
  3943  (3283)      % that is about a room rather than about a person, and putting the room
  3944  (3284)      % where a nickname normally sits means the client can label it without
  3945  (3285)      % having to remember which room it asked about - which matters, because a
  3946  (3286)      % /join can land between the question and the answer.
  3947  (3287)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3948  (3288)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER) : buildTopic(slot)
  3949  (3289)          INTEGER : i, at, t, len
  3950  (3290)  
  3951  (3291)          kTopic =: outBuf(0)
  3952  (3292)          lenMbrRoom(slot) =: outBuf(1)
  3953  (3293)          2 =: at
  3954  (3294)          FOR i IN 1:lenMbrRoom(slot) DO
  3955  (3295)              mbrRoom(slot, i) =: outBuf(at)
  3956  (3296)              at + 1 =: at
  3957  (3297)          ENDFOR
  3958  (3298)  
  3959  (3299)          findTopic(slot) =: t
  3960  (3300)          0 =: len
  3961  (3301)          IF t >< 0 THEN
  3962  (3302)              topicTextLen(t) =: len
  3963  (3303)          ENDIF
  3964  (3304)  
  3965  (3305)          0 =: outBuf(at)                       % text length, high byte
  3966  (3306)          len =: outBuf(at + 1)                  % low byte; len <= 64
  3967  (3307)          at + 2 =: at
  3968  (3308)          IF t >< 0 THEN
  3969  (3309)              FOR i IN 1:len DO
  3970  (3310)                  topicText(t, i) =: outBuf(at)
  3971  (3311)                  at + 1 =: at
  3972  (3312)              ENDFOR
  3973  (3313)          ENDIF
  3974  (3314)          at RETURN
  3975  (3315)      ENDROUTINE
  3976  (3316)  
  3977  (3317)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3978  (3318)      % Build the answer to /list: every room that somebody is sitting in, with
  3979  (3319)      % how many people are in it - "LOBBY(2) GENERAL(1)".
  3980  (3320)      %
  3981  (3321)      % THE ROOM LIST IS DERIVED FROM THE SEATS, NOT KEPT. There is no room
  3982  (3322)      % table to go stale: a room exists exactly as long as somebody is in it,
  3983  (3323)      % which is the same rule the broadcast already follows. A room with a topic
  3984  (3324)      % and nobody in it does NOT appear - it is not a place you can talk to.
  3985  (3325)      %
  3986  (3326)      % The walk is O(seats squared) and that is fine at sixteen: for each seat,
  3987  (3327)      % look back over the earlier seats and skip it if its room has already been
  3988  (3328)      % counted. No temporary room table, so nothing to size wrong.
  3989  (3329)      %
  3990  (3330)      % BOUNDED like buildWho, and for the same reason - see the note there.
  3991  (3331)      % Rooms that do not fit are dropped rather than written past the end.
  3992  (3332)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  3993  (3333)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER : buildRooms
  3994  (3334)          INTEGER : i, j, at, textLen, count, need, tens, ones
  3995  (3335)          BOOLEAN : already
  3996  (3336)  
  3997  (3337)          kRooms =: outBuf(0)
  3998  (3338)          0 =: outBuf(1)                        % no name: the SERVER is answering
  3999  (3339)          2 =: at
  4000  (3340)          0 =: outBuf(at)                       % text length, high byte
  4001  (3341)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)                   % low byte, filled in below
  4002  (3342)          at + 2 =: at
  4003  (3343)          0 =: textLen
  4004  (3344)  
  4005  (3345)          FOR i IN 1:maxSeats DO
  4006  (3346)              IF memberUsed(i) = 1 THEN
  4007  (3347)                  % Has an earlier seat already named this room?
  4008  (3348)                  %
  4009  (3349)                  % GUARDED, because seat 1 has no earlier seats and "FOR j IN
  4010  (3350)                  % 1:0" is an EMPTY RANGE. This compiler checks nothing, so an
  4011  (3351)                  % empty FOR is not a thing to rely on - the first room would be
  4012  (3352)                  % compared against seat 0, which is outside the array.
  4013  (3353)                  FALSE =: already
  4014  (3354)                  IF i > 1 THEN
  4015  (3355)                      FOR j IN 1:(i - 1) DO
  4016  (3356)                          IF memberUsed(j) = 1 THEN
  4017  (3357)                              IF sameRoom(i, j) THEN
  4018  (3358)                                  TRUE =: already
  4019  (3359)                              ENDIF
  4020  (3360)                          ENDIF
  4021  (3361)                      ENDFOR
  4022  (3362)                  ENDIF
  4023  (3363)  
  4024  (3364)                  IF NOT already THEN
  4025  (3365)                      % Count everybody in it, including seat i itself.
  4026  (3366)                      0 =: count
  4027  (3367)                      FOR j IN 1:maxSeats DO
  4028  (3368)                          IF memberUsed(j) = 1 THEN
  4029  (3369)                              IF sameRoom(i, j) THEN
  4030  (3370)                                  count + 1 =: count
  4031  (3371)                              ENDIF
  4032  (3372)                          ENDIF
  4033  (3373)                      ENDFOR
  4034  (3374)  
  4035  (3375)                      % name + "(" + up to two digits + ")" + a leading space
  4036  (3376)                      % once there is something to separate from.
  4037  (3377)                      lenMbrRoom(i) + 4 =: need
  4038  (3378)                      IF textLen > 0 THEN
  4039  (3379)                          need + 1 =: need
  4040  (3380)                      ENDIF
  4041  (3381)  
  4042  (3382)                      IF textLen + need <= 250 THEN
  4043  (3383)                          IF textLen > 0 THEN
  4044  (3384)                              spaceByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  4045  (3385)                              at + 1 =: at
  4046  (3386)                              textLen + 1 =: textLen
  4047  (3387)                          ENDIF
  4048  (3388)                          FOR j IN 1:lenMbrRoom(i) DO
  4049  (3389)                              mbrRoom(i, j) =: outBuf(at)
  4050  (3390)                              at + 1 =: at
  4051  (3391)                          ENDFOR
  4052  (3392)                          textLen + lenMbrRoom(i) =: textLen
  4053  (3393)  
  4054  (3394)                          openParen(0) =: outBuf(at)
  4055  (3395)                          at + 1 =: at
  4056  (3396)                          textLen + 1 =: textLen
  4057  (3397)  
  4058  (3398)                          % At most sixteen seats, so at most two digits. Written
  4059  (3399)                          % straight out rather than through a general routine -
  4060  (3400)                          % a loop for a number that cannot exceed 16 would be
  4061  (3401)                          % more code than the thing it replaces.
  4062  (3402)                          count / 10 =: tens
  4063  (3403)                          count - (tens * 10) =: ones
  4064  (3404)                          IF tens > 0 THEN
  4065  (3405)                              digitZero + tens =: outBuf(at)
  4066  (3406)                              at + 1 =: at
  4067  (3407)                              textLen + 1 =: textLen
  4068  (3408)                          ENDIF
  4069  (3409)                          digitZero + ones =: outBuf(at)
  4070  (3410)                          at + 1 =: at
  4071  (3411)                          textLen + 1 =: textLen
  4072  (3412)  
  4073  (3413)                          closeParen(0) =: outBuf(at)
  4074  (3414)                          at + 1 =: at
  4075  (3415)                          textLen + 1 =: textLen
  4076  (3416)                      ENDIF
  4077  (3417)                  ENDIF
  4078  (3418)              ENDIF
  4079  (3419)          ENDFOR
  4080  (3420)  
  4081  (3421)          textLen =: outBuf(3)
  4082  (3422)          at RETURN
  4083  (3423)      ENDROUTINE
  4084  (3424)  
  4085  (3425)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4086  (3426)      % Build the answer to /who FROM SOMEBODY WITH NO SEAT: every room, and the
  4087  (3427)      % people in each.
  4088  (3428)      %
  4089  (3429)      %     LOBBY: TESTER OLAV  GENERAL: ANNA@D102
  4090  (3430)      %
  4091  (3431)      % Rooms separated by TWO spaces, people inside a room by one. No parsing on
  4092  (3432)      % the client side at all - it prints the text as it stands. That is on
  4093  (3433)      % purpose: a format nobody parses cannot be parsed wrongly, and this answer
  4094  (3434)      % exists to be READ by a person who has just been told they are nowhere.
  4095  (3435)      %
  4096  (3436)      % THE ROOM WALK IS buildRooms', DELIBERATELY. Same O(seats squared) skip of
  4097  (3437)      % rooms an earlier seat already named, same rule that a room exists exactly
  4098  (3438)      % as long as somebody is in it. If the two ever disagree, /list and /who
  4099  (3439)      % would name different sets of rooms, which is the kind of small lie that
  4100  (3440)      % takes an afternoon to chase.
  4101  (3441)      %
  4102  (3442)      % REMOTE PEOPLE ARE INCLUDED, qualified - "ANNA@D102" - and their room comes
  4103  (3443)      % from the peer's own report. A remote room with nobody local in it WILL
  4104  (3444)      % therefore be missed, because the walk starts from local seats. That is a
  4105  (3445)      % known gap, not an oversight: fixing it means walking the remote table for
  4106  (3446)      % rooms too and de-duplicating across both, and it is not worth the code
  4107  (3447)      % until somebody has actually sat alone in a room on another machine.
  4108  (3448)      %
  4109  (3449)      % BOUNDED at 250 like its neighbours - see the note on buildWho. What does
  4110  (3450)      % not fit is dropped, never written past the end of outBuf.
  4111  (3451)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4112  (3452)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER : buildAllWho
  4113  (3453)          INTEGER : i, j, k, at, textAt, textLen, need
  4114  (3454)          BOOLEAN : already
  4115  (3455)  
  4116  (3456)          kAllWho =: outBuf(0)
  4117  (3457)          0 =: outBuf(1)                        % no name: the SERVER is answering
  4118  (3458)          2 =: at
  4119  (3459)          0 =: outBuf(at)                       % text length, high byte
  4120  (3460)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)                   % low byte, filled in below
  4121  (3461)          at + 2 =: textAt
  4122  (3462)          textAt =: at
  4123  (3463)          0 =: textLen
  4124  (3464)  
  4125  (3465)          FOR i IN 1:maxSeats DO
  4126  (3466)              IF memberUsed(i) = 1 THEN
  4127  (3467)  
  4128  (3468)                  % Has an earlier seat already named this room? Guarded for the
  4129  (3469)                  % same reason buildRooms guards it: seat 1 has no earlier seats
  4130  (3470)                  % and an empty FOR range is not a thing this compiler promises.
  4131  (3471)                  FALSE =: already
  4132  (3472)                  IF i > 1 THEN
  4133  (3473)                      FOR j IN 1:(i - 1) DO
  4134  (3474)                          IF memberUsed(j) = 1 THEN
  4135  (3475)                              IF sameRoom(i, j) THEN
  4136  (3476)                                  TRUE =: already
  4137  (3477)                              ENDIF
  4138  (3478)                          ENDIF
  4139  (3479)                      ENDFOR
  4140  (3480)                  ENDIF
  4141  (3481)  
  4142  (3482)                  IF NOT already THEN
  4143  (3483)                      % ---- the room name, then a colon ----------------------
  4144  (3484)                      % Two spaces before it once there is something to separate
  4145  (3485)                      % from, so the groups read apart at a glance.
  4146  (3486)                      lenMbrRoom(i) + 1 =: need
  4147  (3487)                      IF textLen > 0 THEN
  4148  (3488)                          need + 2 =: need
  4149  (3489)                      ENDIF
  4150  (3490)  
  4151  (3491)                      IF textLen + need <= 250 THEN
  4152  (3492)                          IF textLen > 0 THEN
  4153  (3493)                              spaceByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  4154  (3494)                              at + 1 =: at
  4155  (3495)                              spaceByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  4156  (3496)                              at + 1 =: at
  4157  (3497)                          ENDIF
  4158  (3498)                          FOR j IN 1:lenMbrRoom(i) DO
  4159  (3499)                              mbrRoom(i, j) =: outBuf(at)
  4160  (3500)                              at + 1 =: at
  4161  (3501)                          ENDFOR
  4162  (3502)                          colonByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  4163  (3503)                          at + 1 =: at
  4164  (3504)                          at - textAt =: textLen
  4165  (3505)  
  4166  (3506)                          % ---- everybody local in it ------------------------
  4167  (3507)                          FOR j IN 1:maxSeats DO
  4168  (3508)                              IF memberUsed(j) = 1 THEN
  4169  (3509)                                  IF sameRoom(i, j) THEN
  4170  (3510)                                      memberLen(j) + 1 =: need
  4171  (3511)                                      IF textLen + need <= 250 THEN
  4172  (3512)                                          spaceByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  4173  (3513)                                          at + 1 =: at
  4174  (3514)                                          FOR k IN 1:memberLen(j) DO
  4175  (3515)                                              memberName(j, k) =: outBuf(at)
  4176  (3516)                                              at + 1 =: at
  4177  (3517)                                          ENDFOR
  4178  (3518)                                          at - textAt =: textLen
  4179  (3519)                                      ENDIF
