  4180  (3520)                                  ENDIF
  4181  (3521)                              ENDIF
  4182  (3522)                          ENDFOR
  4183  (3523)  
  4184  (3524)                          % ---- and everybody remote in it -------------------
  4185  (3525)                          % sameRoomAs compares a SEAT's room against a remote
  4186  (3526)                          % row, and seat i is in this room by construction, so
  4187  (3527)                          % it is the right thing to compare against.
  4188  (3528)                          FOR j IN 1:maxRemote DO
  4189  (3529)                              IF rmtSystem(j) >< 0 THEN
  4190  (3530)                                  IF sameRoomAs(i, j) THEN
  4191  (3531)                                      % name + '@' + 'D' + up to 5 digits + space
  4192  (3532)                                      rmtNameLen(j) + 8 =: need
  4193  (3533)                                      IF textLen + need <= 250 THEN
  4194  (3534)                                          spaceByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  4195  (3535)                                          at + 1 =: at
  4196  (3536)                                          FOR k IN 1:rmtNameLen(j) DO
  4197  (3537)                                              rmtName(j, k) =: outBuf(at)
  4198  (3538)                                              at + 1 =: at
  4199  (3539)                                          ENDFOR
  4200  (3540)                                          atByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  4201  (3541)                                          at + 1 =: at
  4202  (3542)                                          putSysLabel(rmtSystem(j), at) =: at
  4203  (3543)                                          at - textAt =: textLen
  4204  (3544)                                      ENDIF
  4205  (3545)                                  ENDIF
  4206  (3546)                              ENDIF
  4207  (3547)                          ENDFOR
  4208  (3548)                      ENDIF
  4209  (3549)                  ENDIF
  4210  (3550)              ENDIF
  4211  (3551)          ENDFOR
  4212  (3552)  
  4213  (3553)          % An EMPTY text is a real answer here and the client says so in words:
  4214  (3554)          % it means nobody is anywhere, which is different from "I could not
  4215  (3555)          % tell you". Leaving the length at zero is the whole implementation.
  4216  (3556)          at - textAt =: textLen
  4217  (3557)          textLen =: outBuf(3)
  4218  (3558)          at RETURN
  4219  (3559)      ENDROUTINE
  4220  (3560)  
  4221  (3561)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4222  (3562)      % Build a message whose name comes from a member slot rather than from the
  4223  (3563)      % incoming buffer - used for Said, where the speaker is named by the room
  4224  (3564)      % and NOT by whatever the sender claimed.
  4225  (3565)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4226  (3566)      % ONE OF OUR MESSAGES, built by the LIBRARY.
  4227  (3567)      %
  4228  (3568)      % The sixteen lines that wrote the kind, the name length, the name, a
  4229  (3569)      % two-byte length and the text are gone; cmEncAt does exactly that and is
  4230  (3570)      % checked by CHATTST on every build, so the encoder that SHIPS is the
  4231  (3571)      % encoder that is TESTED. The client's buildOwn was changed the same way.
  4232  (3572)      %
  4233  (3573)      % THE NAME IS COPIED FLAT FIRST, and that is not laziness. memberName is
  4234  (3574)      % TWO-DIMENSIONAL - memberName(slot, i) - and the library takes BYTES.
  4235  (3575)      % Taking a row as memberName(slot, 1:n) would be a subarray with computed
  4236  (3576)      % bounds, and this project has a measured case of that form compiling
  4237  (3577)      % clean, running, and writing NOTHING AT ALL (see drawBar in CHAT.PLNC).
  4238  (3578)      % Three obvious lines beat a form that is not understood.
  4239  (3579)      %
  4240  (3580)      % nameFlat, outBuf and inBuf are THREE DIFFERENT ARRAYS, which the library
  4241  (3581)      % requires: two subarrays of one array passed to a single routine corrupts
  4242  (3582)      % both and compiles clean.
  4243  (3583)      %
  4244  (3584)      % The text length is now written as two real bytes rather than a zero and
  4245  (3585)      % one, so a line of 256 characters or more no longer goes out mis-stated.
  4246  (3586)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : &
  4247  (3587)              buildFromSlot(kind, slot, textAt, textLength)
  4248  (3588)          INTEGER : i
  4249  (3589)  
  4250  (3590)          FOR i IN 1:memberLen(slot) DO
  4251  (3591)              memberName(slot, i) =: nameFlat(i - 1)
  4252  (3592)          ENDFOR
  4253  (3593)  
  4254  (3594)          cmEncAt(outBuf, kind, nameFlat, memberLen(slot), &
  4255  (3595)                  inBuf, textAt, textLength, 256) RETURN
  4256  (3596)      ENDROUTINE
  4257  (3597)  
  4258  (3598)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4259  (3599)      % Copy a name out of the incoming buffer into a member slot.
  4260  (3600)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4261  (3601)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : copyNameIn(slot, at, length)
  4262  (3602)          INTEGER : i
  4263  (3603)          FOR i IN 1:length DO
  4264  (3604)              inBuf(at + i - 1) =: memberName(slot, i)
  4265  (3605)          ENDFOR
  4266  (3606)          length =: memberLen(slot)
  4267  (3607)      ENDROUTINE
  4268  (3608)  
  4269  (3609)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4270  (3610)      % Remember which room a member joined.
  4271  (3611)      %
  4272  (3612)      % The room arrives in the JOIN'S TEXT FIELD - the client puts it there
  4273  (3613)      % because there is one port now and the room can no longer be inferred from
  4274  (3614)      % which port the message came in on.
  4275  (3615)      %
  4276  (3616)      % A JOIN WITH NO ROOM IS PUT IN THE HOME ROOM rather than refused. An older
  4277  (3617)      % client that predates this change sends an empty text, and refusing it
  4278  (3618)      % would take the room out from under somebody whose only crime is not having
  4279  (3619)      % been rebuilt yet. LOBBY is where they used to be, so LOBBY is where they
  4280  (3620)      % go.
  4281  (3621)      %
  4282  (3622)      % Bounded at sixteen, the width of the column. PLANC checks no array bound,
  4283  (3623)      % so this test is the only thing between a long room name and whatever the
  4284  (3624)      % loader put next.
  4285  (3625)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4286  (3626)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : copyRoomIn(slot, at, length)
  4287  (3627)          INTEGER : i, take
  4288  (3628)  
  4289  (3629)          length =: take
  4290  (3630)          IF take > 16 THEN
  4291  (3631)              16 =: take
  4292  (3632)          ENDIF
  4293  (3633)  
  4294  (3634)          IF take <= 0 THEN
  4295  (3635)              FOR i IN 1:lenHomeRoom DO
  4296  (3636)                  homeRoom(i - 1) =: mbrRoom(slot, i)
  4297  (3637)              ENDFOR
  4298  (3638)              lenHomeRoom =: lenMbrRoom(slot)
  4299  (3639)              RETURN
  4300  (3640)          ENDIF
  4301  (3641)  
  4302  (3642)          FOR i IN 1:take DO
  4303  (3643)              inBuf(at + i - 1) =: mbrRoom(slot, i)
  4304  (3644)          ENDFOR
  4305  (3645)          take =: lenMbrRoom(slot)
  4306  (3646)      ENDROUTINE
  4307  (3647)  
  4308  (3648)      % A CLIENT OLD ENOUGH TO CARRY NO VERSION LEAVES THIS EMPTY, on purpose -
  4309  (3649)      % an unknown build reads as "unknown", not as a guess.
  4310  (3650)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : copyVersionIn(slot, at, length)
  4311  (3651)          INTEGER : i, take
  4312  (3652)  
  4313  (3653)          0 =: memberVerLen(slot)
  4314  (3654)          length =: take
  4315  (3655)          IF take > maxVerLen THEN
  4316  (3656)              maxVerLen =: take
  4317  (3657)          ENDIF
  4318  (3658)          IF take <= 0 THEN
  4319  (3659)              RETURN
  4320  (3660)          ENDIF
  4321  (3661)  
  4322  (3662)          FOR i IN 1:take DO
  4323  (3663)              inBuf(at + i - 1) =: memberVer(slot, i)
  4324  (3664)          ENDFOR
  4325  (3665)          take =: memberVerLen(slot)
  4326  (3666)      ENDROUTINE
  4327  (3667)  
  4328  (3668)      % WHERE THE ROOM ENDS AND THE VERSION BEGINS, inside a Join's text field -
  4329  (3669)      % the first '|', searched only within THIS message's own text so a room
  4330  (3670)      % name that runs into whatever inBuf held before it is never read.
  4331  (3671)      %
  4332  (3672)      % Returns -1 when there is none, which is exactly what an OLDER client's
  4333  (3673)      % Join looks like: the whole field is the room name and nothing rides
  4334  (3674)      % after it.
  4335  (3675)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER) : findJoinSep(at, length)
  4336  (3676)          INTEGER : i
  4337  (3677)          IF length > 0 THEN
  4338  (3678)              FOR i IN 0:length - 1 DO
  4339  (3679)                  IF inBuf(at + i) = verSepByte(0) THEN
  4340  (3680)                      at + i RETURN
  4341  (3681)                  ENDIF
  4342  (3682)              ENDFOR
  4343  (3683)          ENDIF
  4344  (3684)          0 - 1 RETURN
  4345  (3685)      ENDROUTINE
  4346  (3686)  
  4347  (3687)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4348  (3688)      % Build the Renamed announcement. Returns its total length.
  4349  (3689)      %
  4350  (3690)      % The NEW name goes in the name field and the OLD one in the text. That is
  4351  (3691)      % not a choice made here - ChatMessageKind.cs states it, and the reason is
  4352  (3692)      % that everybody has a transcript on screen under the old name and no way to
  4353  (3693)      % connect the two without it. CALL THIS BEFORE the slot is changed, while
  4354  (3694)      % the old name is still in it.
  4355  (3695)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4356  (3696)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : &
  4357  (3697)              buildRenamed(slot, newAt, newLength)
  4358  (3698)          INTEGER : i, at
  4359  (3699)          kRenOk =: outBuf(0)
  4360  (3700)          newLength =: outBuf(1)
  4361  (3701)          2 =: at
  4362  (3702)          FOR i IN 1:newLength DO
  4363  (3703)              inBuf(newAt + i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  4364  (3704)              at + 1 =: at
  4365  (3705)          ENDFOR
  4366  (3706)  
  4367  (3707)          % The old name as the text.
  4368  (3708)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  4369  (3709)          memberLen(slot) =: outBuf(at + 1)
  4370  (3710)          at + 2 =: at
  4371  (3711)          FOR i IN 1:memberLen(slot) DO
  4372  (3712)              memberName(slot, i) =: outBuf(at)
  4373  (3713)              at + 1 =: at
  4374  (3714)          ENDFOR
  4375  (3715)          at RETURN
  4376  (3716)      ENDROUTINE
  4377  (3717)  
  4378  (3718)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4379  (3719)      % Send outBuf to one magic number.
  4380  (3720)      %
  4381  (3721)      % XMPFGET reserves a buffer and makes it CURRENT; XMPFWRI writes into the
  4382  (3722)      % current message; XMPFSND sends it. Note the userAddress coercion - the
  4383  (3723)      % guide's own examples write ADDR(buf(0)) FORCE XMUSERADDRESS.
  4384  (3724)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4385  (3725)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER4, INTEGER) : sendTo(magic, length)
  4386  (3726)          XMMSGIDENTIFIER : ident
  4387  (3727)          INTEGER : deadSlot
  4388  (3728)          % Each of the three steps below printed its status on EVERY send while
  4389  (3729)          % the room was being brought up - three lines per message, per member.
  4390  (3730)          % They now speak only when a step fails, which is the case that was
  4391  (3731)          % actually worth seeing: a send that half-succeeds is how a member ends
  4392  (3732)          % up in the room and hearing nothing.
  4393  (3733)          xmpfget(0, length, ident) =: returnStatus
  4394  (3734)          IF returnStatus >< XMOK THEN
  4395  (3735)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL18', 'SV: get failed st=')
  4396  (3736)              OUTPUT(1, 'I6', returnStatus)
  4397  (3737)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL1', '$')
  4398  (3738)              logLine('SV get failed st= ', returnStatus)
  4399  (3739)              RETURN
  4400  (3740)          ENDIF
  4401  (3741)          xmpfwri(0, 0, &
  4402  (3742)              ADDR(outBuf(0)) FORCE XMUSERADDRESS, &
  4403  (3743)              0, length, writtenLength) =: returnStatus
  4404  (3744)          IF returnStatus >< XMOK THEN
  4405  (3745)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL18', 'SV: wri failed st=')
  4406  (3746)              OUTPUT(1, 'I6', returnStatus)
  4407  (3747)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL1', '$')
  4408  (3748)              logLine('SV wri failed st= ', returnStatus)
  4409  (3749)          ENDIF
  4410  (3750)          IF returnStatus >< XMOK THEN
  4411  (3751)              xmpfrel(0, ident) =: returnStatus
  4412  (3752)              RETURN
  4413  (3753)          ENDIF
  4414  (3754)          % XMPSEND, NOT XMPFSND - and this is the whole bug, not a tidy-up.
  4415  (3755)          %
  4416  (3756)          % XMPFSND takes no message parameter. The guide says which one it picks:
  4417  (3757)          % "the current (default) message buffer is assumed, namely the 'port
  4418  (3758)          % current' message if one exists, or, if none, the 'task current'
  4419  (3759)          % message" (ND-60.164.3, XMPFSND, EXPLANATION). XMPFGET above only makes
  4420  (3760)          % the new buffer TASK current - it has no port parameter and cannot make
  4421  (3761)          % it port current. This port has just received a message, so a 'port
  4422  (3762)          % current' message may well still exist, and XMPFSND would send THAT
  4423  (3763)          % instead of the reply we just built. The guide never says whether
  4424  (3764)          % XMPFREL clears the port-current pointer, so relying on it would be a
  4425  (3765)          % guess.
  4426  (3766)          %
  4427  (3767)          % XMPSEND("Send message, not necessarily current") removes the question:
  4428  (3768)          % "The message specified by msgIdentifier is set as 'task current'
  4429  (3769)          % message and as 'port current' message for localPort. Then the message
  4430  (3770)          % will be sent as described under routine XMPFSND."
  4431  (3771)          %
  4432  (3772)          % Appendix G's SERVER gets away with XMPFSND only because it never calls
  4433  (3773)          % XMPFGET - it replies in the very buffer that arrived, which is already
  4434  (3774)          % both port and task current. We cannot copy that: a broadcast needs one
  4435  (3775)          % fresh buffer per member.
  4436  (3776)          xmpsend(sendFlags, ident, myPort, magic) =: returnStatus
  4437  (3777)          IF returnStatus >< XMOK THEN
  4438  (3778)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL18', 'SV: snd failed st=')
  4439  (3779)              OUTPUT(1, 'I6', returnStatus)
  4440  (3780)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL1', '$')
  4441  (3781)              logLine('SV snd failed st= ', returnStatus)
  4442  (3782)  
  4443  (3783)              % ---- A MEMBER WHO IS NO LONGER THERE, IF WE ARE EVER TOLD HERE --
  4444  (3784)              % Two statuses would mean the seat is holding somebody who has gone.
  4445  (3785)              % Both are read off XMP-B02:DEFS rather than guessed:
  4446  (3786)              %
  4447  (3787)              %   XMXEIMA 16915  Invalid magic number
  4448  (3788)              %   XMXEPCL 16932  Remote port closed while message queued
  4449  (3789)              %
  4450  (3790)              % NEITHER OF THEM ACTUALLY TURNS UP for a client killed on this
  4451  (3791)              % machine - measured, see the note on sendFlags. XMPSEND took the
  4452  (3792)              % message for a closed port and answered XMOK. This test is kept
  4453  (3793)              % rather than deleted because it costs one compare and it is right
  4454  (3794)              % if a status ever does arrive - a remote member across a link is
  4455  (3795)              % the obvious untested case - but it is NOT what fixes the leak.
  4456  (3796)              % The XMTRE branch in the receive loop is.
  4457  (3797)              %
  4458  (3798)              % ONLY THESE TWO, and deliberately not "any failure". XMXEMFL
  4459  (3799)              % (message space full) is a bad moment, not a dead member, and
  4460  (3800)              % throwing somebody out of the room because the machine was briefly
  4461  (3801)              % short of buffers would be worse than the bug being fixed. Every
  4462  (3802)              % other status still prints above.
  4463  (3803)              IF returnStatus = XMXEIMA OR returnStatus = XMXEPCL THEN
  4464  (3804)                  findByMagic(magic) =: deadSlot
  4465  (3805)                  IF deadSlot >< 0 THEN
  4466  (3806)                      1 =: memberDead(deadSlot)
  4467  (3807)                      1 =: deadFound
  4468  (3808)                  ENDIF
  4469  (3809)              ENDIF
  4470  (3810)          ENDIF
  4471  (3811)      ENDROUTINE
  4472  (3812)  
  4473  (3813)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4474  (3814)      % Replay what this room remembers, to ONE person, oldest first.
  4475  (3815)      %
  4476  (3816)      % Sent to the JOINER ALONE - the room has not changed, so nobody else has
  4477  (3817)      % anything to learn. Oldest first because that is the order it was said in;
  4478  (3818)      % a screen that fills backwards is a puzzle, not a transcript.
  4479  (3819)      %
  4480  (3820)      % WHERE TO START: the ring holds at most histSize messages, so the oldest
  4481  (3821)      % still present is next - size, or 0 if fewer than size have ever been
  4482  (3822)      % said. Nothing here has to know whether the ring has wrapped; the
  4483  (3823)      % arithmetic gives the same answer either way.
  4484  (3824)      %
  4485  (3825)      % A BLOCK WHOSE ID IS NOT THE ONE EXPECTED IS SKIPPED, and that is what
  4486  (3826)      % makes a half-written or never-written block harmless. It happens for real
  4487  (3827)      % on a file created and not yet filled: SINTRAN gives back whatever the
  4488  (3828)      % blocks held, and without this test the room would replay rubbish as
  4489  (3829)      % though somebody had said it.
  4490  (3830)      %
  4491  (3831)      % NOT SENT ACROSS A TRUNK, and that is deliberate - see the registry.
  4492  (3832)      % Somebody joining on D102 sees what was said ON D102. Relaying history
  4493  (3833)      % would mean deciding whose copy is authoritative and merging rings across
  4494  (3834)      % machines whose clocks differ.
  4495  (3835)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4496  (3836)      % INTEGER4 for the magic, because that is what sendTo takes. An
  4497  (3837)      % XMMSGIDENTIFIER is what xmpfget fills in and is a different thing; the
  4498  (3838)      % magic is the ADDRESS a reply goes to, and it is 32 bits.
  4499  (3839)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER4) : histReplay(t, magic)
  4500  (3840)          INTEGER : dev, id, first, blk, i, at, nLen, tLen, length
  4501  (3841)          INTEGER : base
  4502  (3842)  
  4503  (3843)          IF t < 1 THEN
  4504  (3844)              RETURN
  4505  (3845)          ENDIF
  4506  (3846)          histOpen(t) =: dev
  4507  (3847)          IF dev = 0 THEN
  4508  (3848)              RETURN
  4509  (3849)          ENDIF
  4510  (3850)  
  4511  (3851)          histNext(t) - histSize(t) =: first
  4512  (3852)          IF first < 0 THEN
  4513  (3853)              0 =: first
  4514  (3854)          ENDIF
  4515  (3855)  
  4516  (3856)          FOR id IN first:(histNext(t) - 1) DO
  4517  (3857)              id - ((id / histSize(t)) * histSize(t)) =: blk
  4518  (3858)  
  4519  (3859)              ON ROUTINEERROR DO
  4520  (3860)                  -1 =: histBlock(0)            % unreadable: cannot match an id
  4521  (3861)              ENDON
  4522  (3862)              MN117(dev, 0, histBlock, blk, histWords)
  4523  (3863)  
  4524  (3864)              IF histBlock(0) = id THEN
  4525  (3865)                  histBlock(1) =: nLen
  4526  (3866)                  histBlock(2) =: tLen
  4527  (3867)  
  4528  (3868)                  % ---- WHICH LAYOUT IS THIS BLOCK IN? ------------------
  4529  (3869)                  % A block written before the time was stored has the first
  4530  (3870)                  % character of the NAME in word 3, and a name is ASCII, so
  4531  (3871)                  % it can never equal histStamp. See the note beside the
  4532  (3872)                  % declaration - this is what lets the history already on
  4533  (3873)                  % the three machines go on replaying instead of being
  4534  (3874)                  % thrown away or read as rubbish.
  4535  (3875)                  %
  4536  (3876)                  % base is the word BEFORE the name, so the loops below read
  4537  (3877)                  % base + i exactly as they always did.
  4538  (3878)                  histHdrOld - 1 =: base
  4539  (3879)                  IF histBlock(3) = histStamp THEN
  4540  (3880)                      histHdrNew - 1 =: base
  4541  (3881)                  ENDIF
  4542  (3882)  
  4543  (3883)                  % A BLOCK CAN HOLD RUBBISH AND STILL MATCH ITS ID. The lengths
  4544  (3884)                  % come off the disk, so they are bounded here before they are
  4545  (3885)                  % used as loop limits - a stale block claiming a 30000-byte
  4546  (3886)                  % name would otherwise write clean off the end of outBuf, and
  4547  (3887)                  % PLANC checks no array bound.
  4548  (3888)                  IF nLen < 0 THEN
  4549  (3889)                      0 =: nLen
  4550  (3890)                  ENDIF
  4551  (3891)                  IF nLen > 16 THEN
  4552  (3892)                      16 =: nLen
  4553  (3893)                  ENDIF
  4554  (3894)                  IF tLen < 0 THEN
  4555  (3895)                      0 =: tLen
  4556  (3896)                  ENDIF
  4557  (3897)                  IF tLen > 200 THEN
  4558  (3898)                      200 =: tLen
  4559  (3899)                  ENDIF
  4560  (3900)  
  4561  (3901)                  % AND THE PAIR MUST FIT THE BLOCK. Each length is bounded
  4562  (3902)                  % on its own above, but a stale block can claim two that
  4563  (3903)                  % are each legal and together run off the end - PLANC
  4564  (3904)                  % checks no array bound, so the read would walk into
  4565  (3905)                  % whatever the loader put after histBlock.
  4566  (3906)                  IF base + nLen + tLen > histWords - 1 THEN
  4567  (3907)                      histWords - 1 - base - nLen =: tLen
  4568  (3908)                      IF tLen < 0 THEN
  4569  (3909)                          0 =: tLen
  4570  (3910)                      ENDIF
  4571  (3911)                  ENDIF
  4572  (3912)  
  4573  (3913)                  % FOR, NOT WHILE. PLANC has no WHILE...ENDWHILE: WHILE is a
  4574  (3914)                  % CONTINUE-TEST inside DO ... ENDDO, so "WHILE c DO ... ENDWHILE"
  4575  (3915)                  % is two errors, and the second one - ENDWHILE - is reported as
  4576  (3916)                  % ILLEGAL SYNTAX, which reads like punctuation and is not. The
  4577  (3917)                  % linter caught this before it reached the machine; it would
  4578  (3918)                  % otherwise have cost a four-minute compile to be told.
  4579  (3919)                  kHist =: outBuf(0)
  4580  (3920)  
  4581  (3921)                  % ---- WHEN IT WAS SAID, IN FRONT OF THE NAME --------------
  4582  (3922)                  %
  4583  (3923)                  % Seven bytes in the place a relay header sits, so CHATLIB's
  4584  (3924)                  % trHLen and trStrip handle them with no new code and the
  4585  (3925)                  % frame underneath stays an ordinary chat message.
  4586  (3926)                  %
  4587  (3927)                  % ALL ZERO WHEN THE BLOCK NEVER HELD A TIME. The history
  4588  (3928)                  % already on the three machines was written before there was
  4589  (3929)                  % anywhere to put one, and those blocks still replay - they
  4590  (3930)                  % just cannot say when. Zero is how the client is told that,
  4591  (3931)                  % so it can leave the column blank instead of inventing a
  4592  (3932)                  % moment, which is the whole defect this fixes.
  4593  (3933)                  0 =: outBuf(1)
  4594  (3934)                  0 =: outBuf(2)
  4595  (3935)                  0 =: outBuf(3)
  4596  (3936)                  0 =: outBuf(4)
  4597  (3937)                  0 =: outBuf(5)
  4598  (3938)                  0 =: outBuf(6)
  4599  (3939)                  0 =: outBuf(7)
  4600  (3940)                  IF base = histHdrNew - 1 THEN
  4601  (3941)                      histBlock(4) =: outBuf(1)          % second
  4602  (3942)                      histBlock(5) =: outBuf(2)          % minute
  4603  (3943)                      histBlock(6) =: outBuf(3)          % hour
  4604  (3944)                      histBlock(7) =: outBuf(4)          % day
  4605  (3945)                      histBlock(8) =: outBuf(5)          % month
  4606  (3946)                      histBlock(9) / 256 =: outBuf(6)    % year, HIGH byte
  4607  (3947)                      histBlock(9) MOD 256 =: outBuf(7)  % year, low byte
  4608  (3948)                  ENDIF
  4609  (3949)  
  4610  (3950)                  nLen =: outBuf(8)
  4611  (3951)                  9 =: at
  4612  (3952)                  FOR i IN 1:nLen DO
  4613  (3953)                      histBlock(base + i) =: outBuf(at)
  4614  (3954)                      at + 1 =: at
  4615  (3955)                  ENDFOR
  4616  (3956)                  0 =: outBuf(at)
  4617  (3957)                  tLen =: outBuf(at + 1)
  4618  (3958)                  at + 2 =: at
  4619  (3959)                  FOR i IN 1:tLen DO
  4620  (3960)                      histBlock(base + nLen + i) =: outBuf(at)
  4621  (3961)                      at + 1 =: at
  4622  (3962)                  ENDFOR
  4623  (3963)  
  4624  (3964)                  at =: length
  4625  (3965)                  sendTo(magic, length)
  4626  (3966)              ENDIF
  4627  (3967)          ENDFOR
  4628  (3968)      ENDROUTINE
  4629  (3969)  
  4630  (3970)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4631  (3971)      % Send to everybody in one room, optionally leaving one member out.
  4632  (3972)      %
  4633  (3973)      % ROOM-AWARE NOW, AND IT HAS TO BE. With one port every member of every
  4634  (3974)      % room sits in this one table, so an unfiltered broadcast would put
  4635  (3975)      % GENERAL's traffic on LOBBY's screens. Under the old port-per-room model
  4636  (3976)      % that could not happen - a separate port meant a separate server table -
  4637  (3977)      % which is why this routine had no room test until today.
  4638  (3978)      %
  4639  (3979)      % TWO PARAMETERS, NOT ONE, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE FIX.
  4640  (3980)      %
  4641  (3981)      % There was one slot number doing two unrelated jobs: naming the room to
  4642  (3982)      % send to, AND naming the member to leave out. Slot 0 cannot mean both
  4643  (3983)      % "no room" and "leave nobody out", and it was read as the first while
  4644  (3984)      % every Say, Rename and Leave passed it meaning the second - so the guard
  4645  (3985)      % at the top returned before sending a single letter.
  4646  (3986)      %
  4647  (3987)      % MEASURED ON D100, 2026-08-20, with two clients in LOBBY: neither saw a
  4648  (3988)      % word the other said, while /who listed them both and the Joined notice
  4649  (3989)      % arrived. Those two are exactly the paths that do NOT go through here
  4650  (3990)      % with a zero - /who answers the asker directly, and Joined passes a real
  4651  (3991)      % slot. The room table was never wrong; nothing was ever sent.
  4652  (3992)      %
  4653  (3993)      % So they are separate now and neither is overloaded:
  4654  (3994)      %   roomOfSlot  whose room defines the audience. 0 sends to nobody,
  4655  (3995)      %               because "everybody in the same room as nobody" is nobody.
  4656  (3996)      %   skipSlot    one member to leave out, or 0 to leave nobody out.
  4657  (3997)      %
  4658  (3998)      % roomOfSlot's own seat may already be empty - a member is removed from
  4659  (3999)      % the room BEFORE the room is told they left - and that still works,
  4660  (4000)      % because sameRoom reads only the stored room name, which nothing clears.
  4661  (4001)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4662  (4002)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : broadcast(length, roomOfSlot, skipSlot)
  4663  (4003)          INTEGER : i, sent
  4664  (4004)          IF roomOfSlot = 0 THEN
  4665  (4005)              % Worth a line of its own. "Nobody was in the room" and "this was
  4666  (4006)              % never called" look identical from outside, and telling them apart
  4667  (4007)              % is most of the work when a message does not arrive.
  4668  (4008)              logLine('SV bcast NO ROOM  ', skipSlot)
  4669  (4009)              RETURN
  4670  (4010)          ENDIF
  4671  (4011)  
  4672  (4012)          0 =: sent
  4673  (4013)          FOR i IN 1:maxSeats DO
  4674  (4014)              IF memberUsed(i) >< 0 THEN
  4675  (4015)                  IF i >< skipSlot THEN
  4676  (4016)                      IF sameRoom(i, roomOfSlot) THEN
  4677  (4017)                          sendTo(memberMagic(i), length)
  4678  (4018)                          sent + 1 =: sent
  4679  (4019)                      ENDIF
  4680  (4020)                  ENDIF
  4681  (4021)              ENDIF
  4682  (4022)          ENDFOR
  4683  (4023)  
  4684  (4024)          % HOW MANY IT CHOSE, every time, and this is the instrument the whole
  4685  (4025)          % afternoon of 2026-08-20 was missing. A zero here says the room filter
  4686  (4026)          % rejected everybody; a number with nothing arriving says the sends are
  4687  (4027)          % the problem; no line at all says broadcast was never reached. Three
  4688  (4028)          % different faults that all look the same from a terminal.
  4689  (4029)          logLine('SV bcast sent to  ', sent)
  4690  (4030)      ENDROUTINE
  4691  (4031)  
  4692  (4032)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4693  (4033)      % Empty the seats of members who have vanished, and give each seat back.
  4694  (4034)      %
  4695  (4035)      % CALLED FROM THE TOP OF THE RECEIVE LOOP, never from inside a broadcast -
  4696  (4036)      % see the note on memberDead for why that is not a style choice.
  4697  (4037)      %
  4698  (4038)      % A vanished member costs TWO things and they are not the same thing:
  4699  (4039)      %
  4700  (4040)      %   the seat in this program  memberUsed, which decides who is in the room;
  4701  (4041)      %   the seat in XROUT         the free-connection counter, which decides
  4702  (4042)      %                             whether a new joiner's letter is forwarded at
  4703  (4043)      %                             all. XROUT decremented it to deliver the join
  4704  (4044)      %                             and only the port's owner - this program -
  4705  (4045)      %                             ever puts one back, with XMPINFC.
  4706  (4046)      %
  4707  (4047)      % Clearing only the first leaves the room looking empty in LIST-NAMES while
  4708  (4048)      % it refuses everybody. MEASURED 2026-08-18, before this routine existed:
  4709  (4049)      % CHAT-LOBBY went 16 free to 15 when a client was aborted with ESC
  4710  (4050)      % ("USER BREAK AT 16665B") and stayed at 15 for good.
  4711  (4051)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4712  (4052)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : reapDead
  4713  (4053)          INTEGER : round, i, length
  4714  (4054)  
  4715  (4055)          % Cleared FIRST, not last. A broadcast below can discover another dead
  4716  (4056)          % member - telling the room somebody left is exactly when the next dead
  4717  (4057)          % port gets found - and clearing at the end would throw that mark away.
  4718  (4058)          % Cleared here, a mark raised during our own broadcasts simply re-arms
  4719  (4059)          % this routine for the next message.
  4720  (4060)          0 =: deadFound
  4721  (4061)  
  4722  (4062)          % Bounded rather than a WHILE. Each pass empties at least one seat and a
  4723  (4063)          % pass can only mark members still in the room, so maxSeats passes empty
  4724  (4064)          % the worst room there is. An unbounded loop here would be taking on
  4725  (4065)          % trust that a broadcast to a dying room always terminates, and this
  4726  (4066)          % program has no way to recover if it does not.
  4727  (4067)          FOR round IN 1:maxSeats DO
  4728  (4068)              FOR i IN 1:maxSeats DO
  4729  (4069)                  IF memberDead(i) >< 0 THEN
  4730  (4070)                      0 =: memberDead(i)
  4731  (4071)                      IF memberUsed(i) >< 0 THEN
  4732  (4072)                          % Same order as the kLeave path: build the announcement
  4733  (4073)                          % while the name is still in the slot, empty the seat,
  4734  (4074)                          % THEN tell the room - so the departing member is not
  4735  (4075)                          % sent their own Left notice at a port already gone,
  4736  (4076)                          % which would only mark them dead all over again.
  4737  (4077)                          buildFromSlot(kLeft, i, 0, 0) =: length
  4738  (4078)                          0 =: memberUsed(i)
  4739  (4079)                          broadcast(length, i, i)   % their room, not them
  4740  (4080)  
  4741  (4081)                          xmpinfc(0, myPort, 1, 0) =: returnStatus
  4742  (4082)                          IF returnStatus >< XMOK THEN
  4743  (4083)                              OUTPUT(1, 'AL18', 'SV: infc failed st')
  4744  (4084)                              OUTPUT(1, 'I6', returnStatus)
  4745  (4085)                              OUTPUT(1, 'AL1', '$')
  4746  (4086)                              logLine('SV infc faild st= ', returnStatus)
  4747  (4087)                          ENDIF
  4748  (4088)  
  4749  (4089)                          % Worth one line: an operator watching the room needs to
  4750  (4090)                          % be able to tell "they left" from "they died", because
  4751  (4091)                          % only the second is evidence of anything.
  4752  (4092)                          OUTPUT(1, 'AL21', 'SV: reaped dead slot ')
  4753  (4093)                          OUTPUT(1, 'I6', i)
  4754  (4094)                          OUTPUT(1, 'AL1', '$')
  4755  (4095)                          logLine('SV reaped slot    ', i)
  4756  (4096)                      ENDIF
  4757  (4097)                  ENDIF
  4758  (4098)              ENDFOR
  4759  (4099)          ENDFOR
  4760  (4100)      ENDROUTINE
  4761  (4101)  
  4762  (4102)      % ----- the refusal reasons, worded exactly as ChatRoom.cs words them ----
  4763  (4103)      % Carried on the wire so a client shows the same sentence whichever door it
  4764  (4104)      % came in by. If these and ChatRoom ever diverge, one of the two is wrong.
  4765  (4105)      %
  4766  (4106)      % EACH LENGTH IS WRITTEN OUT beside its string, and that is deliberate. An
  4767  (4107)      % earlier version called LENGTH(reason) - PLANC has no such routine. The one
  4768  (4108)      % it does have is SIZE, whose syntax is "SIZE ident =: var" with no
  4769  (4109)      % parentheses, and which returns the STORAGE size; what that means for a
  4770  (4110)      % BYTES value passed as a routine parameter is not established. Passing the
  4771  (4111)      % count removes the question rather than trading one guess for another.
  4772  (4112)      %
  4773  (4113)      % The cost is that a string and its number must be changed together. That is
  4774  (4114)      % a real risk and it is why they are on adjacent lines.
  4775  (4115)      BYTES : reasonNeedNick := 'a nickname is required'
  4776  (4116)      INTEGER : lenNeedNick := 22
  4777  (4117)      % THE NICK REFUSAL NAMES THE NICK, so it is built rather than stored -
  4778  (4118)      % see refuseNick below. What used to sit here was
  4779  (4119)      %
  4780  (4120)      %     'that nickname is taken'
  4781  (4121)      %
  4782  (4122)      % and "that" is the reader being asked to remember what they typed. They
  4783  (4123)      % may have typed it on a machine across the room ten minutes ago.
  4784  (4124)      BYTES : quoteMark := ''''
  4785  (4125)      BYTES : reasonAlreadyIn    := 'already joined'
  4786  (4126)      INTEGER : lenAlreadyIn := 14
  4787  (4127)      BYTES : reasonRoomFull     := 'the room is full'
  4788  (4128)      INTEGER : lenRoomFull := 16
  4789  (4129)      BYTES : reasonTopicFull    := 'too many rooms have topics'
  4790  (4130)      INTEGER : lenTopicFull := 26
  4791  (4131)  
  4792  (4132)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4793  (4133)      % Send outBuf to a NAMED PORT ON ANOTHER SYSTEM, through XROUT.
  4794  (4134)      %
  4795  (4135)      % THIS SERVER HAS NEVER ORIGINATED A LETTER BEFORE. Everything it sends is a
  4796  (4136)      % reply to a magic number that arrived with a message - sendTo above. A trunk
  4797  (4137)      % is different: we address a machine that has not spoken to us, and may never
  4798  (4138)      % have, so there is no magic to reply to.
  4799  (4139)      %
  4800  (4140)      % The sequence is the COSMOS Programmer Guide's worked example (page 99) and
  4801  (4141)      % is exactly what CHAT.PLNC's sendJoin does:
  4802  (4142)      %
  4803  (4143)      %   XMPBLET   build an XSLET letter header naming SYSTEM and PORT NAME
  4804  (4144)      %   XMPFGET   reserve an XMSG buffer
  4805  (4145)      %   XMPFWRI   write the header, then OUR bytes after it
  4806  (4146)      %   XMPROUT   hand the whole thing to XROUT, which does the lookup
  4807  (4147)      %
  4808  (4148)      % ADDRESSED BY NAME, NEVER BY A REMEMBERED MAGIC. A magic carries a
  4809  (4149)      % generation, so one kept from before the peer restarted is stale in a way
  4810  (4150)      % nothing reports until a send quietly fails. Looking the name up every time
  4811  (4151)      % is what lets a machine that has been switched off for a day come back with
  4812  (4152)      % nobody touching either end.
  4813  (4153)      %
  4814  (4154)      % A NON-ZERO STATUS IS NOT A FAULT HERE. The peer being absent is the normal
  4815  (4155)      % case a trunk exists to survive; the caller records "still not up".
  4816  (4156)      %
  4817  (4157)      % THE SYSTEM IS A NAME, AS BYTES - NOT A NUMBER. That is the whole of this
  4818  (4158)      % trap and it cost THREE compile cycles, all reporting the same thing:
  4819  (4159)      %
  4820  (4160)      %     2521 (1861)/SENDTONAME *** ERROR - ILLEGAL DATA TYPE "XMPBLET"
  4821  (4161)      %
  4822  (4162)      % The error names the CALL, so the first two attempts went looking at the
  4823  (4163)      % PORT NAME argument - was it sliced, was it a parameter - and neither was
  4824  (4164)      % the problem. It is parameter 5, the SYSTEM: XMPBLET wants the system's
  4825  (4165)      % NAME as BYTES ('D102'), and an INTEGER 102 is refused.
  4826  (4166)      %
  4827  (4167)      % CHATMON.PLNC says so in as many words, from 2026-08-20:
  4828  (4168)      %
  4829  (4169)      %     "THE SYSTEM NAME IS BYTES, NOT AN INTEGER ... Declaring it INTEGER
  4830  (4170)      %      drew *** ERROR - ILLEGAL DATA TYPE "XMPBLET" on the CALL rather than
  4831  (4171)      %      on the declaration, which sends you looking at the arguments you
  4832  (4172)      %      changed instead of the one you copied wrong."
  4833  (4173)      %
  4834  (4174)      % It even predicted how the search would go wrong, and it went exactly that
  4835  (4175)      % way. READ THE NOTE THAT NAMES YOUR ERROR before changing anything.
  4836  (4176)      %
  4837  (4177)      % So the number is turned into a name here - 'D' and the decimal digits,
  4838  (4178)      % which is the convention every DEFINE-REMOTE-NAME on these machines uses
  4839  (4179)      % (DEFINE-REMOTE-NAME,,D102,102). peerName is declared with explicit BOUNDS
  4840  (4180)      % and filled at RUNTIME, so it MUST be passed as a subarray saying how much
  4841  (4181)      % of itself is the name - the same shape as the client's roomName.
  4842  (4182)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4843  (4183)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER) : sendToName(system, length)
  4844  (4184)          XMMSGIDENTIFIER : ident
  4845  (4185)          INTEGER : offSet, uLength, wLength, st, nmAt
  4846  (4186)  
  4847  (4187)          % 'D' then the digits - D100, D102, D103.
  4848  (4188)          dByte(0) =: peerName(0)
  4849  (4189)          putPeerDigits(system, 1) =: nmAt
  4850  (4190)  
  4851  (4191)          xmpblet(letterBuf, 64, offSet, 123, peerName(0:nmAt - 1), trkName) =: st
  4852  (4192)          IF st >< XMOK THEN
  4853  (4193)              st RETURN
  4854  (4194)          ENDIF
  4855  (4195)  
  4856  (4196)          xmpfget(0, 200, ident) =: st
  4857  (4197)          IF st >< XMOK THEN
  4858  (4198)              st RETURN
  4859  (4199)          ENDIF
  4860  (4200)  
  4861  (4201)          % The header first...
  4862  (4202)          offSet =: uLength
  4863  (4203)          xmpfwri(0, 0, &
  4864  (4204)              ADDR(letterBuf(0)) FORCE XMUSERADDRESS, &
  4865  (4205)              0, uLength, wLength) =: st
  4866  (4206)  
  4867  (4207)          % ...then our own bytes after it. XROUT reads only the header and
  4868  (4208)          % forwards the rest untouched.
  4869  (4209)          xmpfwri(0, wLength, &
  4870  (4210)              ADDR(outBuf(0)) FORCE XMUSERADDRESS, &
  4871  (4211)              0, length, offSet) =: st
  4872  (4212)  
  4873  (4213)          xmprout(0, ident, trunkPort) =: st
  4874  (4214)          st RETURN
  4875  (4215)      ENDROUTINE
  4876  (4216)  
  4877  (4217)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4878  (4218)      % Which peer row holds this system, or 0.
  4879  (4219)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4880  (4220)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER) : findPeer(system)
  4881  (4221)          INTEGER : i
  4882  (4222)          FOR i IN 1:maxPeers DO
  4883  (4223)              IF peerSystem(i) = system THEN
  4884  (4224)                  i RETURN
  4885  (4225)              ENDIF
  4886  (4226)          ENDFOR
  4887  (4227)          0 RETURN
  4888  (4228)      ENDROUTINE
  4889  (4229)  
  4890  (4230)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4891  (4231)      % Say HELLO to one peer.
  4892  (4232)      %
  4893  (4233)      % ADDRESSED BY NAME THROUGH XROUT, not by a magic number we remember. A
  4894  (4234)      % magic carries a generation, so one kept from before the peer restarted is
  4895  (4235)      % stale in a way that is invisible until a send fails. The name is looked up
  4896  (4236)      % fresh every time, which is exactly what makes a peer that has been away
  4897  (4237)      % come back without anybody touching either machine.
  4898  (4238)      %
  4899  (4239)      % A FAILED SEND IS NOT AN ERROR HERE. A peer that is switched off is the
  4900  (4240)      % normal case this routine exists to handle - the status is recorded by the
  4901  (4241)      % caller as "still not up", not reported as a fault.
  4902  (4242)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4903  (4243)      % A HELLO NOW CARRIES WHAT THIS MACHINE IS CALLED, after the direction
  4904  (4244)      % byte that was always there.
  4905  (4245)      %
  4906  (4246)      % BACKWARD COMPATIBLE, and that is not a hope - it is measured from the
  4907  (4247)      % receiving code. The old handler reads inBuf(textAt) and NOTHING ELSE, so
  4908  (4248)      % a server that has not been upgraded takes the direction byte and ignores
  4909  (4249)      % every byte after it. That is the same property kTrkRly was given, and
  4910  (4250)      % it is what lets three running machines be upgraded one at a time.
  4911  (4251)      %
  4912  (4252)      % A machine with no name sends the one byte exactly as before.
  4913  (4253)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER) : sendHello(system, direction)
  4914  (4254)          INTEGER : at, st, i
  4915  (4255)  
  4916  (4256)          kTrkHello =: outBuf(0)
  4917  (4257)          0 =: outBuf(1)                        % no name: a MACHINE is speaking
  4918  (4258)          2 =: at
  4919  (4259)          0 =: outBuf(at)                       % text length, high byte
  4920  (4260)          1 + lenSysName =: outBuf(at + 1)      % direction byte, then our name
  4921  (4261)          at + 2 =: at
  4922  (4262)          direction =: outBuf(at)               % trkAsk or trkAnswer
  4923  (4263)          at + 1 =: at
  4924  (4264)          IF lenSysName > 0 THEN
  4925  (4265)              FOR i IN 1:lenSysName DO
  4926  (4266)                  sysName(i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  4927  (4267)                  at + 1 =: at
  4928  (4268)              ENDFOR
  4929  (4269)          ENDIF
  4930  (4270)  
  4931  (4271)          sendToName(system, at) =: st
  4932  (4272)          st RETURN
  4933  (4273)      ENDROUTINE
  4934  (4274)  
  4935  (4275)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4936  (4276)      % A peer's member spoke. Show it to our own people in that room.
  4937  (4277)      %
  4938  (4278)      % SENT ONE BY ONE rather than through broadcast(), because broadcast picks
  4939  (4279)      % its audience from a SLOT - "everybody in the same room as this member" -
  4940  (4280)      % and the speaker has no slot here. They are not on this machine.
  4941  (4281)      %
  4942  (4282)      % The room arrives as text and is compared against each member's stored
  4943  (4283)      % room, byte for byte, the same rule as everywhere else: a room IS its name.
  4944  (4284)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  4945  (4285)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : &
  4946  (4286)              deliverRemoteSaid(system, nameAt, nameLength, textAt, textLength)
  4947  (4287)          INTEGER : i, j, at, roomAt, roomLen2, msgAt, msgLen, p, textStart
  4948  (4288)          BOOLEAN : match
  4949  (4289)  
  4950  (4290)          % Split "ROOM/message" at the FIRST slash. A message may well contain
  4951  (4291)          % slashes of its own; the room cannot, so the first one is the divider.
  4952  (4292)          textAt =: roomAt
  4953  (4293)          textAt =: p
  4954  (4294)          DO
  4955  (4295)              WHILE p < textAt + textLength AND inBuf(p) >< slashByte(0)
  4956  (4296)              p + 1 =: p
  4957  (4297)          ENDDO
  4958  (4298)          p - roomAt =: roomLen2
  4959  (4299)  
  4960  (4300)          % No slash at all is a malformed forward - drop it rather than deliver
  4961  (4301)          % something to a room nobody named.
  4962  (4302)          IF p >= textAt + textLength THEN
  4963  (4303)              RETURN
  4964  (4304)          ENDIF
  4965  (4305)          p + 1 =: msgAt
  4966  (4306)          textAt + textLength - msgAt =: msgLen
  4967  (4307)  
  4968  (4308)          FOR i IN 1:maxSeats DO
  4969  (4309)              IF memberUsed(i) = 1 THEN
  4970  (4310)                  TRUE =: match
  4971  (4311)                  IF lenMbrRoom(i) >< roomLen2 THEN
  4972  (4312)                      FALSE =: match
  4973  (4313)                  ELSE
  4974  (4314)                      FOR j IN 1:roomLen2 DO
  4975  (4315)                          IF mbrRoom(i, j) >< inBuf(roomAt + j - 1) THEN
  4976  (4316)                              FALSE =: match
  4977  (4317)                          ENDIF
  4978  (4318)                      ENDFOR
  4979  (4319)                  ENDIF
  4980  (4320)  
  4981  (4321)                  IF match THEN
  4982  (4322)                      % "NAME@Dnnn" in the name field, so a client that already
  4983  (4323)                      % prints "<name>: <text>" needs no change at all.
  4984  (4324)                      kSaid =: outBuf(0)
  4985  (4325)                      2 =: at
  4986  (4326)                      FOR j IN 1:nameLength DO
  4987  (4327)                          inBuf(nameAt + j - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  4988  (4328)                          at + 1 =: at
  4989  (4329)                      ENDFOR
  4990  (4330)                      atByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  4991  (4331)                      at + 1 =: at
  4992  (4332)                      putSysLabel(system, at) =: at
  4993  (4333)                      at - 2 =: outBuf(1)       % the name length, now known
  4994  (4334)  
  4995  (4335)                      0 =: outBuf(at)
  4996  (4336)                      0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  4997  (4337)                      at + 2 =: at
  4998  (4338)                      at =: textStart
  4999  (4339)                      FOR j IN 1:msgLen DO
  5000  (4340)                          IF at < 250 THEN
  5001  (4341)                              inBuf(msgAt + j - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  5002  (4342)                              at + 1 =: at
  5003  (4343)                          ENDIF
  5004  (4344)                      ENDFOR
  5005  (4345)                      at - textStart =: outBuf(textStart - 1)
  5006  (4346)  
  5007  (4347)                      sendTo(memberMagic(i), at)
  5008  (4348)                  ENDIF
  5009  (4349)              ENDIF
  5010  (4350)          ENDFOR
  5011  (4351)      ENDROUTINE
  5012  (4352)  
  5013  (4353)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  5014  (4354)      % THE PRESENCE DIFF - 2026-09-02. Three small routines that turn a peer's
  5015  (4355)      % silent kTrkMbrs table replace into the kJoined / kLeft notices local
  5016  (4356)      % clients already render and count. See the note by oldUsed.
  5017  (4357)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  5018  (4358)  
  5019  (4359)      % Copy every row we hold for one peer into the snapshot, before the
  5020  (4360)      % replace throws them away.
  5021  (4361)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER) : snapshotRem(system)
  5022  (4362)          INTEGER : r, i
  5023  (4363)          FOR r IN 1:maxRemote DO
  5024  (4364)              0 =: oldUsed(r)
  5025  (4365)              IF rmtSystem(r) = system THEN
  5026  (4366)                  1 =: oldUsed(r)
  5027  (4367)                  rmtNameLen(r) =: oldNmLen(r)
  5028  (4368)                  FOR i IN 1:rmtNameLen(r) DO
  5029  (4369)                      rmtName(r, i) =: oldNm(r, i)
  5030  (4370)                  ENDFOR
  5031  (4371)                  rmtRoomLen(r) =: oldRmLen(r)
  5032  (4372)                  FOR i IN 1:rmtRoomLen(r) DO
  5033  (4373)                      rmtRoom(r, i) =: oldRm(r, i)
  5034  (4374)                  ENDFOR
  5035  (4375)              ENDIF
  5036  (4376)          ENDFOR
  5037  (4377)      ENDROUTINE
  5038  (4378)  
  5039  (4379)      % Announce annName@<machine> as joined or left, to every LOCAL seat in
  5040  (4380)      % annRoom. Modeled on deliverRemoteSaid: the person has no seat here, so
  5041  (4381)      % the audience is picked by comparing room names byte for byte, and the
  5042  (4382)      % label comes from putSysLabel exactly as a relayed line's does.
  5043  (4383)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER) : announceGo(kind, system)
  5044  (4384)          INTEGER : i, j, at
  5045  (4385)          BOOLEAN : match
  5046  (4386)  
  5047  (4387)          FOR i IN 1:maxSeats DO
  5048  (4388)              IF memberUsed(i) = 1 THEN
  5049  (4389)                  TRUE =: match
  5050  (4390)                  IF lenMbrRoom(i) >< annRoomLen THEN
  5051  (4391)                      FALSE =: match
  5052  (4392)                  ELSE
  5053  (4393)                      FOR j IN 1:annRoomLen DO
  5054  (4394)                          IF mbrRoom(i, j) >< annRoom(j - 1) THEN
  5055  (4395)                              FALSE =: match
  5056  (4396)                          ENDIF
  5057  (4397)                      ENDFOR
  5058  (4398)                  ENDIF
  5059  (4399)  
  5060  (4400)                  IF match THEN
  5061  (4401)                      kind =: outBuf(0)
  5062  (4402)                      2 =: at
  5063  (4403)                      FOR j IN 1:annNameLen DO
  5064  (4404)                          annName(j - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  5065  (4405)                          at + 1 =: at
  5066  (4406)                      ENDFOR
  5067  (4407)                      atByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  5068  (4408)                      at + 1 =: at
  5069  (4409)                      putSysLabel(system, at) =: at
  5070  (4410)                      at - 2 =: outBuf(1)       % the name length, now known
  5071  (4411)  
  5072  (4412)                      0 =: outBuf(at)           % no text on a presence notice
  5073  (4413)                      0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  5074  (4414)                      at + 2 =: at
  5075  (4415)  
  5076  (4416)                      sendTo(memberMagic(i), at)
  5077  (4417)                  ENDIF
  5078  (4418)              ENDIF
  5079  (4419)          ENDFOR
  5080  (4420)      ENDROUTINE
  5081  (4421)  
  5082  (4422)      % Compare the snapshot against what the replace left, and announce the
  5083  (4423)      % difference. Matching is on NAME AND ROOM, so somebody who changed room
  5084  (4424)      % on their own machine reads here as a leave from the old room and a join
  5085  (4425)      % to the new one - which is exactly what the people in each room saw.
  5086  (4426)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER) : announceDiff(system)
  5087  (4427)          INTEGER : r, k, i
  5088  (4428)          BOOLEAN : match
  5089  (4429)  
  5090  (4430)          % New rows with no snapshot twin: they JOINED.
  5091  (4431)          FOR r IN 1:maxRemote DO
  5092  (4432)              IF rmtSystem(r) = system THEN
  5093  (4433)                  FALSE =: match
  5094  (4434)                  FOR k IN 1:maxRemote DO
  5095  (4435)                      IF oldUsed(k) = 1 THEN
  5096  (4436)                          IF oldNmLen(k) = rmtNameLen(r) AND &
  5097  (4437)                             oldRmLen(k) = rmtRoomLen(r) THEN
  5098  (4438)                              TRUE =: match
  5099  (4439)                              FOR i IN 1:rmtNameLen(r) DO
  5100  (4440)                                  IF oldNm(k, i) >< rmtName(r, i) THEN
  5101  (4441)                                      FALSE =: match
  5102  (4442)                                  ENDIF
  5103  (4443)                              ENDFOR
  5104  (4444)                              FOR i IN 1:rmtRoomLen(r) DO
  5105  (4445)                                  IF oldRm(k, i) >< rmtRoom(r, i) THEN
  5106  (4446)                                      FALSE =: match
  5107  (4447)                                  ENDIF
  5108  (4448)                              ENDFOR
  5109  (4449)                              IF match THEN
  5110  (4450)                                  2 =: oldUsed(k)       % claimed by this row
  5111  (4451)                              ENDIF
  5112  (4452)                          ENDIF
  5113  (4453)                      ENDIF
  5114  (4454)                      WHILE NOT match
  5115  (4455)                  ENDFOR
  5116  (4456)  
  5117  (4457)                  IF NOT match THEN
  5118  (4458)                      rmtNameLen(r) =: annNameLen
  5119  (4459)                      FOR i IN 1:rmtNameLen(r) DO
  5120  (4460)                          rmtName(r, i) =: annName(i - 1)
  5121  (4461)                      ENDFOR
  5122  (4462)                      rmtRoomLen(r) =: annRoomLen
  5123  (4463)                      FOR i IN 1:rmtRoomLen(r) DO
  5124  (4464)                          rmtRoom(r, i) =: annRoom(i - 1)
  5125  (4465)                      ENDFOR
  5126  (4466)                      announceGo(kJoined, system)
  5127  (4467)                  ENDIF
  5128  (4468)              ENDIF
  5129  (4469)          ENDFOR
  5130  (4470)  
  5131  (4471)          % Snapshot rows nothing claimed: they LEFT.
  5132  (4472)          FOR k IN 1:maxRemote DO
  5133  (4473)              IF oldUsed(k) = 1 THEN
  5134  (4474)                  oldNmLen(k) =: annNameLen
  5135  (4475)                  FOR i IN 1:oldNmLen(k) DO
  5136  (4476)                      oldNm(k, i) =: annName(i - 1)
  5137  (4477)                  ENDFOR
  5138  (4478)                  oldRmLen(k) =: annRoomLen
  5139  (4479)                  FOR i IN 1:oldRmLen(k) DO
  5140  (4480)                      oldRm(k, i) =: annRoom(i - 1)
  5141  (4481)                  ENDFOR
  5142  (4482)                  announceGo(kLeft, system)
  5143  (4483)              ENDIF
  5144  (4484)          ENDFOR
  5145  (4485)      ENDROUTINE
  5146  (4486)  
  5147  (4487)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  5148  (4488)      % Send what a local member just said to every peer we believe is up.
  5149  (4489)      %
  5150  (4490)      % ONLY TO PEERS THAT ARE UP. A peer that is Unknown or Down gets nothing:
  5151  (4491)      % the letter would bounce, and a bounce per message per absent machine is a
  5152  (4492)      % lot of noise for something we already know.
  5153  (4493)      %
  5154  (4494)      % The text is rebuilt as "ROOM/message" rather than sent as two fields,
  5155  (4495)      % which keeps it the same shape as the member list and readable on a trace.
  5156  (4496)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  5157  (4497)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : &
  5158  (4498)              forwardSaid(slot, textAt, textLength)
  5159  (4499)          INTEGER : i, j, at, st, textStart
  5160  (4500)  
  5161  (4501)          % STAMPED ONCE, HERE, and the same number goes to every peer. That is
  5162  (4502)          % what lets a machine two hops away tell one line arriving twice from
  5163  (4503)          % two different lines. Stamping inside the loop would give each peer a
  5164  (4504)          % different number and defeat the whole thing.
  5165  (4505)          myLineId + 1 =: myLineId
  5166  (4506)          % WRAPS AT 32000, NOT 65535. A PLANC INTEGER on the ND-100 is SIXTEEN
  5167  (4507)          % BITS SIGNED, so it stops at 32767 - counting past that gives a
  5168  (4508)          % NEGATIVE number, and a negative id would be written out as two
  5169  (4509)          % nonsense bytes. 32000 leaves plenty of room and is obviously safe.
  5170  (4510)          % The window is 64 entries, so a number is long forgotten before it
  5171  (4511)          % can come round again.
  5172  (4512)          IF myLineId > 32000 THEN
  5173  (4513)              1 =: myLineId
  5174  (4514)          ENDIF
  5175  (4515)  
  5176  (4516)          FOR i IN 1:maxPeers DO
  5177  (4517)              IF peerSystem(i) >< 0 THEN
  5178  (4518)                  IF peerState(i) = peerUp THEN
  5179  (4519)  
  5180  (4520)                      % REBUILT FOR EVERY PEER, not built once outside the loop.
  5181  (4521)                      % sendToName hands outBuf to XMPFWRI, and building it once
  5182  (4522)                      % would be fine today - but the moment anything in the send
  5183  (4523)                      % path touches outBuf this becomes a bug that only shows up
  5184  (4524)                      % with two peers, which is the worst kind to find.
  5185  (4525)                      % KIND 53, not 51: it carries who we are and the number we
  5186  (4526)                      % just gave this line, so a machine further out can spot a
  5187  (4527)                      % repeat. hops is one less than the ceiling because this
  5188  (4528)                      % send IS the first hop.
  5189  (4529)                      kTrkRlId =: outBuf(0)
  5190  (4530)                      mySystem / 256 =: outBuf(1)
  5191  (4531)                      mySystem MOD 256 =: outBuf(2)
  5192  (4532)                      maxHops - 1 =: outBuf(3)
  5193  (4533)                      myLineId / 256 =: outBuf(4)
  5194  (4534)                      myLineId MOD 256 =: outBuf(5)
  5195  (4535)                      memberLen(slot) =: outBuf(6)
  5196  (4536)                      7 =: at
  5197  (4537)                      FOR j IN 1:memberLen(slot) DO
  5198  (4538)                          memberName(slot, j) =: outBuf(at)
  5199  (4539)                          at + 1 =: at
  5200  (4540)                      ENDFOR
  5201  (4541)  
  5202  (4542)                      0 =: outBuf(at)
  5203  (4543)                      0 =: outBuf(at + 1)       % low byte, filled in below
  5204  (4544)                      at + 2 =: at
  5205  (4545)                      at =: textStart
  5206  (4546)  
  5207  (4547)                      FOR j IN 1:lenMbrRoom(slot) DO
  5208  (4548)                          mbrRoom(slot, j) =: outBuf(at)
  5209  (4549)                          at + 1 =: at
  5210  (4550)                      ENDFOR
  5211  (4551)                      slashByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  5212  (4552)                      at + 1 =: at
  5213  (4553)  
  5214  (4554)                      % CLAMPED. The text arrived from a client and outBuf is 256
  5215  (4555)                      % bytes; the room and the name have already taken some of
  5216  (4556)                      % it. PLANC checks no bound, so anything long enough would
  5217  (4557)                      % write straight past the end.
  5218  (4558)                      FOR j IN 1:textLength DO
  5219  (4559)                          IF at < 250 THEN
  5220  (4560)                              inBuf(textAt + j - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  5221  (4561)                              at + 1 =: at
  5222  (4562)                          ENDIF
  5223  (4563)                      ENDFOR
  5224  (4564)  
  5225  (4565)                      at - textStart =: outBuf(textStart - 1)
  5226  (4566)  
  5227  (4567)                      % THE STATUS IS READ, not just stored. It was assigned to
  5228  (4568)                      % st and never tested, which made a refused forward the
  5229  (4569)                      % quietest failure in the program: the speaker sees their
  5230  (4570)                      % own echo, the room here is correct, and the line simply
  5231  (4571)                      % never reaches the other machine.
  5232  (4572)                      sendToName(peerSystem(i), at) =: st
  5233  (4573)                      IF st = XMOK THEN
  5234  (4574)                          nForwarded + 1 =: nForwarded
  5235  (4575)                      ELSE
  5236  (4576)                          nFwdBad + 1 =: nFwdBad
  5237  (4577)                      ENDIF
  5238  (4578)                  ENDIF
  5239  (4579)              ENDIF
  5240  (4580)          ENDFOR
  5241  (4581)      ENDROUTINE
  5242  (4582)  
  5243  (4583)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  5244  (4584)      % Pass a line on to every peer EXCEPT the one it came from.
  5245  (4585)      %
  5246  (4586)      % THIS IS THE WHOLE OF PHASE D. D102 and D103 have no trunk between them -
  5247  (4587)      % D100 is the only machine either can reach - so a line typed on D103 gets
  5248  (4588)      % to D102 only if D100 forwards it. Measured on 2026-08-24, with a control:
  5249  (4589)      % a line from D103 reached D100 and did NOT reach D102, while a line from
  5250  (4590)      % D100 reached D102 seconds later on the same client.
  5251  (4591)      %
  5252  (4592)      % TWO THINGS STOP IT LOOPING FOR EVER, and both are needed:
  5253  (4593)      %
  5254  (4594)      %   never back to the sender  a two-machine trunk would otherwise bounce
  5255  (4595)      %                             the same line between the pair immediately;
  5256  (4596)      %   the hop count             a RING would come back round the other way,
  5257  (4597)      %                             where the sender test does not help at all.
  5258  (4598)      %
  5259  (4599)      % WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO, and the difference matters: the hop count makes a
  5260  (4600)      % ring TERMINATE, it does not make it DELIVER ONCE. Trace three machines
  5261  (4601)      % all trunked to each other and a line arrives at some of them twice - once
  5262  (4602)      % direct, once relayed - and the count only stops that repeating for ever.
  5263  (4603)      % De-duplication is Phase E of CHAT-FEDERATION-DESIGN.md and is not written.
  5264  (4604)      % It costs nothing today because the real topology is a CHAIN: D102 and
  5265  (4605)      % D103 each have exactly one trunk, both to D100, so no line reaches either
  5266  (4606)      % of them by two routes.
  5267  (4607)      %
  5268  (4608)      % The ORIGIN travels rather than being taken from the letter. A receiver
  5269  (4609)      % normally qualifies the speaker with the system the letter arrived from,
  5270  (4610)      % and on a forwarded line that is the RELAY - so without this D102 would
  5271  (4611)      % show a D103 user as being on D100.
  5272  (4612)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  5273  (4613)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  5274  (4614)      % Have we seen this line before? Records it if not.
  5275  (4615)      %
  5276  (4616)      % ONE routine that both asks and records, deliberately. Two - a check and
  5277  (4617)      % a separate remember - invite the bug where a caller tests, forgets to
  5278  (4618)      % record, and every copy looks new, which is a dedup that does nothing.
  5279  (4619)      %
  5280  (4620)      % Answers 1 for a line we have not seen (and remembers it), 0 for a
  5281  (4621)      % repeat (and changes nothing).
  5282  (4622)      %
  5283  (4623)      % A line older than the window is treated as NEW again. That is the
  5284  (4624)      % deliberate limit of a fixed window, written down here so it is not read
  5285  (4625)      % as a fault later: a repeat arrives within a hop or two of the original,
  5286  (4626)      % so it is always well inside 64.
  5287  (4627)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  5288  (4628)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER) : lineIsNew(fromSys, id)
  5289  (4629)          INTEGER : i
  5290  (4630)  
  5291  (4631)          FOR i IN 1:seenUsed DO
  5292  (4632)              IF seenSys(i) = fromSys THEN
  5293  (4633)                  IF seenId(i) = id THEN
  5294  (4634)                      0 RETURN
  5295  (4635)                  ENDIF
  5296  (4636)              ENDIF
  5297  (4637)          ENDFOR
  5298  (4638)  
  5299  (4639)          fromSys =: seenSys(seenAt)
  5300  (4640)          id =: seenId(seenAt)
  5301  (4641)  
  5302  (4642)          seenAt + 1 =: seenAt
  5303  (4643)          IF seenAt > maxSeen THEN
  5304  (4644)              1 =: seenAt
  5305  (4645)          ENDIF
  5306  (4646)          IF seenUsed < maxSeen THEN
  5307  (4647)              seenUsed + 1 =: seenUsed
  5308  (4648)          ENDIF
  5309  (4649)  
  5310  (4650)          1 RETURN
  5311  (4651)      ENDROUTINE
  5312  (4652)  
  5313  (4653)      % SEVEN parameters, so SEVEN types. Getting this wrong is not caught by the
  5314  (4654)      % eye: the compiler answers EXPECTS ")" at the first name it has no type
  5315  (4655)      % for, which reads like a bracket fault rather than a counting fault.
  5316  (4656)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, &
  5317  (4657)              INTEGER, INTEGER) : &
  5318  (4658)              relayOn(fromSys, originSys, lineId, hopsLeft, nmAt, nmLen, &
  5319  (4659)              txAt, txLen)
  5320  (4660)          INTEGER : i, j, at, st, textStart
  5321  (4661)  
  5322  (4662)          % Nothing to do once the count runs out. Said plainly rather than
  5323  (4663)          % guarded at the call site, so every caller gets the rule.
  5324  (4664)          IF hopsLeft <= 0 THEN
  5325  (4665)              RETURN
  5326  (4666)          ENDIF
  5327  (4667)  
  5328  (4668)          FOR i IN 1:maxPeers DO
  5329  (4669)              IF peerSystem(i) >< 0 THEN
  5330  (4670)                  IF peerState(i) = peerUp THEN
  5331  (4671)                      IF peerSystem(i) >< fromSys THEN
  5332  (4672)  
  5333  (4673)                          % THE ID IS PASSED ON UNTOUCHED. Renumbering it here
  5334  (4674)                          % would make one line look like two to anybody who
  5335  (4675)                          % can reach us by a second path - which is the whole
  5336  (4676)                          % fault dedup exists to stop.
  5337  (4677)                          kTrkRlId =: outBuf(0)
  5338  (4678)                          originSys / 256 =: outBuf(1)
  5339  (4679)                          originSys MOD 256 =: outBuf(2)
  5340  (4680)                          hopsLeft =: outBuf(3)
  5341  (4681)                          lineId / 256 =: outBuf(4)
  5342  (4682)                          lineId MOD 256 =: outBuf(5)
  5343  (4683)                          nmLen =: outBuf(6)
  5344  (4684)                          7 =: at
  5345  (4685)                          FOR j IN 1:nmLen DO
  5346  (4686)                              inBuf(nmAt + j - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  5347  (4687)                              at + 1 =: at
  5348  (4688)                          ENDFOR
  5349  (4689)  
  5350  (4690)                          0 =: outBuf(at)
  5351  (4691)                          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)     % low byte, filled in below
  5352  (4692)                          at + 2 =: at
  5353  (4693)                          at =: textStart
  5354  (4694)  
  5355  (4695)                          % CLAMPED, for the same reason forwardSaid is: the text
  5356  (4696)                          % came from a stranger and outBuf is 256 bytes. PLANC
  5357  (4697)                          % checks no bound, so a long enough line would write
  5358  (4698)                          % straight past the end of it.
  5359  (4699)                          FOR j IN 1:txLen DO
  5360  (4700)                              IF at < 250 THEN
  5361  (4701)                                  inBuf(txAt + j - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  5362  (4702)                                  at + 1 =: at
  5363  (4703)                              ENDIF
  5364  (4704)                          ENDFOR
  5365  (4705)  
  5366  (4706)                          at - textStart =: outBuf(textStart - 1)
  5367  (4707)                          sendToName(peerSystem(i), at) =: st
  5368  (4708)                          nRelayed + 1 =: nRelayed
  5369  (4709)                      ENDIF
  5370  (4710)                  ENDIF
  5371  (4711)              ENDIF
  5372  (4712)          ENDFOR
  5373  (4713)      ENDROUTINE
  5374  (4714)  
  5375  (4715)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  5376  (4716)      % One second has passed. Age every trunk.
  5377  (4717)      %
  5378  (4718)      % Called ONLY from the idle sleep, so a tick really is a second. Hanging it
  5379  (4719)      % off the message loop would make a busy room time peers out faster, which
  5380  (4720)      % is exactly backwards.
  5381  (4721)      %
  5382  (4722)      % TWO THINGS HAPPEN HERE and they are deliberately separate:
  5383  (4723)      %
  5384  (4724)      %   a peer we HAD goes quiet    -> declare it Down and DROP ITS PEOPLE, so
  5385  (4725)      %                                  nobody is shown chatting from a machine
  5386  (4726)      %                                  that is not answering;
  5387  (4727)      %   a peer that is not Up       -> say hello again, on a doubling wait.
  5388  (4728)      %
  5389  (4729)      % A DOWN PEER KEEPS BEING GREETED. That is the automatic re-join: nobody has
  5390  (4730)      % to notice the machine came back, and no operator command is needed. The
  5391  (4731)      % peer's own hello usually arrives first anyway.
  5392  (4732)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  5393  (4733)      % =======================================================================
  5394  (4734)      % DIRECT MESSAGES
  5395  (4735)      %
  5396  (4736)      % THE ADDRESS FORMS, and what each one costs to resolve:
  5397  (4737)      %
  5398  (4738)      %   RONNY            a bare alias. Looked for on THIS machine and on every
  5399  (4739)      %                    machine we hold a member list for. Exactly one match
  5400  (4740)      %                    is delivered; two or more are REFUSED with the list.
  5401  (4741)      %   D102!RONNY       qualified. Looked for on that machine alone.
  5402  (4742)      %
  5403  (4743)      % A BARE ALIAS MATCHING TWO PEOPLE IS NEVER GUESSED AT. Two machines can
  5404  (4744)      % each hold a RONNY long before they are ever trunked together, so no
  5405  (4745)      % global uniqueness rule could have been applied even in principle - and a
  5406  (4746)      % private message delivered to the wrong person is a privacy failure, not
  5407  (4747)      % an inconvenience. The refusal carries both candidates so the sender can
  5408  (4748)      % retype one of them off the screen.
  5409  (4749)      % =======================================================================
  5410  (4750)  
  5411  (4751)      % Where the '/' is that separates the target from the message, counted
  5412  (4752)      % from "at". -1 if there is none.
  5413  (4753)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER) : findSlash(at, length)
  5414  (4754)          INTEGER : i
  5415  (4755)          IF length < 1 THEN
  5416  (4756)              0 - 1 RETURN
  5417  (4757)          ENDIF
  5418  (4758)          FOR i IN 0:length - 1 DO
  5419  (4759)              IF inBuf(at + i) = slashByte(0) THEN
  5420  (4760)                  i RETURN
  5421  (4761)              ENDIF
  5422  (4762)          ENDFOR
  5423  (4763)          0 - 1 RETURN
  5424  (4764)      ENDROUTINE
  5425  (4765)  
  5426  (4766)      % Where the '!' is inside a target, counted from "at". -1 if there is none,
  5427  (4767)      % which is what says "this is a bare alias".
  5428  (4768)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER) : findBang(at, length)
  5429  (4769)          INTEGER : i
  5430  (4770)          IF length < 1 THEN
  5431  (4771)              0 - 1 RETURN
  5432  (4772)          ENDIF
  5433  (4773)          FOR i IN 0:length - 1 DO
  5434  (4774)              IF inBuf(at + i) = bangByte(0) THEN
  5435  (4775)                  i RETURN
  5436  (4776)              ENDIF
  5437  (4777)          ENDFOR
  5438  (4778)          0 - 1 RETURN
  5439  (4779)      ENDROUTINE
  5440  (4780)  
  5441  (4781)      % "D102" or "102" as the number 102.
  5442  (4782)      %
  5443  (4783)      % trunkNumber already parses digits and is used for the admin commands, but
  5444  (4784)      % it REFUSES anything that is not a digit - so it answers 0 for "D102",
  5445  (4785)      % which is exactly how people write a machine. Leading letters are skipped
  5446  (4786)      % here rather than changing trunkNumber, because the admin commands take a
  5447  (4787)      % bare number and loosening that would let a typo through there.
  5448  (4788)      %
  5449  (4789)      % Answers 0 for anything with no digits at all, and 0 is not a machine.
  5450  (4790)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER) : machineNum(at, length)
  5451  (4791)          INTEGER : i, d, value, seen
  5452  (4792)          0 =: value
  5453  (4793)          0 =: seen
  5454  (4794)          IF length < 1 THEN
  5455  (4795)              0 RETURN
  5456  (4796)          ENDIF
  5457  (4797)          FOR i IN 1:length DO
  5458  (4798)              inBuf(at + i - 1) =: d
  5459  (4799)              IF d >= 48 THEN
  5460  (4800)                  IF d <= 57 THEN
  5461  (4801)                      (value * 10) + (d - 48) =: value
  5462  (4802)                      1 =: seen
  5463  (4803)                  ENDIF
  5464  (4804)              ENDIF
  5465  (4805)          ENDFOR
  5466  (4806)          IF seen = 0 THEN
  5467  (4807)              0 RETURN
  5468  (4808)          ENDIF
  5469  (4809)          value RETURN
  5470  (4810)      ENDROUTINE
  5471  (4811)  
  5472  (4812)      % A MACHINE BY ITS NAME - "FJELL" or "VIDDA" - answering its number.
  5473  (4813)      %
  5474  (4814)      % WHY THIS EXISTS: this server PRINTS machines by name. A direct message
  5475  (4815)      % from somebody on this machine arrives labelled FJELL!BOB, and one from a
  5476  (4816)      % peer arrives VIDDA!SYSTEM, because a name reads far better than D100. But
  5477  (4817)      % the target parser only ever understood DIGITS, so the very label the
  5478  (4818)      % server had just printed could not be typed back at it:
  5479  (4819)      %
  5480  (4820)      %     NOT sent to FJELL!BOB: that machine has no number in it
  5481  (4821)      %
  5482  (4822)      % MEASURED on D100 2026-08-26, and it is the one thing that stopped a reply
  5483  (4823)      % typed in a conversation window from arriving. A product that shows a name
  5484  (4824)      % it will not accept is asking a person to translate for it.
  5485  (4825)      %
  5486  (4826)      % Case folded, exactly as sameRmt does for a person: a name that works in
  5487  (4827)      % one case and fails in the other is a maddening thing to report.
  5488  (4828)      %
