  5489  (4829)      % Answers 0 for a name nobody has, and 0 is not a machine.
  5490  (4830)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER) : machineByName(at, length)
  5491  (4831)          INTEGER : i, j, a, b, same
  5492  (4832)  
  5493  (4833)          IF length < 1 THEN
  5494  (4834)              0 RETURN
  5495  (4835)          ENDIF
  5496  (4836)  
  5497  (4837)          % OURSELVES FIRST. A local conversation is the common case, and it is
  5498  (4838)          % also the one that has to work when no trunk has ever come up.
  5499  (4839)          IF lenSysName = length THEN
  5500  (4840)              1 =: same
  5501  (4841)              FOR j IN 1:length DO
  5502  (4842)                  sysName(j - 1) =: a
  5503  (4843)                  inBuf(at + j - 1) =: b
  5504  (4844)                  IF a > 96 THEN
  5505  (4845)                      a - 32 =: a
  5506  (4846)                  ENDIF
  5507  (4847)                  IF b > 96 THEN
  5508  (4848)                      b - 32 =: b
  5509  (4849)                  ENDIF
  5510  (4850)                  IF a >< b THEN
  5511  (4851)                      0 =: same
  5512  (4852)                  ENDIF
  5513  (4853)              ENDFOR
  5514  (4854)              IF same >< 0 THEN
  5515  (4855)                  mySystem RETURN
  5516  (4856)              ENDIF
  5517  (4857)          ENDIF
  5518  (4858)  
  5519  (4859)          % Then the peers we have been told the names of. A peer that has never
  5520  (4860)          % said its name has pNameLen 0 and simply does not match.
  5521  (4861)          FOR i IN 1:maxPeers DO
  5522  (4862)              IF peerSystem(i) >< 0 THEN
  5523  (4863)                  IF pNameLen(i) = length THEN
  5524  (4864)                      1 =: same
  5525  (4865)                      FOR j IN 1:length DO
  5526  (4866)                          pName(i, j) =: a
  5527  (4867)                          inBuf(at + j - 1) =: b
  5528  (4868)                          IF a > 96 THEN
  5529  (4869)                              a - 32 =: a
  5530  (4870)                          ENDIF
  5531  (4871)                          IF b > 96 THEN
  5532  (4872)                              b - 32 =: b
  5533  (4873)                          ENDIF
  5534  (4874)                          IF a >< b THEN
  5535  (4875)                              0 =: same
  5536  (4876)                          ENDIF
  5537  (4877)                      ENDFOR
  5538  (4878)                      IF same >< 0 THEN
  5539  (4879)                          peerSystem(i) RETURN
  5540  (4880)                      ENDIF
  5541  (4881)                  ENDIF
  5542  (4882)              ENDIF
  5543  (4883)          ENDFOR
  5544  (4884)  
  5545  (4885)          0 RETURN
  5546  (4886)      ENDROUTINE
  5547  (4887)  
  5548  (4888)      % Does the person we hold in remote row "row" have this name?
  5549  (4889)      %
  5550  (4890)      % Case folded exactly as sameName does for a local seat. A direct message
  5551  (4891)      % that works for a local RONNY and fails for a remote ronny would be a
  5552  (4892)      % maddening thing to report and an obvious thing to prevent.
  5553  (4893)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : sameRmt(row, at, length)
  5554  (4894)          INTEGER : j, a, b
  5555  (4895)          IF rmtNameLen(row) >< length THEN
  5556  (4896)              0 RETURN
  5557  (4897)          ENDIF
  5558  (4898)          FOR j IN 1:length DO
  5559  (4899)              rmtName(row, j) =: a
  5560  (4900)              inBuf(at + j - 1) =: b
  5561  (4901)              IF a > 96 THEN a - 32 =: a ENDIF
  5562  (4902)              IF b > 96 THEN b - 32 =: b ENDIF
  5563  (4903)              IF a >< b THEN
  5564  (4904)                  0 RETURN
  5565  (4905)              ENDIF
  5566  (4906)          ENDFOR
  5567  (4907)          1 RETURN
  5568  (4908)      ENDROUTINE
  5569  (4909)  
  5570  (4910)      % Who does this alias mean? Answers into the four dir* variables rather
  5571  (4911)      % than returning, because it has four things to say and PLANC returns one.
  5572  (4912)      %
  5573  (4913)      % wantSys = 0 means "any machine"; otherwise only that one is searched.
  5574  (4914)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : &
  5575  (4915)              resolveTo(at, length, wantSys)
  5576  (4916)          INTEGER : i
  5577  (4917)          0 =: dirCount
  5578  (4918)          0 =: dirLocal
  5579  (4919)          0 =: dirRemote
  5580  (4920)          0 =: dirSystem
  5581  (4921)  
  5582  (4922)          IF wantSys = 0 OR wantSys = mySystem THEN
  5583  (4923)              FOR i IN 1:maxSeats DO
  5584  (4924)                  IF memberUsed(i) = 1 THEN
  5585  (4925)                      IF sameName(i, at, length) = 1 THEN
  5586  (4926)                          dirCount + 1 =: dirCount
  5587  (4927)                          IF dirLocal = 0 THEN
  5588  (4928)                              i =: dirLocal
  5589  (4929)                              mySystem =: dirSystem
  5590  (4930)                          ENDIF
  5591  (4931)                      ENDIF
  5592  (4932)                  ENDIF
  5593  (4933)              ENDFOR
  5594  (4934)          ENDIF
  5595  (4935)  
  5596  (4936)          FOR i IN 1:maxRemote DO
  5597  (4937)              IF rmtSystem(i) >< 0 THEN
  5598  (4938)                  IF wantSys = 0 OR wantSys = rmtSystem(i) THEN
  5599  (4939)                      IF sameRmt(i, at, length) = 1 THEN
  5600  (4940)                          dirCount + 1 =: dirCount
  5601  (4941)                          IF dirRemote = 0 THEN
  5602  (4942)                              IF dirLocal = 0 THEN
  5603  (4943)                                  i =: dirRemote
  5604  (4944)                                  rmtSystem(i) =: dirSystem
  5605  (4945)                              ENDIF
  5606  (4946)                          ENDIF
  5607  (4947)                      ENDIF
  5608  (4948)                  ENDIF
  5609  (4949)              ENDIF
  5610  (4950)          ENDFOR
  5611  (4951)      ENDROUTINE
  5612  (4952)  
  5613  (4953)      % Put "FJELL!" - or "Dnnn!" when nothing is named. Answers the new cursor.
  5614  (4954)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER) : putMachine(sysNum, at)
  5615  (4955)          INTEGER : p
  5616  (4956)          putSysLabel(sysNum, at) =: p
  5617  (4957)          bangByte(0) =: outBuf(p)
  5618  (4958)          p + 1 =: p
  5619  (4959)          p RETURN
  5620  (4960)      ENDROUTINE
  5621  (4961)  
  5622  (4962)      % "Dnnn!NAME" from a LOCAL seat. Answers the new cursor.
  5623  (4963)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : &
  5624  (4964)              putQualSlot(sysNum, slot, at)
  5625  (4965)          INTEGER : i, p
  5626  (4966)          putMachine(sysNum, at) =: p
  5627  (4967)          FOR i IN 1:memberLen(slot) DO
  5628  (4968)              memberName(slot, i) =: outBuf(p)
  5629  (4969)              p + 1 =: p
  5630  (4970)          ENDFOR
  5631  (4971)          p RETURN
  5632  (4972)      ENDROUTINE
  5633  (4973)  
  5634  (4974)      % "Dnnn!NAME" where the name is sitting in inBuf. Answers the new cursor.
  5635  (4975)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : &
  5636  (4976)              putQualIn(sysNum, nameAt, nmLen, at)
  5637  (4977)          INTEGER : i, p
  5638  (4978)          putMachine(sysNum, at) =: p
  5639  (4979)          FOR i IN 1:nmLen DO
  5640  (4980)              inBuf(nameAt + i - 1) =: outBuf(p)
  5641  (4981)              p + 1 =: p
  5642  (4982)          ENDFOR
  5643  (4983)          p RETURN
  5644  (4984)      ENDROUTINE
  5645  (4985)  
  5646  (4986)      % REFUSED, and here is why.
  5647  (4987)      %
  5648  (4988)      % The reason's length comes from MAXINDEX rather than being typed beside
  5649  (4989)      % the literal - a hand-counted length builds clean and shows only as a
  5650  (4990)      % truncated sentence on somebody else's screen.
  5651  (4991)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER4, INTEGER, INTEGER, BYTES) : &
  5652  (4992)              sendDirBad(magic, tgtAt, tgtLen, reason)
  5653  (4993)          INTEGER : i, at, textStart, reasonLen
  5654  (4994)  
  5655  (4995)          kDirBad =: outBuf(0)
  5656  (4996)          tgtLen =: outBuf(1)
  5657  (4997)          2 =: at
  5658  (4998)          FOR i IN 1:tgtLen DO
  5659  (4999)              inBuf(tgtAt + i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  5660  (5000)              at + 1 =: at
  5661  (5001)          ENDFOR
  5662  (5002)  
  5663  (5003)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  5664  (5004)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  5665  (5005)          at + 2 =: at
  5666  (5006)          at =: textStart
  5667  (5007)  
  5668  (5008)          MAXINDEX(reason, 1) + 1 =: reasonLen
  5669  (5009)          FOR i IN 1:reasonLen DO
  5670  (5010)              IF at < 250 THEN
  5671  (5011)                  reason(i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  5672  (5012)                  at + 1 =: at
  5673  (5013)              ENDIF
  5674  (5014)          ENDFOR
  5675  (5015)  
  5676  (5016)          at - textStart =: outBuf(textStart - 1)
  5677  (5017)          dmBad + 1 =: dmBad
  5678  (5018)          sendTo(magic, at)
  5679  (5019)      ENDROUTINE
  5680  (5020)  
  5681  (5021)      % REFUSED BECAUSE THE NAME MEANS MORE THAN ONE PERSON, and here they are.
  5682  (5022)      %
  5683  (5023)      % Carrying the candidates is what makes this usable rather than merely
  5684  (5024)      % annoying: the sender retypes one of them straight off their screen. The
  5685  (5025)      % table is walked a second time - resolveTo only kept the FIRST match,
  5686  (5026)      % because keeping all of them would need a list for a case that is rare.
  5687  (5027)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER4, INTEGER, INTEGER) : &
  5688  (5028)              sendDirAmbig(magic, aliasAt, aliasLen)
  5689  (5029)          INTEGER : i, j, at, textStart, first
  5690  (5030)  
  5691  (5031)          kDirBad =: outBuf(0)
  5692  (5032)          aliasLen =: outBuf(1)
  5693  (5033)          2 =: at
  5694  (5034)          FOR i IN 1:aliasLen DO
  5695  (5035)              inBuf(aliasAt + i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  5696  (5036)              at + 1 =: at
  5697  (5037)          ENDFOR
  5698  (5038)  
  5699  (5039)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  5700  (5040)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  5701  (5041)          at + 2 =: at
  5702  (5042)          at =: textStart
  5703  (5043)  
  5704  (5044)          putWord('which one? ', at) =: at
  5705  (5045)          1 =: first
  5706  (5046)  
  5707  (5047)          FOR i IN 1:maxSeats DO
  5708  (5048)              IF memberUsed(i) = 1 THEN
  5709  (5049)                  IF sameName(i, aliasAt, aliasLen) = 1 THEN
  5710  (5050)                      IF first = 0 THEN
  5711  (5051)                          putWord(', ', at) =: at
  5712  (5052)                      ENDIF
  5713  (5053)                      0 =: first
  5714  (5054)                      IF at < 200 THEN
  5715  (5055)                          putQualSlot(mySystem, i, at) =: at
  5716  (5056)                      ENDIF
  5717  (5057)                  ENDIF
  5718  (5058)              ENDIF
  5719  (5059)          ENDFOR
  5720  (5060)  
  5721  (5061)          FOR i IN 1:maxRemote DO
  5722  (5062)              IF rmtSystem(i) >< 0 THEN
  5723  (5063)                  IF sameRmt(i, aliasAt, aliasLen) = 1 THEN
  5724  (5064)                      IF first = 0 THEN
  5725  (5065)                          putWord(', ', at) =: at
  5726  (5066)                      ENDIF
  5727  (5067)                      0 =: first
  5728  (5068)                      IF at < 200 THEN
  5729  (5069)                          putMachine(rmtSystem(i), at) =: at
  5730  (5070)                          FOR j IN 1:rmtNameLen(i) DO
  5731  (5071)                              rmtName(i, j) =: outBuf(at)
  5732  (5072)                              at + 1 =: at
  5733  (5073)                          ENDFOR
  5734  (5074)                      ENDIF
  5735  (5075)                  ENDIF
  5736  (5076)              ENDIF
  5737  (5077)          ENDFOR
  5738  (5078)  
  5739  (5079)          at - textStart =: outBuf(textStart - 1)
  5740  (5080)          dmBad + 1 =: dmBad
  5741  (5081)          sendTo(magic, at)
  5742  (5082)      ENDROUTINE
  5743  (5083)  
  5744  (5084)      % DELIVERED - and this says to whom, in the form it can be typed back in.
  5745  (5085)      %
  5746  (5086)      % Sent on EVERY delivery and not only the ambiguous ones. Refusing an
  5747  (5087)      % ambiguous alias protects the sender when the collision is visible; this
  5748  (5088)      % protects them when it is NOT - a machine that just went down, a second
  5749  (5089)      % RONNY who logged in a moment ago, a trunk that came back with a different
  5750  (5090)      % user list. A wrong delivery becomes visible at once instead of days
  5751  (5091)      % later, and it costs one line.
  5752  (5092)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER4, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : &
  5753  (5093)              sendDirSent(magic, sysNum, nameAt, nmLen)
  5754  (5094)          INTEGER : at
  5755  (5095)  
  5756  (5096)          kDirSent =: outBuf(0)
  5757  (5097)          0 =: outBuf(1)                     % filled in below
  5758  (5098)          2 =: at
  5759  (5099)          putQualIn(sysNum, nameAt, nmLen, at) =: at
  5760  (5100)          at - 2 =: outBuf(1)
  5761  (5101)  
  5762  (5102)          % AN EMPTY TEXT STILL WRITES BOTH LENGTH BYTES. Leaving them out makes
  5763  (5103)          % the message one byte short and the reader takes the next field from
  5764  (5104)          % the wrong offset - which looks like a corrupt name rather than a
  5765  (5105)          % missing length. Pinned by the C# golden test.
  5766  (5106)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  5767  (5107)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  5768  (5108)          at + 2 =: at
  5769  (5109)  
  5770  (5110)          dmSent + 1 =: dmSent
  5771  (5111)          sendTo(magic, at)
  5772  (5112)      ENDROUTINE
  5773  (5113)  
  5774  (5114)      % HAND IT TO SOMEBODY ON THIS MACHINE.
  5775  (5115)      %
  5776  (5116)      % The sender is named by their SEAT and never by anything in the message -
  5777  (5117)      % otherwise anyone could send a direct message as anyone. That is the same
  5778  (5118)      % rule kSay already follows, and it matters more here, not less.
  5779  (5119)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : &
  5780  (5120)              deliverLocal(fromSlot, toSlot, msgAt, msgLen)
  5781  (5121)          INTEGER : i, at, textStart
  5782  (5122)  
  5783  (5123)          kDirGot =: outBuf(0)
  5784  (5124)          0 =: outBuf(1)
  5785  (5125)          2 =: at
  5786  (5126)          putQualSlot(mySystem, fromSlot, at) =: at
  5787  (5127)          at - 2 =: outBuf(1)
  5788  (5128)  
  5789  (5129)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  5790  (5130)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  5791  (5131)          at + 2 =: at
  5792  (5132)          at =: textStart
  5793  (5133)  
  5794  (5134)          % CLAMPED. The text came from a client and outBuf is 256 bytes; the
  5795  (5135)          % qualified sender has already taken some of it. PLANC checks no bound,
  5796  (5136)          % so a long enough message would write straight past the end.
  5797  (5137)          FOR i IN 1:msgLen DO
  5798  (5138)              IF at < 250 THEN
  5799  (5139)                  inBuf(msgAt + i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  5800  (5140)                  at + 1 =: at
  5801  (5141)              ENDIF
  5802  (5142)          ENDFOR
  5803  (5143)  
  5804  (5144)          at - textStart =: outBuf(textStart - 1)
  5805  (5145)          sendTo(memberMagic(toSlot), at)
  5806  (5146)      ENDROUTINE
  5807  (5147)  
  5808  (5148)      % SEND IT ACROSS A TRUNK.
  5809  (5149)      %
  5810  (5150)      % Same six-byte header as a relayed room line, so the far end can
  5811  (5151)      % de-duplicate it with the machinery that already exists. The text is
  5812  (5152)      % TARGET/message where a room line carries ROOM/message, which leaves the
  5813  (5153)      % name field for the SENDER - the one thing the far end has to display.
  5814  (5154)      %
  5815  (5155)      % THE TARGET GOES OUT UNQUALIFIED. The letter is addressed to that machine
  5816  (5156)      % already, so repeating the machine inside the text would only give the two
  5817  (5157)      % copies a chance to disagree.
  5818  (5158)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, &
  5819  (5159)                             INTEGER) : &
  5820  (5160)              sendDirTrunk(fromSlot, toSys, aliasAt, aliasLen, msgAt, msgLen)
  5821  (5161)          INTEGER : j, at, st, textStart
  5822  (5162)  
  5823  (5163)          % STAMPED HERE, exactly as forwardSaid stamps a room line and for the
  5824  (5164)          % same reason: the number has to come from the ORIGIN, or a machine two
  5825  (5165)          % hops out cannot tell one message arriving twice from two messages.
  5826  (5166)          myLineId + 1 =: myLineId
  5827  (5167)          IF myLineId > 32000 THEN
  5828  (5168)              1 =: myLineId
  5829  (5169)          ENDIF
  5830  (5170)  
  5831  (5171)          kTrkDir =: outBuf(0)
  5832  (5172)          mySystem / 256 =: outBuf(1)
  5833  (5173)          mySystem MOD 256 =: outBuf(2)
  5834  (5174)          maxHops - 1 =: outBuf(3)
  5835  (5175)          myLineId / 256 =: outBuf(4)
  5836  (5176)          myLineId MOD 256 =: outBuf(5)
  5837  (5177)          memberLen(fromSlot) =: outBuf(6)
  5838  (5178)          7 =: at
  5839  (5179)          FOR j IN 1:memberLen(fromSlot) DO
  5840  (5180)              memberName(fromSlot, j) =: outBuf(at)
  5841  (5181)              at + 1 =: at
  5842  (5182)          ENDFOR
  5843  (5183)  
  5844  (5184)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  5845  (5185)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  5846  (5186)          at + 2 =: at
  5847  (5187)          at =: textStart
  5848  (5188)  
  5849  (5189)          FOR j IN 1:aliasLen DO
  5850  (5190)              inBuf(aliasAt + j - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  5851  (5191)              at + 1 =: at
  5852  (5192)          ENDFOR
  5853  (5193)          slashByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  5854  (5194)          at + 1 =: at
  5855  (5195)  
  5856  (5196)          FOR j IN 1:msgLen DO
  5857  (5197)              IF at < 250 THEN
  5858  (5198)                  inBuf(msgAt + j - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  5859  (5199)                  at + 1 =: at
  5860  (5200)              ENDIF
  5861  (5201)          ENDFOR
  5862  (5202)  
  5863  (5203)          at - textStart =: outBuf(textStart - 1)
  5864  (5204)          sendToName(toSys, at) =: st
  5865  (5205)          st RETURN
  5866  (5206)      ENDROUTINE
  5867  (5207)  
  5868  (5208)      % =======================================================================
  5869  (5209)      % A DIRECT MESSAGE COULD NOT BE DELIVERED HERE - TELL THE SENDER'S MACHINE
  5870  (5210)      %
  5871  (5211)      % kTrkDBad, kind 55. Without it a direct message that crosses a trunk and
  5872  (5212)      % finds nobody at the far end DISAPPEARS: the sender was already told
  5873  (5213)      % kDirSent by their own server, so a person who logged out - or renamed - in
  5874  (5214)      % the gap between the two ends agreeing loses the line with nobody told.
  5875  (5215)      %
  5876  (5216)      % THE ORIGIN FIELD IS **US**, not the sender's machine. Every other kind
  5877  (5217)      % uses that field for the machine the line was TYPED on, and lineIsNew
  5878  (5218)      % dedups on the pair (origin, id) - so a refusal we compose is ours. Nothing
  5879  (5219)      % routes by it: the refusal reaches the sender's machine because sendToName
  5880  (5220)      % is told where to put it, not because of anything in the header.
  5881  (5221)      %
  5882  (5222)      % THE ID IS THE FAILED MESSAGE'S, passed straight through. That is what lets
  5883  (5223)      % the far end say WHICH message, and it is why the id has to be handed to
  5884  (5224)      % takeRemDirect rather than left in handleMessage where it is parsed.
  5885  (5225)      %
  5886  (5226)      % The text is TARGET/reason, which is exactly the shape kDirBad already uses
  5887  (5227)      % locally - so the machine that receives this hands it to code it already
  5888  (5228)      % has, and no client changes at all.
  5889  (5229)      % =======================================================================
  5890  (5230)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, &
  5891  (5231)                             INTEGER, BYTES) : &
  5892  (5232)              sendTrkBad(toSys, lineId, senderAt, senderLen, tgtAt, tgtLen, why)
  5893  (5233)          INTEGER : j, at, st, textStart, whyLen
  5894  (5234)  
  5895  (5235)          kTrkDBad =: outBuf(0)
  5896  (5236)          mySystem / 256 =: outBuf(1)
  5897  (5237)          mySystem MOD 256 =: outBuf(2)
  5898  (5238)          maxHops - 1 =: outBuf(3)
  5899  (5239)          lineId / 256 =: outBuf(4)
  5900  (5240)          lineId MOD 256 =: outBuf(5)
  5901  (5241)          senderLen =: outBuf(6)
  5902  (5242)          7 =: at
  5903  (5243)  
  5904  (5244)          FOR j IN 1:senderLen DO
  5905  (5245)              inBuf(senderAt + j - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  5906  (5246)              at + 1 =: at
  5907  (5247)          ENDFOR
  5908  (5248)  
  5909  (5249)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  5910  (5250)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  5911  (5251)          at + 2 =: at
  5912  (5252)          at =: textStart
  5913  (5253)  
  5914  (5254)          FOR j IN 1:tgtLen DO
  5915  (5255)              IF at < 250 THEN
  5916  (5256)                  inBuf(tgtAt + j - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  5917  (5257)                  at + 1 =: at
  5918  (5258)              ENDIF
  5919  (5259)          ENDFOR
  5920  (5260)          slashByte(0) =: outBuf(at)
  5921  (5261)          at + 1 =: at
  5922  (5262)  
  5923  (5263)          % The length comes from MAXINDEX and is never typed beside the literal -
  5924  (5264)          % a hand-counted length builds clean and shows only as a truncated
  5925  (5265)          % sentence on somebody else's screen.
  5926  (5266)          MAXINDEX(why, 1) + 1 =: whyLen
  5927  (5267)          FOR j IN 1:whyLen DO
  5928  (5268)              IF at < 250 THEN
  5929  (5269)                  why(j - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  5930  (5270)                  at + 1 =: at
  5931  (5271)              ENDIF
  5932  (5272)          ENDFOR
  5933  (5273)  
  5934  (5274)          at - textStart =: outBuf(textStart - 1)
  5935  (5275)          sendToName(toSys, at) =: st
  5936  (5276)          st RETURN
  5937  (5277)      ENDROUTINE
  5938  (5278)  
  5939  (5279)      % =======================================================================
  5940  (5280)      % A DIRECT MESSAGE ARRIVES FROM A CLIENT
  5941  (5281)      %
  5942  (5282)      % The text is TARGET/message. Everything that can go wrong is answered with
  5943  (5283)      % kDirBad and a sentence, because the person who typed it is the only one
  5944  (5284)      % who can put it right and they cannot see this machine.
  5945  (5285)      % =======================================================================
  5946  (5286)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : &
  5947  (5287)              handleDirect(slot, textAt, textLength)
  5948  (5288)          INTEGER : cut, tgtAt, tgtLen, msgAt, msgLen
  5949  (5289)          INTEGER : bang, sysNum, aliasAt, aliasLen, row, st
  5950  (5290)  
  5951  (5291)          findSlash(textAt, textLength) =: cut
  5952  (5292)          IF cut < 1 THEN
  5953  (5293)              sendDirBad(memberMagic(slot), textAt, 0, &
  5954  (5294)                  'a direct message is NAME/text - a name, a slash, then what to say')
  5955  (5295)              RETURN
  5956  (5296)          ENDIF
  5957  (5297)  
  5958  (5298)          textAt =: tgtAt
  5959  (5299)          cut =: tgtLen
  5960  (5300)          textAt + cut + 1 =: msgAt
  5961  (5301)          textLength - cut - 1 =: msgLen
  5962  (5302)          IF msgLen < 1 THEN
  5963  (5303)              sendDirBad(memberMagic(slot), tgtAt, tgtLen, &
  5964  (5304)                  'there was nothing after the slash to send')
  5965  (5305)              RETURN
  5966  (5306)          ENDIF
  5967  (5307)  
  5968  (5308)          % Qualified by machine, or a bare alias?
  5969  (5309)          findBang(tgtAt, tgtLen) =: bang
  5970  (5310)          IF bang < 0 THEN
  5971  (5311)              tgtAt =: aliasAt
  5972  (5312)              tgtLen =: aliasLen
  5973  (5313)              0 =: sysNum
  5974  (5314)          ELSE
  5975  (5315)              machineNum(tgtAt, bang) =: sysNum
  5976  (5316)  
  5977  (5317)              % NO DIGITS? THEN TRY IT AS A NAME. This is what makes the label
  5978  (5318)              % the server itself printed - FJELL!BOB, VIDDA!SYSTEM - typeable
  5979  (5319)              % straight back, which is what a reply in a conversation window
  5980  (5320)              % does. Digits are still tried FIRST so D102!RONNY is unaffected
  5981  (5321)              % and costs nothing.
  5982  (5322)              IF sysNum = 0 THEN
  5983  (5323)                  machineByName(tgtAt, bang) =: sysNum
  5984  (5324)              ENDIF
  5985  (5325)  
  5986  (5326)              tgtAt + bang + 1 =: aliasAt
  5987  (5327)              tgtLen - bang - 1 =: aliasLen
  5988  (5328)              IF sysNum = 0 THEN
  5989  (5329)                  sendDirBad(memberMagic(slot), tgtAt, tgtLen, &
  5990  (5330)                      'no machine of that name or number - try D102!RONNY')
  5991  (5331)                  RETURN
  5992  (5332)              ENDIF
  5993  (5333)              IF aliasLen < 1 THEN
  5994  (5334)                  sendDirBad(memberMagic(slot), tgtAt, tgtLen, &
  5995  (5335)                      'there is a machine but no name after it - try D102!RONNY')
  5996  (5336)                  RETURN
  5997  (5337)              ENDIF
  5998  (5338)          ENDIF
  5999  (5339)  
  6000  (5340)          resolveTo(aliasAt, aliasLen, sysNum)
  6001  (5341)  
  6002  (5342)          IF dirCount = 0 THEN
  6003  (5343)              % NOT A QUEUE. There is deliberately no store and forward: a
  6004  (5344)              % message that waits for somebody is a mailbox, a different product
  6005  (5345)              % with different rules, and SINTRAN already has mail for that.
  6006  (5346)              sendDirBad(memberMagic(slot), tgtAt, tgtLen, &
  6007  (5347)                  'nobody of that name is here now, and nothing is kept for later')
  6008  (5348)              RETURN
  6009  (5349)          ENDIF
  6010  (5350)  
  6011  (5351)          IF dirCount > 1 THEN
  6012  (5352)              % NEVER GUESSED AT. Two machines can each hold a RONNY long before
  6013  (5353)              % they are ever trunked together, so no uniqueness rule could have
  6014  (5354)              % been applied even in principle - and a private message delivered
  6015  (5355)              % to the wrong person is a privacy failure, not an inconvenience.
  6016  (5356)              sendDirAmbig(memberMagic(slot), aliasAt, aliasLen)
  6017  (5357)              RETURN
  6018  (5358)          ENDIF
  6019  (5359)  
  6020  (5360)          IF dirLocal > 0 THEN
  6021  (5361)              deliverLocal(slot, dirLocal, msgAt, msgLen)
  6022  (5362)              sendDirSent(memberMagic(slot), mySystem, aliasAt, aliasLen)
  6023  (5363)              RETURN
  6024  (5364)          ENDIF
  6025  (5365)  
  6026  (5366)          % ---- ON ANOTHER MACHINE -------------------------------------------
  6027  (5367)          %
  6028  (5368)          % ONE HOP ONLY, AND THAT IS A DELIBERATE FIRST CUT.
  6029  (5369)          %
  6030  (5370)          % A room line is FLOODED to every peer and de-duplicated at the far
  6031  (5371)          % end. A private message must NOT be: flooding one hands it to machines
  6032  (5372)          % that have no business seeing it, which is a privacy failure dressed
  6033  (5373)          % up as routing. So this sends only to the machine that holds the
  6034  (5374)          % person, and refuses when that machine is not a direct peer.
  6035  (5375)          %
  6036  (5376)          % On the D102 - D100 - D103 chain that means D100 reaches both ends and
  6037  (5377)          % each end reaches only D100. THAT LIMIT IS REAL AND IT IS VISIBLE: the
  6038  (5378)          % sender is told in words rather than the message quietly vanishing.
  6039  (5379)          % Routing onward by the target machine - each hop forwarding to the
  6040  (5380)          % peer that matches - is the next step, and it needs a way to carry a
  6041  (5381)          % refusal back, which no kind does yet.
  6042  (5382)          findPeer(dirSystem) =: row
  6043  (5383)          IF row = 0 THEN
  6044  (5384)              sendDirBad(memberMagic(slot), tgtAt, tgtLen, &
  6045  (5385)                  'they are on a machine this one has no trunk to')
  6046  (5386)              RETURN
  6047  (5387)          ENDIF
  6048  (5388)          IF peerState(row) >< peerUp THEN
  6049  (5389)              sendDirBad(memberMagic(slot), tgtAt, tgtLen, &
  6050  (5390)                  'the trunk to their machine is down just now')
  6051  (5391)              RETURN
  6052  (5392)          ENDIF
  6053  (5393)  
  6054  (5394)          sendDirTrunk(slot, dirSystem, aliasAt, aliasLen, msgAt, msgLen) =: st
  6055  (5395)          sendDirSent(memberMagic(slot), dirSystem, aliasAt, aliasLen)
  6056  (5396)      ENDROUTINE
  6057  (5397)  
  6058  (5398)      % A DIRECT MESSAGE THAT ARRIVED OVER A TRUNK, addressed to somebody here.
  6059  (5399)      %
  6060  (5400)      % The text is TARGET/message with the target UNQUALIFIED - the letter was
  6061  (5401)      % addressed to this machine, so the machine is not repeated inside it.
  6062  (5402)      %
  6063  (5403)      % THE SENDER IS QUALIFIED WITH THE ORIGIN, not with the machine the letter
  6064  (5404)      % came from. On a relayed message those are different, and naming the relay
  6065  (5405)      % would tell the reader to type a reply to the wrong place. The origin
  6066  (5406)      % travels in the header for exactly this reason.
  6067  (5407)      %
  6068  (5408)      % NOT FOUND HERE MEANS DROPPED, and that is worth being honest about: the
  6069  (5409)      % sender was already told DirectSent by their own server, so a person who
  6070  (5410)      % logged out in the second between the two ends disagreeing loses the
  6071  (5411)      % message with nobody told. Closing that needs a refusal that can travel
  6072  (5412)      % back over a trunk, and no kind carries one yet.
  6073  (5413)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, &
  6074  (5414)                          INTEGER) : &
  6075  (5415)              takeRemDirect(origSys, lineId, nameAt, nameLength, textAt, &
  6076  (5416)                            textLength)
  6077  (5417)          INTEGER : cut, tgtAt, tgtLen, msgAt, msgLen, i, at, textStart, st
  6078  (5418)  
  6079  (5419)          findSlash(textAt, textLength) =: cut
  6080  (5420)          IF cut < 1 THEN
  6081  (5421)              RETURN
  6082  (5422)          ENDIF
  6083  (5423)          textAt =: tgtAt
  6084  (5424)          cut =: tgtLen
  6085  (5425)          textAt + cut + 1 =: msgAt
  6086  (5426)          textLength - cut - 1 =: msgLen
  6087  (5427)          IF msgLen < 1 THEN
  6088  (5428)              RETURN
  6089  (5429)          ENDIF
  6090  (5430)  
  6091  (5431)          % THIS MACHINE ONLY. Asking for any machine would find the sender's own
  6092  (5432)          % entry in our remote table and send the message straight back where it
  6093  (5433)          % came from.
  6094  (5434)          resolveTo(tgtAt, tgtLen, mySystem)
  6095  (5435)          IF dirLocal = 0 THEN
  6096  (5436)              % NOT FOUND HERE, AND NOW WE SAY SO. This used to be a bare RETURN
  6097  (5437)              % and the comment above it admitted the message was lost with
  6098  (5438)              % nobody told. The sender's own machine gets a kTrkDBad carrying the
  6099  (5439)              % target we tried and why, and turns it into an ordinary kDirBad for
  6100  (5440)              % the person who typed it.
  6101  (5441)              sendTrkBad(origSys, lineId, nameAt, nameLength, tgtAt, tgtLen, &
  6102  (5442)                         'not logged in') =: st
  6103  (5443)              RETURN
  6104  (5444)          ENDIF
  6105  (5445)  
  6106  (5446)          kDirGot =: outBuf(0)
  6107  (5447)          0 =: outBuf(1)
  6108  (5448)          2 =: at
  6109  (5449)          putQualIn(origSys, nameAt, nameLength, at) =: at
  6110  (5450)          at - 2 =: outBuf(1)
  6111  (5451)  
  6112  (5452)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  6113  (5453)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  6114  (5454)          at + 2 =: at
  6115  (5455)          at =: textStart
  6116  (5456)  
  6117  (5457)          FOR i IN 1:msgLen DO
  6118  (5458)              IF at < 250 THEN
  6119  (5459)                  inBuf(msgAt + i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  6120  (5460)                  at + 1 =: at
  6121  (5461)              ENDIF
  6122  (5462)          ENDFOR
  6123  (5463)  
  6124  (5464)          at - textStart =: outBuf(textStart - 1)
  6125  (5465)          sendTo(memberMagic(dirLocal), at)
  6126  (5466)      ENDROUTINE
  6127  (5467)  
  6128  (5468)      % =======================================================================
  6129  (5469)      % A REFUSAL COMES BACK OVER A TRUNK - TELL THE PERSON WHO TYPED IT
  6130  (5470)      %
  6131  (5471)      % The far end could not deliver, and this is the other half of kTrkDBad.
  6132  (5472)      % The name field holds the SENDER, who is one of ours; the text is
  6133  (5473)      % TARGET/reason.
  6134  (5474)      %
  6135  (5475)      % It becomes an ordinary kDirBad, which is the entire point: the sentence a
  6136  (5476)      % person sees for a failure two machines away is the same sentence, in the
  6137  (5477)      % same shape, as the one they see for a failure on their own machine. No
  6138  (5478)      % client changes at all.
  6139  (5479)      %
  6140  (5480)      % IF THE SENDER HAS GONE, THE REFUSAL IS DROPPED, and that is honest rather
  6141  (5481)      % than lazy: there is nobody to tell. It is also the hole this kind does not
  6142  (5482)      % close - the sender is found by NAME, so somebody who renamed between
  6143  (5483)      % sending and this arriving is not found. Closing THAT needs an identity
  6144  (5484)      % that outlives a rename, which is a bigger change than this one.
  6145  (5485)      % =======================================================================
  6146  (5486)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER, INTEGER) : &
  6147  (5487)              takeRemBad(nameAt, nameLength, textAt, textLength)
  6148  (5488)          INTEGER : cut, tgtAt, tgtLen, whyAt, whyLen, i, at, textStart
  6149  (5489)  
  6150  (5490)          findSlash(textAt, textLength) =: cut
  6151  (5491)          IF cut < 1 THEN
  6152  (5492)              RETURN
  6153  (5493)          ENDIF
  6154  (5494)          textAt =: tgtAt
  6155  (5495)          cut =: tgtLen
  6156  (5496)          textAt + cut + 1 =: whyAt
  6157  (5497)          textLength - cut - 1 =: whyLen
  6158  (5498)          IF whyLen < 1 THEN
  6159  (5499)              RETURN
  6160  (5500)          ENDIF
  6161  (5501)  
  6162  (5502)          % THE SENDER, not the target - this message is addressed to whoever
  6163  (5503)          % typed the line that failed.
  6164  (5504)          resolveTo(nameAt, nameLength, mySystem)
  6165  (5505)          IF dirLocal = 0 THEN
  6166  (5506)              RETURN
  6167  (5507)          ENDIF
  6168  (5508)  
  6169  (5509)          kDirBad =: outBuf(0)
  6170  (5510)          tgtLen =: outBuf(1)
  6171  (5511)          2 =: at
  6172  (5512)          FOR i IN 1:tgtLen DO
  6173  (5513)              inBuf(tgtAt + i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  6174  (5514)              at + 1 =: at
  6175  (5515)          ENDFOR
  6176  (5516)  
  6177  (5517)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  6178  (5518)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  6179  (5519)          at + 2 =: at
  6180  (5520)          at =: textStart
  6181  (5521)  
  6182  (5522)          FOR i IN 1:whyLen DO
  6183  (5523)              IF at < 250 THEN
  6184  (5524)                  inBuf(whyAt + i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  6185  (5525)                  at + 1 =: at
  6186  (5526)              ENDIF
  6187  (5527)          ENDFOR
  6188  (5528)  
  6189  (5529)          at - textStart =: outBuf(textStart - 1)
  6190  (5530)          dmBad + 1 =: dmBad
  6191  (5531)          sendTo(memberMagic(dirLocal), at)
  6192  (5532)      ENDROUTINE
  6193  (5533)  
  6194  (5534)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : peerTick
  6195  (5535)          INTEGER : i, st
  6196  (5536)  
  6197  (5537)          FOR i IN 1:maxPeers DO
  6198  (5538)              IF peerSystem(i) >< 0 THEN
  6199  (5539)  
  6200  (5540)                  peerHeard(i) + 1 =: peerHeard(i)
  6201  (5541)  
  6202  (5542)                  % ---- has a peer we had gone quiet? -------------------------
  6203  (5543)                  IF peerState(i) = peerUp THEN
  6204  (5544)                      IF peerHeard(i) > peerDeadAfter THEN
  6205  (5545)                          peerDown =: peerState(i)
  6206  (5546)  
  6207  (5547)                          % TELL THE ROOM WHO WENT WITH IT - 2026-09-02. The
  6208  (5548)                          % snapshot-and-diff against a table the forget has
  6209  (5549)                          % just emptied announces every one of its members as
  6210  (5550)                          % left, through the same path a kTrkMbrs change uses.
  6211  (5551)                          snapshotRem(peerSystem(i))
  6212  (5552)                          forgetRemote(peerSystem(i))
  6213  (5553)                          announceDiff(peerSystem(i))
  6214  (5554)  
  6215  (5555)                          peerFirstWait =: peerWait(i)
  6216  (5556)                          peerFirstWait =: peerCount(i)
  6217  (5557)                          logLine('SV trunk down sys ', peerSystem(i))
  6218  (5558)                      ELSE
  6219  (5559)                          % ---- THE KEEPALIVE, 2026-09-02 ---------------------
  6220  (5560)                          %
  6221  (5561)                          % Nothing else speaks on an idle trunk, so before this
  6222  (5562)                          % line existed a quiet minute GUARANTEED the down
  6223  (5563)                          % branch above: down, forget, greet, up, every ~75
  6224  (5564)                          % seconds, for ever - the endless "trunk down/up"
  6225  (5565)                          % pairs in the log, and the remote tables wiped on
  6226  (5566)                          % every lap. Two hellos fit inside peerDeadAfter, so
  6227  (5567)                          % one lost letter does not cost the trunk.
  6228  (5568)                          IF peerHeard(i) = peerPingAt OR &
  6229  (5569)                             peerHeard(i) = peerPingAt + peerPingAt THEN
  6230  (5570)                              sendHello(peerSystem(i), trkAsk) =: st
  6231  (5571)                          ENDIF
  6232  (5572)                      ENDIF
  6233  (5573)                  ENDIF
  6234  (5574)  
  6235  (5575)                  % ---- and greet anything that is not up ---------------------
  6236  (5576)                  IF peerState(i) >< peerUp THEN
  6237  (5577)                      peerCount(i) - 1 =: peerCount(i)
  6238  (5578)                      IF peerCount(i) <= 0 THEN
  6239  (5579)                          sendHello(peerSystem(i), trkAsk) =: st
  6240  (5580)  
  6241  (5581)                          % DOUBLE, THEN CAP. The multiply is done before the
  6242  (5582)                          % test so the cap is a ceiling on the WAIT, not on the
  6243  (5583)                          % number of tries - a peer away for a week keeps being
  6244  (5584)                          % greeted every five minutes for ever, which is what
  6245  (5585)                          % makes it come back by itself.
  6246  (5586)                          peerWait(i) * 2 =: peerWait(i)
  6247  (5587)                          IF peerWait(i) > peerMaxWait THEN
  6248  (5588)                              peerMaxWait =: peerWait(i)
  6249  (5589)                          ENDIF
  6250  (5590)                          peerWait(i) =: peerCount(i)
  6251  (5591)                      ENDIF
  6252  (5592)                  ENDIF
  6253  (5593)              ENDIF
  6254  (5594)          ENDFOR
  6255  (5595)      ENDROUTINE
  6256  (5596)  
  6257  (5597)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6258  (5598)      % Ask one peer who is on it. Empty text - the kind IS the question.
  6259  (5599)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6260  (5600)      ROUTINE VOID, INTEGER (INTEGER) : askWho(system)
  6261  (5601)          INTEGER : at, st
  6262  (5602)  
  6263  (5603)          kTrkWho =: outBuf(0)
  6264  (5604)          0 =: outBuf(1)                        % no name: a MACHINE is asking
  6265  (5605)          2 =: at
  6266  (5606)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  6267  (5607)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  6268  (5608)          at + 2 =: at
  6269  (5609)  
  6270  (5610)          sendToName(system, at) =: st
  6271  (5611)          st RETURN
  6272  (5612)      ENDROUTINE
  6273  (5613)  
  6274  (5614)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6275  (5615)      % TELL EVERY PEER WHO IS ON US NOW, WITHOUT BEING ASKED.
  6276  (5616)      %
  6277  (5617)      % THE FAULT THIS FIXES, MEASURED 2026-08-28. A member renamed on D103 and
  6278  (5618)      % D102 went on advertising the OLD name in /who for as long as it was
  6279  (5619)      % watched. Everything else followed from that one fact: a private message
  6280  (5620)      % addressed to the name D102 was showing was sent correctly, found nobody
  6281  (5621)      % of that name at the far end, and was DROPPED WITH NOBODY TOLD.
  6282  (5622)      %
  6283  (5623)      % WHY IT DRIFTED. The member list only ever moved in ONE place - as the
  6284  (5624)      % reply to kTrkWho - and kTrkWho is only ever asked when a HELLO arrives.
  6285  (5625)      % So between two hellos a peer's picture of us is frozen, and any change
  6286  (5626)      % to our membership is invisible to it until the trunk cycles.
  6287  (5627)      %
  6288  (5628)      % NO NEW WIRE KIND, ON PURPOSE. The receiver's kTrkMbrs handler does not
  6289  (5629)      % require having asked - it accepts the list and REPLACES that peer's rows
  6290  (5630)      % wholesale - so sending one unasked is already legal and already handled
  6291  (5631)      % at both ends. A new kind would have meant a registry entry, golden bytes
  6292  (5632)      % and a parser on a machine that may still be running last week's build;
  6293  (5633)      % this works against every build that already understands kTrkMbrs.
  6294  (5634)      %
  6295  (5635)      % AND BECAUSE IT REPLACES RATHER THAN PATCHES, it is idempotent and
  6296  (5636)      % self-healing: it repairs any drift, not only the change that triggered
  6297  (5637)      % it. Sending one too many costs a message; sending one too few costs a
  6298  (5638)      % private message that vanishes.
  6299  (5639)      %
  6300  (5640)      % ONLY TO PEERS THAT ARE UP, like forwardSaid - a letter to a peer that is
  6301  (5641)      % Down or Unknown only bounces.
  6302  (5642)      %
  6303  (5643)      % REBUILT INSIDE THE LOOP, also like forwardSaid: sendToName hands outBuf
  6304  (5644)      % to XMPFWRI, and building it once outside would be fine today but becomes
  6305  (5645)      % a two-peer-only bug the moment anything in the send path touches outBuf.
  6306  (5646)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6307  (5647)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : tellMbrs
  6308  (5648)          INTEGER : i, length, st
  6309  (5649)          FOR i IN 1:maxPeers DO
  6310  (5650)              IF peerSystem(i) >< 0 THEN
  6311  (5651)                  IF peerState(i) = peerUp THEN
  6312  (5652)                      buildMembers =: length
  6313  (5653)                      sendToName(peerSystem(i), length) =: st
  6314  (5654)                  ENDIF
  6315  (5655)              ENDIF
  6316  (5656)          ENDFOR
  6317  (5657)      ENDROUTINE
  6318  (5658)  
  6319  (5659)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6320  (5660)      % Mark a peer heard-from. Called for ANY arrival on the trunk port.
  6321  (5661)      %
  6322  (5662)      % AN UNKNOWN SYSTEM IS ADDED, not ignored. If a machine trunks to us we want
  6323  (5663)      % the trunk to work in both directions without an operator having to type the
  6324  (5664)      % matching TRUNK here - the design's "whichever machine boots second brings
  6325  (5665)      % the trunk up". If the table is full the greeting is simply dropped; a full
  6326  (5666)      % peer table is an operator problem, not something to fail a message over.
  6327  (5667)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6328  (5668)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER) : markPeerUp(system)
  6329  (5669)          INTEGER : row, i
  6330  (5670)  
  6331  (5671)          % OURSELVES IS NOT A PEER. A hello to a machine with no CHAT-TRUNK name
  6332  (5672)          % bounces back to this very port wearing our own magic, and without this
  6333  (5673)          % the server adds a row for itself and reports it up. Measured the first
  6334  (5674)          % time a trunk was configured - see the note on mySystem.
  6335  (5675)          IF system = mySystem THEN
  6336  (5676)              RETURN
  6337  (5677)          ENDIF
  6338  (5678)          IF system = 0 THEN
  6339  (5679)              RETURN
  6340  (5680)          ENDIF
  6341  (5681)  
  6342  (5682)          findPeer(system) =: row
  6343  (5683)  
  6344  (5684)          % ---- A SYSTEM WE HAVE NEVER SEEN --------------------------------
  6345  (5685)          %
  6346  (5686)          % Learning one is how a trunk comes up without an operator on both
  6347  (5687)          % ends, and it is the default. When the table has been LOCKED it is
  6348  (5688)          % also how a stranger would get in, so the answer is simply to stop
  6349  (5689)          % here: a peer already in the table keeps working, because findPeer
  6350  (5690)          % found it above and this branch is not taken at all.
  6351  (5691)          %
  6352  (5692)          % Dropped in silence on purpose. Anything that reaches this point sent
  6353  (5693)          % a message on the trunk port, and answering a stranger tells them a
  6354  (5694)          % server is here.
  6355  (5695)          IF row = 0 THEN
  6356  (5696)              IF NOT peersOpen THEN
  6357  (5697)                  RETURN
  6358  (5698)              ENDIF
  6359  (5699)              FOR i IN 1:maxPeers DO
  6360  (5700)                  IF peerSystem(i) = 0 THEN
  6361  (5701)                      IF row = 0 THEN
  6362  (5702)                          system =: peerSystem(i)
  6363  (5703)                          % LEARNED, not asked for. This is the row addPeer is
  6364  (5704)                          % allowed to take back when an operator needs one.
  6365  (5705)                          peerLearned =: peerFixed(i)
  6366  (5706)                          i =: row
  6367  (5707)                      ENDIF
  6368  (5708)                  ENDIF
  6369  (5709)              ENDFOR
  6370  (5710)          ENDIF
  6371  (5711)          IF row >< 0 THEN
  6372  (5712)              peerUp =: peerState(row)
  6373  (5713)              0 =: peerHeard(row)
  6374  (5714)  
  6375  (5715)              % RESET THE BACKOFF ON EVERY TRANSITION TO UP, not just the first.
  6376  (5716)              % A peer that flaps would otherwise inherit a five-minute wait from
  6377  (5717)              % its last absence and take five minutes to be missed again.
  6378  (5718)              peerFirstWait =: peerWait(row)
  6379  (5719)              peerFirstWait =: peerCount(row)
  6380  (5720)  
  6381  (5721)              logLine('SV trunk up sys   ', system)
  6382  (5722)          ENDIF
  6383  (5723)      ENDROUTINE
  6384  (5724)  
  6385  (5725)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6386  (5726)      % TRUNK <system>: remember the peer and greet it.
  6387  (5727)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6388  (5728)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER) : addPeer(system)
  6389  (5729)          INTEGER : row, i, length, st
  6390  (5730)  
  6391  (5731)          findPeer(system) =: row
  6392  (5732)          IF row = 0 THEN
  6393  (5733)              FOR i IN 1:maxPeers DO
  6394  (5734)                  IF peerSystem(i) = 0 THEN
  6395  (5735)                      IF row = 0 THEN
  6396  (5736)                          i =: row
  6397  (5737)                      ENDIF
  6398  (5738)                  ENDIF
  6399  (5739)              ENDFOR
  6400  (5740)          ENDIF
  6401  (5741)  
  6402  (5742)          % ---- STILL NO ROW: TAKE ONE BACK FROM A LEARNED PEER -------------
  6403  (5743)          %
  6404  (5744)          % An operator asking for a trunk must always be able to have one. Every
  6405  (5745)          % row can be occupied by a system nobody configured, because markPeerUp
  6406  (5746)          % adds any system that reaches the trunk port - so without this, eight
  6407  (5747)          % strangers are enough to stop the machine being given a real trunk,
  6408  (5748)          % and the operator is told "no room" by machines they never chose.
  6409  (5749)          %
  6410  (5750)          % A learned row is the right one to take: nobody asked for it, and if
  6411  (5751)          % that peer is genuinely there it will be learned again on its next
  6412  (5752)          % message. A row an operator asked for is never taken.
  6413  (5753)          IF row = 0 THEN
  6414  (5754)              FOR i IN 1:maxPeers DO
  6415  (5755)                  IF peerFixed(i) = peerLearned THEN
  6416  (5756)                      IF row = 0 THEN
  6417  (5757)                          i =: row
  6418  (5758)                      ENDIF
  6419  (5759)                  ENDIF
  6420  (5760)              ENDFOR
  6421  (5761)          ENDIF
  6422  (5762)  
  6423  (5763)          % Only when EVERY row was asked for is the refusal a true one - and
  6424  (5764)          % that is the case the table's small size is meant to make loud.
  6425  (5765)          IF row = 0 THEN
  6426  (5766)              buildAdmText('no room for another trunk') =: length
  6427  (5767)              sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6428  (5768)              RETURN
  6429  (5769)          ENDIF
  6430  (5770)  
  6431  (5771)          % Set the row up, whether it was free, reclaimed, or already this peer.
  6432  (5772)          %
  6433  (5773)          % A ROW CHANGING HANDS MUST LOSE THE OLD PEER'S NAME. pName is learned
  6434  (5774)          % from a Hello, so a reclaimed row would otherwise report the machine
  6435  (5775)          % that used to be in it - LIST-TRUNKS would print the wrong name beside
  6436  (5776)          % a perfectly good system number, which is the kind of wrong that gets
  6437  (5777)          % believed.
  6438  (5778)          IF peerSystem(row) >< system THEN
  6439  (5779)              0 =: pNameLen(row)
  6440  (5780)          ENDIF
  6441  (5781)  
  6442  (5782)          system =: peerSystem(row)
  6443  (5783)          peerAsked =: peerFixed(row)
  6444  (5784)          IF peerState(row) >< peerUp THEN
  6445  (5785)              peerUnknown =: peerState(row)
  6446  (5786)              0 =: peerHeard(row)
  6447  (5787)              % Start the backoff clock, or peerTick would count down from an
  6448  (5788)              % uninitialised value and greet at random.
  6449  (5789)              peerFirstWait =: peerWait(row)
  6450  (5790)              peerFirstWait =: peerCount(row)
  6451  (5791)          ENDIF
  6452  (5792)  
  6453  (5793)          % Greet it now rather than waiting for the retry tick, so an operator
  6454  (5794)          % typing TRUNK sees the answer in seconds and not in half a minute.
  6455  (5795)          % A FAILURE HERE IS NOT REPORTED AS ONE - the peer being off is exactly
  6456  (5796)          % what the retry exists for.
  6457  (5797)          sendHello(system, trkAsk) =: st
  6458  (5798)  
  6459  (5799)          buildAdmText('trunk added') =: length
  6460  (5800)          sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6461  (5801)      ENDROUTINE
  6462  (5802)  
  6463  (5803)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6464  (5804)      % UNTRUNK <system>: forget the peer.
  6465  (5805)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6466  (5806)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER) : dropPeer(system)
  6467  (5807)          INTEGER : row, length
  6468  (5808)  
  6469  (5809)          findPeer(system) =: row
  6470  (5810)          IF row = 0 THEN
  6471  (5811)              buildAdmText('no such trunk') =: length
  6472  (5812)              sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6473  (5813)              RETURN
  6474  (5814)          ENDIF
  6475  (5815)  
  6476  (5816)          0 =: peerSystem(row)
  6477  (5817)          peerUnknown =: peerState(row)
  6478  (5818)          0 =: peerHeard(row)
  6479  (5819)  
  6480  (5820)          % AND ITS PEOPLE GO WITH IT. Leaving them behind would show somebody
  6481  (5821)          % chatting from a machine this server no longer talks to, and nothing
  6482  (5822)          % would ever come along to remove them.
  6483  (5823)          forgetRemote(system)
  6484  (5824)  
  6485  (5825)          buildAdmText('trunk removed') =: length
  6486  (5826)          sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6487  (5827)      ENDROUTINE
  6488  (5828)  
  6489  (5829)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6490  (5830)      % Refuse the sender, echoing the name they asked for and naming the reason.
  6491  (5831)      %
  6492  (5832)      % The name is echoed so the asker can tell WHICH request failed when it has
  6493  (5833)      % more than one outstanding; the reason is carried so the wording is the
  6494  (5834)      % same on both doors.
  6495  (5835)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6496  (5836)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER, BYTES, INTEGER) : &
  6497  (5837)              refuse(nameAt, nameLength, reason, reasonLength)
  6498  (5838)          INTEGER : i, at
  6499  (5839)  
  6500  (5840)          % EVERY refusal passes through here - seven call sites do, and that is
  6501  (5841)          % why the count belongs here rather than at any of them. A join that
  6502  (5842)          % fails used to leave no trace at all: the client saw a reject and the
  6503  (5843)          % machine said nothing, so "it will not let me in" could not be told
  6504  (5844)          % from "it never heard me". STATUS answers that now.
  6505  (5845)          nRefused + 1 =: nRefused
  6506  (5846)  
  6507  (5847)          kReject =: outBuf(0)
  6508  (5848)          nameLength =: outBuf(1)
  6509  (5849)          2 =: at
  6510  (5850)          FOR i IN 1:nameLength DO
  6511  (5851)              inBuf(nameAt + i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  6512  (5852)              at + 1 =: at
  6513  (5853)          ENDFOR
  6514  (5854)  
  6515  (5855)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  6516  (5856)          reasonLength =: outBuf(at + 1)
  6517  (5857)          at + 2 =: at
  6518  (5858)          % SUBSCRIPT FROM ZERO. A BYTES declared from a literal - which is what
  6519  (5859)          % every reason string above is - has its first character at index 0, so
  6520  (5860)          % "reason(i)" for i in 1:len silently drops the first letter and reads
  6521  (5861)          % one byte past the end. Same defect, same day, in CHAT.PLNC's buildOwn,
  6522  (5862)          % where it turned RONNY into ONNY on the wire and was only visible
  6523  (5863)          % because the raw bytes were dumped. Nothing warns; PLANC checks no
  6524  (5864)          % bound. Compare cmdIs in CHAT.PLNC, which has always used word(i - 1).
  6525  (5865)          FOR i IN 1:reasonLength DO
  6526  (5866)              reason(i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  6527  (5867)              at + 1 =: at
  6528  (5868)          ENDFOR
  6529  (5869)  
  6530  (5870)          sendTo(senderMagic, at)
  6531  (5871)      ENDROUTINE
  6532  (5872)  
  6533  (5873)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6534  (5874)      % Decode one arrived message and apply the room rules.
  6535  (5875)      %
  6536  (5876)      % A transcription of Xmsg.Chat/ChatRoom.cs. Where that file refuses, this
  6537  (5877)      % refuses for the same reason and in the same order, because the two doors
  6538  (5878)      % lead into one room.
  6539  (5879)      % -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  6540  (5880)      % REFUSE A NICKNAME, AND SAY WHICH ONE.
  6541  (5881)      %
  6542  (5882)      %     refused: nickname 'RONNY' is taken
  6543  (5883)      %
  6544  (5884)      % rather than "that nickname is taken". The name is already in the reject's
  6545  (5885)      % NAME field, so a client could assemble this itself - but then the client
  6546  (5886)      % has to know which refusal it is looking at in order to phrase it, and
  6547  (5887)      % every client has to agree. The server knows, so the server says it, which
  6548  (5888)      % is the same rule the rest of this file follows: one place decides the
  6549  (5889)      % words.
  6550  (5890)      %
  6551  (5891)      % The quote marks are not decoration. A nickname can be anything the user
  6552  (5892)      % typed, including something with a space in it, and without them
  6553  (5893)      % "nickname my name is taken" reads as a sentence rather than a name.
  6554  (5894)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID (INTEGER, INTEGER) : refuseNick(nameAt, nameLength)
  6555  (5895)          INTEGER : i, at
  6556  (5896)  
  6557  (5897)          nRefused + 1 =: nRefused
  6558  (5898)  
  6559  (5899)          kReject =: outBuf(0)
  6560  (5900)          nameLength =: outBuf(1)
  6561  (5901)          2 =: at
  6562  (5902)          FOR i IN 1:nameLength DO
  6563  (5903)              inBuf(nameAt + i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  6564  (5904)              at + 1 =: at
  6565  (5905)          ENDFOR
  6566  (5906)  
  6567  (5907)          0 =: outBuf(at)
  6568  (5908)          0 =: outBuf(at + 1)
  6569  (5909)          at + 2 =: at
  6570  (5910)  
  6571  (5911)          putWord('nickname ', at) =: at
  6572  (5912)          quoteMark(0) =: outBuf(at)
  6573  (5913)          at + 1 =: at
  6574  (5914)          FOR i IN 1:nameLength DO
  6575  (5915)              inBuf(nameAt + i - 1) =: outBuf(at)
  6576  (5916)              at + 1 =: at
  6577  (5917)          ENDFOR
  6578  (5918)          quoteMark(0) =: outBuf(at)
  6579  (5919)          at + 1 =: at
  6580  (5920)          putWord(' is taken', at) =: at
  6581  (5921)  
  6582  (5922)          % The text length, now that it is known. The two length bytes sit
  6583  (5923)          % immediately before the text.
  6584  (5924)          at - (2 + nameLength + 2) =: outBuf(2 + nameLength + 1)
  6585  (5925)  
  6586  (5926)          sendTo(senderMagic, at)
  6587  (5927)      ENDROUTINE
  6588  (5928)  
  6589  (5929)      ROUTINE VOID, VOID : handleMessage
  6590  (5930)          INTEGER : kind, nameLength, nameAt, textLength, textAt
  6591  (5931)          INTEGER : slot, taken, length
  6592  (5932)          % i is used by the topic copy, which is the only loop in this routine.
  6593  (5933)          INTEGER : i
  6594  (5934)          % The room's entry in the topic/history table. Named for what it is
  6595  (5935)          % used for here rather than for the table it comes from - the table is
  6596  (5936)          % called "topic" only because that was its first job.
  6597  (5937)          INTEGER : histSlot
  6598  (5938)          % Where the space between "room" and "count" was found in INITIALIZE.
  6599  (5939)          INTEGER : initSpace
  6600  (5940)          % A relayed line carries these three bytes in front of the usual shape.
  6601  (5941)          % Zero on every other kind, and zero is not a real system number, so a
  6602  (5942)          % handler that reads them by mistake gets an obviously wrong answer
  6603  (5943)          % rather than a plausible one.
  6604  (5944)          INTEGER : relayOrig
  6605  (5945)          INTEGER : relayHops
  6606  (5946)          % SET-NAME and the name on a Hello. Named apart from nameLength, which
  6607  (5947)          % is the MESSAGE's name field and is a different thing entirely.
  6608  (5948)          INTEGER : nameRow
  6609  (5949)          INTEGER : nameLen2
  6610  (5950)          % The number the ORIGIN gave this line, on a kind 53. Zero on
  6611  (5951)          % anything else, and zero is never a real line number - the
  6612  (5952)          % stamper starts at 1 - so a handler that reads it by mistake
  6613  (5953)          % gets an obviously wrong answer rather than a plausible one.
  6614  (5954)          INTEGER : relayId
  6615  (5955)          % Where the '|' splits a Join's room name from a client build
  6616  (5956)          % version - see findJoinSep.
  6617  (5957)          INTEGER : sepAt
  6618  (5958)  
  6619  (5959)          cmKind(inBuf) =: kind
  6620  (5960)  
  6621  (5961)          % TEMPORARY - chasing the 2026-09-01 "you are X on Y" bug. Every kind
  6622  (5962)          % that reaches this point, raw, before any dispatch touches it.
  6623  (5963)          logLine('SV raw kind       ', kind)
  6624  (5964)  
  6625  (5965)          % ---- A RELAYED LINE HAS A LONGER HEADER, so take it off HERE --------
  6626  (5966)          %
  6627  (5967)          % Every field below this point is read at a fixed offset - nameLength
  6628  (5968)          % at byte 1, the name at byte 2 - and a dozen handlers depend on that.
  6629  (5969)          % Rather than teach all of them about a second layout, the three extra
  6630  (5970)          % bytes are read off and the rest of the message is SLID DOWN to where
  6631  (5971)          % it would have been. From the next line on, a relayed line looks
  6632  (5972)          % exactly like any other and only its own handler knows the difference.
  6633  (5973)          %
  6634  (5974)          % The length test is not decoration: readLength is what actually
  6635  (5975)          % arrived, and a truncated relay header would otherwise be read as a
  6636  (5976)          % name length and produce a plausible-looking message out of rubbish.
  6637  (5977)          0 =: relayOrig
  6638  (5978)          0 =: relayHops
  6639  (5979)          0 =: relayId
  6640  (5980)          % ---- THE RELAY HEADER, READ AND TAKEN OFF BY THE LIBRARY ----------
  6641  (5981)          %
  6642  (5982)          % This was written out TWICE here - once for kind 52 and again for 53
  6643  (5983)          % and 54 - with the length tests, the slide and every byte offset
  6644  (5984)          % duplicated between them. CHATLIB owns the layout now, and CHATTST
  6645  (5985)          % proves it against HAND-BUILT headers rather than against its own
  6646  (5986)          % encoder, including the short ones.
  6647  (5987)          %
  6648  (5988)          % The short cases are the reason it matters. A truncated relay header
  6649  (5989)          % read as a chat frame builds a PLAUSIBLE-LOOKING message out of
  6650  (5990)          % rubbish, because the origin's high byte lands exactly where the name
  6651  (5991)          % length belongs. And a kind 53 needs SIX bytes where a 52 needs four -
  6652  (5992)          % a message long enough to satisfy the shorter test can still be too
  6653  (5993)          % short for the longer one, which is precisely the sort of thing two
  6654  (5994)          % hand-written copies drift apart on.
  6655  (5995)          %
  6656  (5996)          % READ THE FIELDS BEFORE STRIPPING. They are gone afterwards.
  6657  (5997)          IF trHLen(kind) > 0 THEN
  6658  (5998)              trOrig(inBuf) =: relayOrig
  6659  (5999)              trHops(inBuf) =: relayHops
  6660  (6000)              IF trHLen(kind) = 6 THEN
  6661  (6001)                  trId(inBuf) =: relayId
  6662  (6002)              ENDIF
  6663  (6003)          ENDIF
  6664  (6004)  
  6665  (6005)          % A kind carrying no relay header answers the length unchanged and
  6666  (6006)          % touches nothing, so this is called without a special case. -1 means
  6667  (6007)          % the message was too short to hold the header it claimed - drop it
  6668  (6008)          % rather than read past the end.
  6669  (6009)          trStrip(inBuf, readLength, kind) =: readLength
  6670  (6010)          IF readLength < 0 THEN
  6671  (6011)              RETURN
  6672  (6012)          ENDIF
  6673  (6013)  
  6674  (6014)          % THE RELAY HEADER, IF THERE WAS ONE, HAS ALREADY BEEN SLID OFF ABOVE -
  6675  (6015)          % so inBuf(1) is the chat name length again here, and cmNmLen is the
  6676  (6016)          % right reader for it. Above the slide it is the relay origin's high
  6677  (6017)          % byte, which is why that code parses by hand and must keep doing so.
  6678  (6018)          cmNmLen(inBuf) =: nameLength
  6679  (6019)          2 =: nameAt
  6680  (6020)  
  6681  (6021)          % A "parse k= nlen= rlen=" line printed here on EVERY message while the
  6682  (6022)          % join was being brought up. It said "remove once the join works", and
  6683  (6023)          % the join works - it was what showed the kind arriving as the XSLET
  6684  (6024)          % serial number instead of kJoin, which is how the missing header skip
  6685  (6025)          % was found.
  6686  (6026)          %
  6687  (6027)          % THE THREE "DROP" MESSAGES BELOW STAY. They are not the same thing: a
  6688  (6028)          % silent drop is right for a stranger on the wire but leaves the operator
  6689  (6029)          % unable to tell a refused message from a lost one, and each prints only
  6690  (6030)          % when something is actually wrong rather than once per message.
  6691  (6031)  
  6692  (6032)          % ---- TRUST NOTHING THAT ARRIVED ------------------------------------
  6693  (6033)          % Every field below is a byte a stranger chose. Two things have to hold
  6694  (6034)          % before any of them is used, and neither is checked by the language:
  6695  (6035)          %
  6696  (6036)          %   the name must FIT      memberName has maxNameLen bytes per seat, and
  6697  (6037)          %                          a length byte can say 255.
  6698  (6038)          %   the fields must be IN  the message actually read is readLength
  6699  (6039)          %   THE MESSAGE            bytes; a length that points past it would read
  6700  (6040)          %                          whatever the previous, longer message left in
  6701  (6041)          %                          inBuf and answer with it.
  6702  (6042)          %
  6703  (6043)          % Dropped in silence rather than refused: a refusal would need a name to
  6704  (6044)          % echo, and the name is the part that is not trustworthy. The seat is
  6705  (6045)          % settled by the receive loop either way, which is exactly why that
  6706  (6046)          % accounting does not live in here.
  6707  (6047)          %
  6708  (6048)          % The sixteen is a rule of the ROOM, not a detail of this program:
  6709  (6049)          % ChatRoom.MaxNicknameLength says the same number, so a name this side
  6710  (6050)          % must drop is one the other side refuses rather than admits. Otherwise
  6711  (6051)          % the two rooms would disagree about who is in them.
  6712  (6052)          IF nameLength > maxNameLen THEN
  6713  (6053)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL21', 'SV: DROP name too big')
  6714  (6054)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL1', '$')
  6715  (6055)              RETURN
  6716  (6056)          ENDIF
  6717  (6057)          IF nameAt + nameLength + 1 > readLength THEN
  6718  (6058)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL22', 'SV: DROP name past end')
  6719  (6059)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL1', '$')
  6720  (6060)              RETURN
  6721  (6061)          ENDIF
  6722  (6062)  
  6723  (6063)          % READ ONLY AFTER THE TWO GUARDS ABOVE - cmTxLen fetches the two bytes
  6724  (6064)          % at nameLength + 2, which is the over-read "name past end" prevents.
  6725  (6065)          cmTxLen(inBuf) =: textLength
  6726  (6066)          cmTxAt(inBuf) =: textAt
  6727  (6067)  
  6728  (6068)          IF textAt + textLength > readLength THEN
  6729  (6069)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL22', 'SV: DROP text past end')
  6730  (6070)              OUTPUT(1, 'AL1', '$')
  6731  (6071)              RETURN
  6732  (6072)          ENDIF
  6733  (6073)  
  6734  (6074)          % ---- ONE VOCABULARY IS NOT ONE AUTHORITY ---------------------------
  6735  (6075)          %
  6736  (6076)          % Admin kinds share the room's numbering on purpose - a second enum
  6737  (6077)          % would mean a second decoder bound, and that bound has been left
  6738  (6078)          % behind twice, silently both times. But sharing the words is not
  6739  (6079)          % sharing the right to use them anywhere: an admin kind arriving on
  6740  (6080)          % CHAT-LOBBY would let any client that can reach the room stop the
  6741  (6081)          % server, and a room kind on the command port would put a member in a
  6742  (6082)          % seat nobody counted.
  6743  (6083)          %
  6744  (6084)          % Refused in SILENCE rather than with a reason. A caller on the wrong
  6745  (6085)          % port is either confused or probing, and neither is owed a map.
  6746  (6086)          % THREE PORTS, THREE VOCABULARIES. Each kind belongs on exactly one of
  6747  (6087)          % them, and arriving on the wrong one is refused in silence.
  6748  (6088)          %
  6749  (6089)          %   48 and up   TRUNK    machine to machine, on CHAT-TRUNK
  6750  (6090)          %   32 .. 47    ADMIN    an operator, on CHAT-ADMIN
  6751  (6091)          %   below 32    ROOM     a member, on the room port
  6752  (6092)          %
  6753  (6093)          % The trunk test comes FIRST because trunk numbers are also >= kAdmStat,
  6754  (6094)          % and testing admin first would demand fromAdmin of a HELLO - which
  6755  (6095)          % arrives on the trunk port, where fromAdmin is false. That is exactly
  6756  (6096)          % how the first build of this swallowed every hello in silence.
  6757  (6097)          IF kind >= kTrkHello THEN
  6758  (6098)              IF NOT fromTrunk THEN
  6759  (6099)                  RETURN
  6760  (6100)              ENDIF
  6761  (6101)          ELSE
  6762  (6102)              IF kind >= kAdmStat THEN
  6763  (6103)                  IF NOT fromAdmin THEN
  6764  (6104)                      RETURN
  6765  (6105)                  ENDIF
  6766  (6106)              ELSE
  6767  (6107)                  IF fromAdmin THEN
  6768  (6108)                      RETURN
  6769  (6109)                  ENDIF
  6770  (6110)                  IF fromTrunk THEN
  6771  (6111)                      RETURN
  6772  (6112)                  ENDIF
  6773  (6113)              ENDIF
  6774  (6114)          ENDIF
  6775  (6115)  
  6776  (6116)          % ---- THE ADMIN COMMANDS --------------------------------------------
  6777  (6117)          % ---- TRUNK CONFIGURATION, from CHAT-MON on the command port ----------
  6778  (6118)          %
  6779  (6119)          % Guarded by fromAdmin like every other admin verb: rewiring the network
  6780  (6120)          % is not something a room member may do.
  6781  (6121)          % ---- LOCK-PEERS / OPEN-PEERS ---------------------------------------
  6782  (6122)          %
  6783  (6123)          % Both are guarded by fromAdmin like every other admin verb, and for a
  6784  (6124)          % sharper reason than most: a member who could unlock the peer table
  6785  (6125)          % could then introduce any machine they liked.
  6786  (6126)          %
  6787  (6127)          % Locking does NOT drop the peers already known - they were learned
  6788  (6128)          % before the operator drew the line, and cutting live trunks is not what
  6789  (6129)          % "take no more" means. STOP-TRUNK is how a peer is removed.
  6790  (6130)          IF kind = kAdmLock THEN
  6791  (6131)              IF NOT fromAdmin THEN
  6792  (6132)                  RETURN
  6793  (6133)              ENDIF
  6794  (6134)              FALSE =: peersOpen
  6795  (6135)              buildAdmText('trunks locked - no new machines will be learned') =: length
  6796  (6136)              sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6797  (6137)              RETURN
  6798  (6138)          ENDIF
  6799  (6139)  
  6800  (6140)          IF kind = kAdmOpen THEN
  6801  (6141)              IF NOT fromAdmin THEN
  6802  (6142)                  RETURN
  6803  (6143)              ENDIF
  6804  (6144)              TRUE =: peersOpen
  6805  (6145)              buildAdmText('trunks unlocked - any machine may be learned') =: length
  6806  (6146)              sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6807  (6147)              RETURN
  6808  (6148)          ENDIF
  6809  (6149)  
  6810  (6150)          % ---- DROP-MEMBER <name> - FREE A SEAT AND TELL NOBODY --------------
  6811  (6151)          %
  6812  (6152)          % See the comment on kAdmDrop where it is declared for WHY this exists.
  6813  (6153)          % In one line: it is the only way to put this machine and a peer into
  6814  (6154)          % the disagreement that kTrkDBad answers, because every ordinary way of
  6815  (6155)          % leaving keeps them in step.
  6816  (6156)          %
  6817  (6157)          % fromAdmin like every other verb here - a room member must not be able
  6818  (6158)          % to unseat anybody, least of all silently.
  6819  (6159)          %
  6820  (6160)          % NOTE WHAT IS DELIBERATELY MISSING, and compare it with kLeave below:
  6821  (6161)          % there is no buildFromSlot(kLeft...), no broadcast to the room, and no
  6822  (6162)          % tellMbrs to the peers. Freeing memberUsed is the WHOLE action. Adding
  6823  (6163)          % any of those back would defeat the only purpose this verb has.
  6824  (6164)          IF kind = kAdmDrop THEN
  6825  (6165)              IF NOT fromAdmin THEN
  6826  (6166)                  RETURN
  6827  (6167)              ENDIF
  6828  (6168)              findByName(textAt, textLength) =: taken
  6829  (6169)              IF taken = 0 THEN
  6830  (6170)                  buildAdmText('no member of that name is seated here') =: length
  6831  (6171)                  sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6832  (6172)                  RETURN
  6833  (6173)              ENDIF
  6834  (6174)              0 =: memberUsed(taken)
  6835  (6175)              buildAdmText('seat freed - room and peers NOT told') =: length
  6836  (6176)              sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6837  (6177)              RETURN
  6838  (6178)          ENDIF
  6839  (6179)  
  6840  (6180)          IF kind = kAdmTrunk THEN
  6841  (6181)              IF NOT fromAdmin THEN
  6842  (6182)                  RETURN
  6843  (6183)              ENDIF
  6844  (6184)              trunkNumber(textAt, textLength) =: taken
  6845  (6185)              IF taken = 0 THEN
  6846  (6186)                  buildAdmText('TRUNK needs a system number') =: length
  6847  (6187)                  sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6848  (6188)                  RETURN
  6849  (6189)              ENDIF
  6850  (6190)              % NO "=: length" - addPeer is a VOID routine and sends its own
  6851  (6191)              % reply. Assigning from it drew ILLEGAL DATA TYPE "ADDPEER" at the
  6852  (6192)              % CALL, which reads like a problem with the argument.
  6853  (6193)              addPeer(taken)
  6854  (6194)              RETURN
  6855  (6195)          ENDIF
  6856  (6196)  
  6857  (6197)          % INITIALIZE <room> <n> - how many messages a room remembers.
  6858  (6198)          %
  6859  (6199)          % TWO FIELDS, unlike every other admin verb, so the text is split at the
  6860  (6200)          % first space: the room name, then the count. Split at the FIRST one
  6861  (6201)          % because a room name cannot contain a space and a trailing one would
  6862  (6202)          % otherwise become part of the number.
  6863  (6203)          %
  6864  (6204)          % REFUSED ONCE THE ROOM HAS HISTORY, and this is the part worth being
  6865  (6205)          % careful about. The ring is "block = id MOD size", so CHANGING size
  6866  (6206)          % moves where every existing id lands: the messages do not move, but
  6867  (6207)          % the arithmetic that finds them does, and the replay would hand back
  6868  (6208)          % other messages in the wrong order while looking perfectly healthy.
  6869  (6209)          % There is no cheap correct rewrite - it means reading the whole ring
  6870  (6210)          % out and writing it back in the new shape - so an empty room is the
  6871  (6211)          % only case allowed, and the refusal says exactly that. Setting a room
  6872  (6212)          % up BEFORE anybody speaks in it is the case this was asked for.
  6873  (6213)          IF kind = kAdmInit THEN
  6874  (6214)              IF NOT fromAdmin THEN
  6875  (6215)                  RETURN
  6876  (6216)              ENDIF
  6877  (6217)  
  6878  (6218)              % Find the space that separates the two fields.
  6879  (6219)              0 =: initSpace
  6880  (6220)              FOR i IN 1:textLength DO
  6881  (6221)                  IF initSpace = 0 THEN
  6882  (6222)                      IF inBuf(textAt + i - 1) = spaceByte(0) THEN
  6883  (6223)                          i =: initSpace
  6884  (6224)                      ENDIF
  6885  (6225)                  ENDIF
  6886  (6226)              ENDFOR
  6887  (6227)  
  6888  (6228)              % ---- NO ROOM NAMED: SET THIS MACHINE'S DEFAULT ------------------
  6889  (6229)              %
  6890  (6230)              % Ronny, 2026-08-23: "INITIALIZE without room sets the default
  6891  (6231)              % amount for this machine, spread out by rooms as the rooms may be
  6892  (6232)              % created later."
  6893  (6233)              %
  6894  (6234)              % So one field is a DEFAULT and two are one ROOM. It reads the way
