  6895  (6235)              % an operator would say it - "INITIALIZE 20" is how much a room
  6896  (6236)              % keeps here, "INITIALIZE LOBBY 20" is how much LOBBY keeps.
  6897  (6237)              %
  6898  (6238)              % IT DOES NOT TOUCH ROOMS THAT ALREADY EXIST, and that is the whole
  6899  (6239)              % meaning of "as the rooms may be created later": claimTopic and
  6900  (6240)              % claimRoomNamed take a copy of histDefault when they allocate, so
  6901  (6241)              % a room created after this picks the new number up and one created
  6902  (6242)              % before it keeps what it was given. Reaching back into live rooms
  6903  (6243)              % would change a ring that already has messages in it, which is the
  6904  (6244)              % thing the per-room form refuses to do for good reason.
  6905  (6245)              IF initSpace = 0 THEN
  6906  (6246)                  trunkNumber(textAt, textLength) =: taken
  6907  (6247)                  IF taken < 1 THEN
  6908  (6248)                      buildAdmText('INITIALIZE needs a count above zero') &
  6909  (6249)                          =: length
  6910  (6250)                      sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6911  (6251)                      RETURN
  6912  (6252)                  ENDIF
  6913  (6253)                  IF taken > maxHistSize THEN
  6914  (6254)                      buildAdmText('that is more than a room can keep') &
  6915  (6255)                          =: length
  6916  (6256)                      sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6917  (6257)                      RETURN
  6918  (6258)                  ENDIF
  6919  (6259)                  taken =: histDefault
  6920  (6260)                  buildAdmText('default history size set') =: length
  6921  (6261)                  sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6922  (6262)                  RETURN
  6923  (6263)              ENDIF
  6924  (6264)  
  6925  (6265)              trunkNumber(textAt + initSpace, textLength - initSpace) =: taken
  6926  (6266)              IF taken < 1 THEN
  6927  (6267)                  buildAdmText('INITIALIZE needs a count above zero') &
  6928  (6268)                      =: length
  6929  (6269)                  sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6930  (6270)                  RETURN
  6931  (6271)              ENDIF
  6932  (6272)              IF taken > maxHistSize THEN
  6933  (6273)                  buildAdmText('that is more than a room can keep') =: length
  6934  (6274)                  sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6935  (6275)                  RETURN
  6936  (6276)              ENDIF
  6937  (6277)  
  6938  (6278)              claimRoomNamed(textAt, initSpace - 1) =: histSlot
  6939  (6279)              IF histSlot = 0 THEN
  6940  (6280)                  buildAdmText('no room left in the table') =: length
  6941  (6281)                  sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6942  (6282)                  RETURN
  6943  (6283)              ENDIF
  6944  (6284)  
  6945  (6285)              IF histNext(histSlot) > 0 THEN
  6946  (6286)                  buildAdmText('that room already has history') =: length
  6947  (6287)                  sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6948  (6288)                  RETURN
  6949  (6289)              ENDIF
  6950  (6290)  
  6951  (6291)              taken =: histSize(histSlot)
  6952  (6292)              buildAdmText('room history size set') =: length
  6953  (6293)              sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6954  (6294)              RETURN
  6955  (6295)          ENDIF
  6956  (6296)  
  6957  (6297)          IF kind = kAdmUntrnk THEN
  6958  (6298)              IF NOT fromAdmin THEN
  6959  (6299)                  RETURN
  6960  (6300)              ENDIF
  6961  (6301)              trunkNumber(textAt, textLength) =: taken
  6962  (6302)              IF taken = 0 THEN
  6963  (6303)                  buildAdmText('UNTRUNK needs a system number') =: length
  6964  (6304)                  sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6965  (6305)                  RETURN
  6966  (6306)              ENDIF
  6967  (6307)              dropPeer(taken)
  6968  (6308)              RETURN
  6969  (6309)          ENDIF
  6970  (6310)  
  6971  (6311)          IF kind = kAdmTrunks THEN
  6972  (6312)              IF NOT fromAdmin THEN
  6973  (6313)                  RETURN
  6974  (6314)              ENDIF
  6975  (6315)              buildTrunks =: length
  6976  (6316)              sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6977  (6317)              RETURN
  6978  (6318)          ENDIF
  6979  (6319)  
  6980  (6320)          IF kind = kAdmMbrs THEN
  6981  (6321)              IF NOT fromAdmin THEN
  6982  (6322)                  RETURN
  6983  (6323)              ENDIF
  6984  (6324)              buildSeats =: length
  6985  (6325)              sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  6986  (6326)              RETURN
  6987  (6327)          ENDIF
  6988  (6328)  
  6989  (6329)          % SET-NAME <name> - what this machine calls itself.
  6990  (6330)          %
  6991  (6331)          % AN EMPTY NAME CLEARS IT, and that is deliberate rather than an
  6992  (6332)          % oversight: a machine with no name falls back to D<number> everywhere,
  6993  (6333)          % which is exactly how it behaved before names existed. So there is a
  6994  (6334)          % way back from a name typed wrongly that does not need a restart.
  6995  (6335)          %
  6996  (6336)          % The peers are told at the next hello - the trunk sends one on its own
  6997  (6337)          % schedule and nothing has to be poked. An operator in a hurry can use
  6998  (6338)          % RESTART-TRUNK to make it happen now.
  6999  (6339)          IF kind = kAdmName THEN
  7000  (6340)              IF NOT fromAdmin THEN
  7001  (6341)                  RETURN
  7002  (6342)              ENDIF
  7003  (6343)  
  7004  (6344)              textLength =: nameLen2
  7005  (6345)              IF nameLen2 > maxNameLen THEN
  7006  (6346)                  maxNameLen =: nameLen2
  7007  (6347)              ENDIF
  7008  (6348)              IF nameLen2 > 0 THEN
  7009  (6349)                  FOR i IN 1:nameLen2 DO
  7010  (6350)                      inBuf(textAt + i - 1) =: sysName(i - 1)
  7011  (6351)                  ENDFOR
  7012  (6352)              ENDIF
  7013  (6353)              nameLen2 =: lenSysName
  7014  (6354)  
  7015  (6355)              buildNameReply =: length
  7016  (6356)              sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  7017  (6357)              RETURN
  7018  (6358)          ENDIF
  7019  (6359)  
  7020  (6360)          % ---- A HELLO FROM ANOTHER SERVER, on the trunk port -----------------
  7021  (6361)          %
  7022  (6362)          % ANYTHING ARRIVING FROM A PEER MARKS IT UP, including an unsolicited
  7023  (6363)          % HELLO from a machine we have not trunked. That is what lets whichever
  7024  (6364)          % machine boots second bring the trunk up without the first noticing.
  7025  (6365)          %
  7026  (6366)          % An ASK is answered, an ANSWER is not - see the note on trkAsk. Without
  7027  (6367)          % that the two servers would greet each other for ever.
  7028  (6368)          IF kind = kTrkHello THEN
  7029  (6369)              IF NOT fromTrunk THEN
  7030  (6370)                  RETURN
  7031  (6371)              ENDIF
  7032  (6372)  
  7033  (6373)              % A RETURNED LETTER IS NOT A PEER SAYING HELLO - IT IS THE OPPOSITE.
  7034  (6374)              %
  7035  (6375)              % XMTRE means the message came BACK: "If msgType is XMTRE,
  7036  (6376)              % msgLengthOrStat contains the error status". The design counts on
  7037  (6377)              % exactly this as the CHEAP way to learn a peer is absent - "the
  7038  (6378)              % letter comes straight back undeliverable ... Immediate, no
  7039  (6379)              % waiting" - so reading it as proof of life inverts the one signal
  7040  (6380)              % that costs nothing.
  7041  (6381)              %
  7042  (6382)              % The bounce does NOT say who it was addressed to, so there is
  7043  (6383)              % nobody specific to mark down here; it is simply not treated as
  7044  (6384)              % good news, and the peer stays whatever it was until the idle
  7045  (6385)              % count declares it down. Carrying the addressee in the hello text
  7046  (6386)              % would let this mark the right row immediately - worth doing when
  7047  (6387)              % the state machine grows.
  7048  (6388)              IF msgType = XMTRE THEN
  7049  (6389)                  logLine('SV trunk bounce   ', receiveStat)
  7050  (6390)                  RETURN
  7051  (6391)              ENDIF
  7052  (6392)  
  7053  (6393)              markPeerUp(senderSystem)
  7054  (6394)  
  7055  (6395)              % WHAT THAT MACHINE CALLS ITSELF, if it said. Anything after the
  7056  (6396)              % direction byte is its name.
  7057  (6397)              %
  7058  (6398)              % Taken on EVERY hello and not only the first, for the same reason
  7059  (6399)              % the member list is: a peer saying hello again has very likely
  7060  (6400)              % restarted, and a name it was given before the restart may not be
  7061  (6401)              % the one it has now.
  7062  (6402)              %
  7063  (6403)              % A NAME IS ONLY A LABEL ON A NUMBER. senderSystem comes from the
  7064  (6404)              % kernel's magic and is not something the sender can choose, so a
  7065  (6405)              % machine may call itself whatever it likes and still cannot claim
  7066  (6406)              % to BE another machine.
  7067  (6407)              IF textLength > 1 THEN
  7068  (6408)                  findPeer(senderSystem) =: nameRow
  7069  (6409)                  IF nameRow > 0 THEN
  7070  (6410)                      textLength - 1 =: nameLen2
  7071  (6411)                      IF nameLen2 > maxNameLen THEN
  7072  (6412)                          maxNameLen =: nameLen2
  7073  (6413)                      ENDIF
  7074  (6414)                      FOR i IN 1:nameLen2 DO
  7075  (6415)                          inBuf(textAt + i) =: pName(nameRow, i)
  7076  (6416)                      ENDFOR
  7077  (6417)                      nameLen2 =: pNameLen(nameRow)
  7078  (6418)                  ENDIF
  7079  (6419)              ENDIF
  7080  (6420)  
  7081  (6421)              IF textLength > 0 THEN
  7082  (6422)                  IF inBuf(textAt) = trkAsk THEN
  7083  (6423)                      sendHello(senderSystem, trkAnswer) =: taken
  7084  (6424)                  ENDIF
  7085  (6425)              ENDIF
  7086  (6426)  
  7087  (6427)              % PHASE C: A HELLO IS ALSO THE MOMENT TO ASK WHO IS THERE.
  7088  (6428)              %
  7089  (6429)              % Asked on EVERY hello, not only the first. A peer that says hello
  7090  (6430)              % again has very likely restarted, and everything we remember about
  7091  (6431)              % its members is then stale - the design's "treat Up as start
  7092  (6432)              % again". The answer REPLACES our rows for it, so asking twice
  7093  (6433)              % costs one message and can never leave a ghost.
  7094  (6434)              askWho(senderSystem) =: taken
  7095  (6435)              RETURN
  7096  (6436)          ENDIF
  7097  (6437)  
  7098  (6438)          % ---- A PEER ASKS WHO IS ON US ---------------------------------------
  7099  (6439)          IF kind = kTrkWho THEN
  7100  (6440)              IF NOT fromTrunk THEN
  7101  (6441)                  RETURN
  7102  (6442)              ENDIF
  7103  (6443)              IF msgType = XMTRE THEN
  7104  (6444)                  RETURN
  7105  (6445)              ENDIF
  7106  (6446)              buildMembers =: length
  7107  (6447)              sendToName(senderSystem, length) =: taken
  7108  (6448)              RETURN
  7109  (6449)          ENDIF
  7110  (6450)  
  7111  (6451)          % ---- SOMEBODY SPOKE ON ANOTHER MACHINE ------------------------------
  7112  (6452)          %
  7113  (6453)          % The text is "ROOM/what they said". The room decides who hears it here;
  7114  (6454)          % the speaker is shown qualified with the system the LETTER came from,
  7115  (6455)          % never with anything the sender put in the message.
  7116  (6456)          IF kind = kTrkSaid THEN
  7117  (6457)              IF NOT fromTrunk THEN
  7118  (6458)                  RETURN
  7119  (6459)              ENDIF
  7120  (6460)              IF msgType = XMTRE THEN
  7121  (6461)                  RETURN
  7122  (6462)              ENDIF
  7123  (6463)              markPeerUp(senderSystem)
  7124  (6464)              deliverRemoteSaid(senderSystem, nameAt, nameLength, &
  7125  (6465)                  textAt, textLength)
  7126  (6466)  
  7127  (6467)              % AND PASS IT ON. This is what makes a chain work without changing
  7128  (6468)              % what the SENDER does at all: the machine in the middle turns a
  7129  (6469)              % direct line into a relayed one. An older server simply does not
  7130  (6470)              % do this, which is why nothing had to be upgraded in step.
  7131  (6471)              %
  7132  (6472)              % The origin is senderSystem because this line came STRAIGHT from
  7133  (6473)              % the machine it was typed on - one hop so far.
  7134  (6474)              % Id ZERO: kind 51 carries none, because it comes from a server
  7135  (6475)              % that predates dedup. Passing zero on is honest - it says "this
  7136  (6476)              % line has no name" - and the receiver treats an unnamed line as
  7137  (6477)              % always new, which is exactly today's behaviour. Inventing a
  7138  (6478)              % number here would be worse than useless: a second relay would
  7139  (6479)              % invent a different one for the same line.
  7140  (6480)              relayOn(senderSystem, senderSystem, 0, maxHops - 1, &
  7141  (6481)                  nameAt, nameLength, textAt, textLength)
  7142  (6482)              RETURN
  7143  (6483)          ENDIF
  7144  (6484)  
  7145  (6485)          % ---- SOMEBODY SPOKE FURTHER AWAY ------------------------------------
  7146  (6486)          %
  7147  (6487)          % A line that has already been through at least one other machine. The
  7148  (6488)          % speaker is qualified with the ORIGIN THE LETTER CARRIES, not with the
  7149  (6489)          % system it arrived from - that one is the relay, and using it would
  7150  (6490)          % show a D103 user as being on D100.
  7151  (6491)          IF kind = kTrkRly THEN
  7152  (6492)              IF NOT fromTrunk THEN
  7153  (6493)                  RETURN
  7154  (6494)              ENDIF
  7155  (6495)              IF msgType = XMTRE THEN
  7156  (6496)                  RETURN
  7157  (6497)              ENDIF
  7158  (6498)  
  7159  (6499)              % The machine that PASSED it to us is alive; that is what we heard
  7160  (6500)              % from. Says nothing about the origin, which we may have no trunk
  7161  (6501)              % to at all - and that is the entire point of relaying.
  7162  (6502)              markPeerUp(senderSystem)
  7163  (6503)  
  7164  (6504)              deliverRemoteSaid(relayOrig, nameAt, nameLength, &
  7165  (6505)                  textAt, textLength)
  7166  (6506)  
  7167  (6507)              % On again, one hop shorter, never back the way it came.
  7168  (6508)              relayOn(senderSystem, relayOrig, 0, relayHops - 1, &
  7169  (6509)                  nameAt, nameLength, textAt, textLength)
  7170  (6510)              RETURN
  7171  (6511)          ENDIF
  7172  (6512)  
  7173  (6513)          % ---- SOMEBODY SPOKE FURTHER AWAY, AND THE LINE HAS A NAME ------------
  7174  (6514)          %
  7175  (6515)          % Same as kTrkRly above, with the one difference that matters: this
  7176  (6516)          % line carries the number its ORIGIN gave it, so we can tell a second
  7177  (6517)          % copy from a new line and show it once.
  7178  (6518)          IF kind = kTrkRlId THEN
  7179  (6519)              IF NOT fromTrunk THEN
  7180  (6520)                  RETURN
  7181  (6521)              ENDIF
  7182  (6522)              IF msgType = XMTRE THEN
  7183  (6523)                  RETURN
  7184  (6524)              ENDIF
  7185  (6525)  
  7186  (6526)              % The machine that PASSED it on is alive. Says nothing about the
  7187  (6527)              % origin, which we may have no trunk to at all.
  7188  (6528)              markPeerUp(senderSystem)
  7189  (6529)  
  7190  (6530)              % SEEN IT ALREADY? Then stop here - do not show it and do not pass
  7191  (6531)              % it on. Passing on a repeat would multiply it round a mesh even
  7192  (6532)              % though nobody would be shown it twice.
  7193  (6533)              IF lineIsNew(relayOrig, relayId) = 0 THEN
  7194  (6534)                  nDupes + 1 =: nDupes
  7195  (6535)                  RETURN
  7196  (6536)              ENDIF
  7197  (6537)  
  7198  (6538)              deliverRemoteSaid(relayOrig, nameAt, nameLength, &
  7199  (6539)                  textAt, textLength)
  7200  (6540)  
  7201  (6541)              % On again, one hop shorter, never back the way it came, and with
  7202  (6542)              % the id UNCHANGED.
  7203  (6543)              relayOn(senderSystem, relayOrig, relayId, relayHops - 1, &
  7204  (6544)                  nameAt, nameLength, textAt, textLength)
  7205  (6545)              RETURN
  7206  (6546)          ENDIF
  7207  (6547)  
  7208  (6548)          % ---- A PEER TELLS US WHO IS ON IT -----------------------------------
  7209  (6549)          % A DIRECT MESSAGE FROM ANOTHER MACHINE.
  7210  (6550)          %
  7211  (6551)          % Deduplicated by the same window as a relayed room line, because it
  7212  (6552)          % carries the same origin and id. NOT RELAYED ONWARD: a room line is
  7213  (6553)          % passed to every peer and sorted out at the far end, but doing that
  7214  (6554)          % with a private message would hand it to machines that have no
  7215  (6555)          % business seeing it. It arrived here because it was addressed here.
  7216  (6556)          IF kind = kTrkDir THEN
  7217  (6557)              IF NOT fromTrunk THEN
  7218  (6558)                  RETURN
  7219  (6559)              ENDIF
  7220  (6560)              IF msgType = XMTRE THEN
  7221  (6561)                  RETURN
  7222  (6562)              ENDIF
  7223  (6563)  
  7224  (6564)              markPeerUp(senderSystem)
  7225  (6565)  
  7226  (6566)              IF lineIsNew(relayOrig, relayId) = 0 THEN
  7227  (6567)                  nDupes + 1 =: nDupes
  7228  (6568)                  RETURN
  7229  (6569)              ENDIF
  7230  (6570)  
  7231  (6571)              takeRemDirect(relayOrig, relayId, nameAt, nameLength, &
  7232  (6572)                  textAt, textLength)
  7233  (6573)              RETURN
  7234  (6574)          ENDIF
  7235  (6575)  
  7236  (6576)          % A REFUSAL COMING HOME. Same guards as kTrkDir above, for the same
  7237  (6577)          % reasons: only from a trunk, never a returned letter, and never twice.
  7238  (6578)          % Not relayed onward - it was addressed here, like the message it is

  7269  (6609)              % SNAPSHOT, REPLACE, ANNOUNCE THE DIFFERENCE - 2026-09-02. The
  7270  (6610)              % replace itself is unchanged; what is new is that local seats

  7272  (6612)              % kLeft, so the count and the notices finally cover the other

  7331  (6671)  
  7332  (6672)              % AND WHICH ROOM. The Join carries it in the TEXT field,
  7333  (6673)              % because with one port the room can no longer be told from
  7334  (6674)              % which port the message arrived on. Everything this member
  7335  (6675)              % says afterwards is broadcast only to the others in it.
  7336  (6676)              %
  7337  (6677)              % A BUILD VERSION MAY RIDE AFTER IT, split off here so copyRoomIn
  7338  (6678)              % never sees it and cannot mistake it for part of the room name.
  7339  (6679)              findJoinSep(textAt, textLength) =: sepAt
  7340  (6680)              IF sepAt >= 0 THEN
  7341  (6681)                  copyRoomIn(slot, textAt, sepAt - textAt)
  7342  (6682)                  copyVersionIn(slot, sepAt + 1, textAt + textLength - sepAt - 1)
  7343  (6683)              ELSE
  7344  (6684)                  copyRoomIn(slot, textAt, textLength)
  7345  (6685)                  0 =: memberVerLen(slot)
  7346  (6686)              ENDIF
  7347  (6687)  
  7348  (6688)              % The welcome goes only to the joiner; the room hears Joined.
  7349  (6689)              buildWelcome(nameAt, nameLength) =: length
  7350  (6690)              sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  7351  (6691)  
  7352  (6692)              % ---- WHAT THEY MISSED, BEFORE THE ROOM HEARS THEY ARRIVED -----
  7353  (6693)              %
  7354  (6694)              % ORDER MATTERS AND THIS IS THE RIGHT ONE. After the welcome,
  7355  (6695)              % because the client is not in the room until it has that and
  7356  (6696)              % would drop anything sent before it. Before the Joined broadcast,
  7357  (6697)              % so the transcript reads the way it happened: everything said
  7358  (6698)              % earlier, then "OLAV joined", then whatever is said next. Sending
  7359  (6699)              % history after the announcement would put old lines below the
  7360  (6700)              % arrival and read as though they were new.
  7361  (6701)              %
  7362  (6702)              % findTopic, NOT claimTopic - joining a room must not allocate a
  7363  (6703)              % history slot. A room nobody has ever spoken in has no history to
  7364  (6704)              % replay, and giving it a table entry on a join would let eight
  7365  (6705)              % people joining eight empty rooms fill the table with nothing.
  7366  (6706)              findTopic(slot) =: histSlot
  7367  (6707)              histReplay(histSlot, senderMagic)
  7368  (6708)  
  7369  (6709)              buildMessage(kJoined, nameAt, nameLength, 0, 0) =: length
  7370  (6710)              broadcast(length, slot, slot)        % their room, not them
  7371  (6711)  
  7372  (6712)              % AND TELL THE OTHER MACHINES SOMEBODY NEW IS HERE.
  7373  (6713)              % Without this a peer cannot see them until the next trunk
  7374  (6714)              % hello, so a private message addressed to them is refused
  7375  (6715)              % with 'nobody of that name is here now' while they sit in
  7376  (6716)              % the room. Same cause as the rename drift - see tellMbrs.
  7377  (6717)              tellMbrs
  7378  (6718)              RETURN
  7379  (6719)          ENDIF
  7380  (6720)  
  7381  (6721)          % ---- THE TWO THINGS A PERSON WITH NO SEAT MAY ASK ------------------
  7382  (6722)          %
  7383  (6723)          % Both are READ-ONLY and both are what somebody actually needs at the
  7384  (6724)          % moment they have no seat: they have just left, or been refused a
  7385  (6725)          % nickname, and the next question is always "what is there" or "who is
  7386  (6726)          % about". Answering silence to that is the client's worst behaviour.
  7387  (6727)          %
  7388  (6728)          % kRooms WAS SUPPOSED TO BE ALLOWED HERE ALREADY, and was not.
  7389  (6729)          % DOC/protocols/chat-wire.json has said since it was written:
  7390  (6730)          %
  7391  (6731)          %   "Allowed while UNJOINED, unlike Who: 'what rooms are there' is
  7392  (6732)          %    exactly what somebody asks after being refused a name, and the
  7393  (6733)          %    server needs no seat to answer it."
  7394  (6734)          %
  7395  (6735)          % The handler sat BELOW the membership guard, so /list from somebody
  7396  (6736)          % with no seat was dropped without a word. The registry described the
  7397  (6737)          % intention and the code never matched it - found 2026-08-22 by reading
  7398  (6738)          % the registry entry while building the /who case beside it.
  7399  (6739)          %
  7400  (6740)          % THE GUARD'S REASON STILL STANDS FOR EVERYTHING ELSE. Silence for a
  7401  (6741)          % stranger is deliberate: answering confirms the port is a chat server
  7402  (6742)          % to something that never joined. These two answers give up exactly
  7403  (6743)          % that one fact and nothing else - no seat is spent, no state changes,
  7404  (6744)          % and a roster is not a secret in a chat for one building. Saying and
  7405  (6745)          % joining and renaming stay behind the guard.
  7406  (6746)          IF kind = kRooms THEN
  7407  (6747)              buildRooms =: length
  7408  (6748)              sendTo(senderMagic, length)
  7409  (6749)              RETURN
  7410  (6750)          ENDIF
  7411  (6751)  
  7412  (6752)          % /map belongs here for the same reason /list does: it is READ-ONLY,
  7413  (6753)          % costs no seat, and is exactly what somebody asks when they cannot see
  7414  (6754)          % anyone - "is the other machine even there?". Refusing it to a person
  7415  (6755)          % with no seat would refuse it at the one moment it explains something.
  7416  (6756)          IF kind = kMap THEN
  7417  (6757)              buildMap =: length

  7425  (6765)                  % whole house. A DIFFERENT KIND, because the client labels a
  7426  (6766)                  % kWho answer with the room it thinks it is in.
  7427  (6767)                  buildAllWho =: length
  7428  (6768)                  sendTo(senderMagic, length)

  7430  (6770)              ENDIF
  7431  (6771)          ENDIF

  7434  (6774)              RETURN
